==========================================================================================
(1) القسم الأول: رواية The Lost World | The Novel — Arthur Conan Doyle
==========================================================================================

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
مقدمة الرواية | Introduction
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

◆ A) The Writer (Arthur Conan Doyle) — التعريف بالكاتب
• Arthur Conan Doyle (1859–1930) was a Scottish writer and doctor who is best known for creating the famous detective محقق Sherlock Holmes. He was born in Edinburgh, Scotland, in 1859. Before becoming a full-time writer, he studied medicine and worked as a doctor.
• Doyle published his first Sherlock Holmes story, A Study in Scarlet, in 1887. Holmes became one of the most famous fictional detectives in the world because of his intelligence, careful observation, and logical reasoning الاستدلال المنطقي. Doyle wrote many stories and novels featuring التي تدور حول Holmes and his friend, Dr. John Watson.
• Besides detective fiction, Doyle wrote historical novels, science fiction الخيال العلمي, adventure stories, and other works. He also had a strong interest in history and different scientific and social issues القضايا الاجتماعية.
• Arthur Conan Doyle died in 1930 at the age of 71. His Sherlock Holmes stories continue to be widely read and have inspired countless films, television series, and adaptations.

◆ B) The Novel — نبذة عن الرواية
• The Lost World is an adventure novel by Arthur Conan Doyle, published in 1912. It tells the story of Professor Challenger, a bold جريء and confident scientist who claims that prehistoric animals, including dinosaurs, still exist in a remote area of South America.
• A journalist named Edward Malone joins Professor Challenger and a small group of explorers on a dangerous expedition رحلة استكشافية. They travel to a mysterious plateau هضبة that is difficult to reach. When they arrive, they discover a strange world where dinosaurs and other prehistoric creatures still live.
• The explorers face many dangers and unexpected challenges. They also meet a group of humans living on the plateau and become involved in a conflict صراع between different groups.
• The novel combines adventure, science, mystery, and exploration. It explores themes such as courage, teamwork, discovery, and the desire to explore the unknown.

◆ C) The Setting — المكان والزمان
• The novel begins in London, England, where Edward Malone works as a journalist. The main events take place in a remote plateau in the Amazon region of South America. The plateau is isolated معزول and difficult to reach, and it is home to dinosaurs and other prehistoric creatures. The story is set in the early 20th century.

◆ D) Characters in the Novel — شخصيات الرواية

◆ 1) Professor George Edward Challenger البروفيسور جورج إدوارد تشالنجر
• A brilliant but proud and confident scientist.
• He believes that prehistoric creatures still exist in a remote part of South America.
• He leads the expedition to the mysterious غامض plateau.
• He is courageous and determined to prove his scientific theory نظرية.

◆ 2) Edward Malone إدوارد مالون
• A young journalist who works for the Daily Gazette.
• He is brave, curious, and eager for adventure.
• He joins Challenger's expedition to prove himself as a capable reporter.
• He tells much of the story and records the group's experiences.

◆ 3) Lord John Roxton اللورد جون روكستون
• A brave and experienced explorer and hunter.
• He is calm, courageous, and skilled at dealing with dangerous situations.
• He has experience fighting against slavery العبودية in South America.
• He becomes an important member of the expedition and helps protect the others.

◆ 4) Professor Summerlee البروفيسور سمرلي
• An elderly scientist and Challenger's rival منافس.
• At first, he does not believe Challenger's claims about dinosaurs.
• He joins the expedition to examine the evidence himself.
• Although sceptical متشكك, he is intelligent, brave, and committed to ملتزم بـ science.

◆ 5) Zambo زامبو
• A loyal and strong African servant who accompanies the explorers.
• He remains at the base قاعدة of the plateau while the others explore.
• He helps the group by providing food and supplies مؤن.
• He is dependable جدير بالثقة and loyal to his companions.

◆ 6) Gladys Hungerton جلاديس هونجيرتون
• Edward Malone's love interest at the beginning of the novel.
• She encourages Malone to become a brave and successful man.
• His desire to prove himself is partly connected to his feelings for her.

◆ 7) The Ape-Men رجال القرية
• They are primitive بدائي human-like creatures living on the plateau.
• They are enemies of the human tribe قبيلة living there.
• Their conflict with the humans creates some of the novel's major dangers.

◆ 8) The Indians الهنود
• A group of human inhabitants السكان living on the plateau.
• They are involved in a long conflict with the Ape-Men.
• The explorers help them in their struggle كفاح.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
الفصل 1 | Chapter 1: A Dream of Adventure
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

◆ المفردات | Vocabulary
act (v) (ed) | يتصرف | experienced (adj) | خبير/متمرس | mistaken (adj) | مخطئ
adventure (n) | مغامرة | expression (n) | تعبير/لوجه | plain (adj) | واضح/بلي
aggressive (adj) | عدوانى | fossils (n) | حفريات | pretend (v) (ed) | يتظاهر
appointment (n) | موعد | honestly (adv) | بأمانة/بصدق | question (v) (ed) | يستجوب
assignment (n) | تكليف/مهمة | impress (v) (ed) | يؤثر على/يبهر | reporter (n) | مراسل صحفى
barely (adv) | بالكاد | joke (v) (d) | يمزح | reveal (v) (ed) | يكشف
battle (n) | معركة | journalist (n) | صحفى | roar (v) (ed) | يزأر/يرتفع صوتيجيه
beard (n) | لحية | literally (adv) | حرفيا | sensible (adj) | عاقل/عقلانى
brilliant (adj) | بارع/ذكى | little-known (adj) | قليل الشهرة | servant (n) | خادم

chest (n) | الصدر | lower (v) (ed) | يخفض | technical (adj) | فنى

editor (n) | مُحرّر | measure (v) (d) | يقيس | willing (adj) | راغب/مستعد
evolution (n) | تطور | mention (v) (ed) | يذكر | zoologist (n) | عالم حيوانات

◆ التعبيرات والمصطلحات وحروف الجر | Expressions, Phrases & Prepositions
argue with | يتجادل مع | her words stayed with me | ظلت كلماتها معى
explain the matter in person | يشرح الأمر شخصيا | in the way I hoped | بالطريقة التى كنت أتمنى
get through | يخترق | stare at | يحدق في
give ... an appointment | يحدد موعدا للشخص | take a risk | يخاطر
give ... an idea | يعطى... فكرة | take my chance | أغتنم فرصتى
give my life a clearer purpose يعطى حياتى هدفا أكثر وضوحا | travel into | يسافر إلى
hear of | يسمع من | went straight to | اتجه مباشرة الى

◆ الفصل في نقاط | Chapter in Points
1. Edward Malone is a 23-year-old young reporter who works for the Daily Gazette in London. He is energetic and wants to do something important with his life.
- إدوارد مالون صحفي شاب يبلغ من العمر ٣٢ عاما ويعمل في جريدة ديلي جازيت في لندن. كان نشيطاً ويريد أن يفعل شيئا مهما في حياته.
2. Edward loves a young woman named Gladys Hungerton and decides to ask her to marry him.
- كان إدوارد يحب فتاة تدعى جلاديس هانجيرتون، وقرر أن يطلب منها الزواج.
3. Gladys tells Edward that she likes him as a friend, but she does not love him in the way he wishes.
- أخبرت جلاديس إدوارد أنها تحبه كصديق، لكنها لا تحبه بالطريقة التي يتمناها.
4. Gladys says that she could love a man who is brave, faces danger, travels to unknown places, and proves his courage.
- قالت جلاديس إنها يمكن أن تحب رجلاً شجاعاً يواجه الخطر، ويسافر إلى أماكن مجهولة، ويثبت شجاعته.
5. Gladys's words make Edward think about his life. He decides that he needs a difficult and dangerous challenge.
- جعلت كلمات جلاديس إدوارد يفكر في حياته. وقرر أنه يحتاج إلى تحدٍّ صعب وخطير.
6. The next day, Edward goes to his editor, Mr. McArdle, and asks him for a dangerous assignment.
- في اليوم التالي، ذهب إدوارد إلى رئيسه تحريره السيدين مكاردل وطلب منه مهمة خطيرة.
7. McArdle tells Edward about Professor George Edward Challenger, a famous but difficult zoologist.
- أخبر مكاردل إدوارد عن البروفيسور جورج إدوارد تشالنجر، وهو عالم حيوان مشهور لكنه صعب التعامل.
8. Challenger had returned from South America with a strange story about unknown animals. However, he refused to give scientists or reporters clear information.
- عاد تشالنجر من أمريكا الجنوبية بقصة غريبة عن حيوانات مجهولة. لكنه رفض أن يعطي العلماء أو الصحفيين معلومات واضحة.
9. McArdle asks Edward to discover whether Challenger's story is true or false.
- طلب مكاردل من إدوارد أن يكتشف ما إذا كانت قصة تشالنجر حقيقية أم كاذبة.
10. Edward learns that Challenger strongly dislikes journalists and usually refuses to speak to them.
- عرف إدوارد أن تشالنجر يكره الصحفيين بشدة، وعادة ما يرفض التحدث معهم.
11. Edward asks his friend Tarp Henry for help. He gets a scientific paper by Challenger and uses a difficult sentence from it in a letter.
- طلب إدوارد المساعدة من صديقه تارب هنري. وحصل على بحث علمي لتشالنجر واستخدم جملاً صعبة منه في رسالة.
12. In the letter, Edward pretends to be a young student who has a question about Challenger's scientific ideas.
- تظاهر إدوارد في الرسالة بأنه طالب شاب لديه سؤال عن أفكار تشالنجر العلمية.
13. Challenger replies to the letter. Although he says Edward does not understand the subject well, he agrees to meet him and explain it.
- رد تشالنجر على الرسالة. وغض أنه قال إن إدوارد لا يفهم الموضوع جيداً، فإنه وافق على مقابله وشرح الأمر له.
14. On Wednesday, Edward visits Challenger's large house in Kensington. Challenger's wife warns him that her husband can become very angry and difficult.
- يوم الأربعاء، ذهب إدوارد إلى منزل تشالنجر الكبير في كنسينجتون. وحذّرته زوجة تشالنجر أن زوجها قد يصبح غاضباً وصعب التعامل معه.
15. Edward enters Challenger's study and sees a strong, broad man with a large black beard and sharp grey-blue eyes.
- دخل إدوارد غرفة دراسة تشالنجر ورأى رجلاً قوياً عريض الجسم له لحية سوداء كبيرة وعينان حادتان رماديتان مزرقتان.
16. Challenger immediately questions Edward and challenges his knowledge.
- بدأ تشالنجر في استجواب إدوارد وتحدي معلوماته فوراً.

◆ نص الفصل | Chapter Text
My name is Edward Malone, and at the time this story began I was a young reporter on the Daily Gazette in London. I was twenty-three, full of energy, and certain that a great life was waiting for me. The problem was that I had no idea how to begin it. There was also Gladys Hungerton. I admired her more than anyone I had ever met, and one evening I finally asked her to marry me. She did not laugh at me or become angry. Instead, she looked serious and told me that she valued our friendship but did not love me in the way I hoped.
"What kind of man could you love?" I asked.
Gladys thought for a moment. "A man who has done something brave," she said. "Someone who has faced danger, traveled into the unknown, and proved that he can act when it matters."
I tried to joke. "So, I must discover a new country before you will consider me?""Not for me," she replied. "If a man is truly brave, he does not act just to impress someone else. He acts because courage is part of his character."
Her words stayed with me. I left her house feeling disappointed, but also strangely excited.
London suddenly seemed too safe. I wanted a challenge - something that would test me and give my life a clearer purpose.
The next evening, I went straight to my editor, Mr. McArdle. He was an experienced newspaper man who had seen every kind of young reporter. When I asked him to send me on a dangerous assignment, he pushed his glasses up and stared at me. "You want danger?" he said. "Most sensible people want to avoid it." "I want a real story, sir. Something difficult."
He studied me for a moment, then smiled. "Perhaps I do have a job for you. Have you heard of Professor George Edward Challenger?"
Of course I had. Almost everyone in the newspaper world knew his name.

Challenger was a famous zoologist, brilliant but extremely difficult. He had recently returned from South America with an extraordinary story about unknown animals. When scientists questioned him, he refused to give clear details. When reporters tried to interview him, he often threw them out of his house - sometimes literally.
"Do you want me to interview him?" I asked.
"If you can get through the front door," McArdle replied. "Two years ago, Challenger traveled alone through a little-known part of South America. He came back claiming that something remarkable had happened. He had damaged photographs, strange sketches, and a few pieces of evidence, but he would not reveal the exact place. Most people think he is either mistaken or lying. Find out which."
This was exactly the kind of chance I had been looking for. Yet there was one problem: Challenger hated journalists. If I arrived honestly and said I worked for the Gazette, the interview would probably end before it began.
That night I went to the Savage Club, where many writers and scientists met. I found my friend Tarp Henry, who worked for a scientific journal, and asked what he knew about Challenger.
"He is clever," Henry said, "but impossible. He argues with everyone. At a scientific meeting in Vienna, he almost started a battle over a theory of evolution. If you want to speak to him, do not mention newspapers."
That gave me an idea. I asked Henry to find Challenger's latest scientific paper. It was full of technical language I barely understood, but I copied one complicated sentence and used it in a letter. I pretended to be a young student who admired Challenger's work but had a question about one of his arguments.
Henry read the letter and laughed. "You are taking a risk, Malone. He may realize at once that you know nothing about the subject."
"I only need to get inside his study," I said. "After that, I will take my chance."
Two days later a reply arrived. The handwriting looked as aggressive as the man himself. Challenger wrote that my question showed poor understanding, but he was willing to explain the matter in person. He gave me an appointment for eleven o'clock on Wednesday morning.
I should have been nervous. Instead, I felt as if my real life had finally begun. On Wednesday I traveled to Professor Challenger's large house in Kensington. A quiet servant named Austin opened the door. Before he could take me to the study, a small, lively woman stopped me in the hall.
"You have come to see my husband?" she asked. "Yes, Mrs. Challenger." She lowered her voice. "Then I should warn you. George is a perfectly impossible person. If he becomes angry, do not argue too long. Leave the room while you still can."

I smiled, but her expression told me she was serious.
A deep voice suddenly roared from behind a closed door. Austin opened it, and I entered. Professor Challenger sat behind a wide desk covered with books, maps, fossils, and papers.
He was shorter than I expected, but enormously broad. His black beard spread across his chest, and his sharp grey-blue eyes seemed to measure every weakness in me.
"Well?" he demanded. "You are the young man who has difficulty understanding plain science?"
I had entered looking for adventure. I had found it sooner than I expected.

◆ الأسئلة والأجوبة | Questions and Answers
1. Explain how Gladys's idea of courage changes Malone's plans for his future. [SB]
- اشرح كيف غيرت فكرة جلاديس للشجاعة خطط مالون لمستقبله.
• Gladys says she could only love a brave man who has faced danger. This makes Malone feel his safe life in London is not enough, so he decides to seek adventure.
2. Describe Malone's personality as shown through his conversation with Gladys and his visit to McArdle.
- صف شخصية مالون كما تجلت من خلال محادثته مع جلاديس وزيارته لمكاردل.
• Malone seems hopeful, bold, and a bit impulsive مندفع. He doesn't give up after Gladys says no — he quickly goes to McArdle looking for adventure.
3. Why does Malone ask Mr. McArdle to give him a dangerous assignment? [SB]
- لماذا يطلب مالون من السيد مكاردل تكليفه بمهمة خطيرة؟
• He wants to prove he is brave, inspired by Gladys, and he wants a real challenge in his life.
4. Why does Tarp Henry laugh when Malone tells him his plan?
- لماذا يضحك تارب هنري عندما يخبره مالون بخطته؟
• He laughs because the plan seems risky—Malone knows little science but is pretending to be a smart student. Henry thinks Challenger might notice the trick.

5. Explain why Malone writes to Professor Challenger as if he understands Challenger's scientific work.
[SB]
– اشرح لماذا يكتب مالون إلى البروفيسور تشالنجر كما لو كان يفهم عمله العلمي.
• Challenger hates journalists, so Malone pretends to be an admiring student and copies a line from Challenger's paper to trick him into a meeting.
6. Analyze what Mr. McArdle's reaction to Malone's request reveals about Malone's attitude to danger.
[SB]
– حلل ما يكشفه رد فعل السيد مكاردل على طلب مالون عن موقف مالون من الخطر.
• McArdle is surprised, saying "most sensible people want to avoid" danger. This shows Malone is unusually bold and eager for risk.
7. Analyze the role Tarp Henry plays in helping Malone gain access to Professor Challenger.
[SB]
– حلل دور تارب هنري في مساعدة مالون على الوصول إلى البروفيسور تشالنجر.
• Henry warns Malone about Challenger's temper and helps him get Challenger's paper, which Malone uses in his trick letter.
8. Why does Challenger refuse to reveal the exact location of his discoveries in South America?
– لماذا يرفض تشالنجر الكشف عن الموقع الدقيق لاكتشافاته في أمريكا الجنوبية؟
• The text doesn't say exactly why, but it seems Challenger doesn't trust others and wants to protect his discovery. This makes people suspect he is lying.
9. Explain why Professor Challenger has a bad reputation among scientists and journalists.
– اشرح لماذا يتمتع البروفيسور تشالنجر بسمعة سيئة بين العلماء والصحفيين.
• Challenger is brilliant but very difficult. He argues with people and throws journalists out of his house. His claims also have no clear proof, so people doubt him.
10. How do Mrs. Challenger's warning and Challenger's first appearance build suspense at the end of the chapter?
[SB]
– كيف يُسهم تحذير السيدة تشالنجر وظهور تشالنجر الأول في بناء التشويق في نهاية الفصل؟
• Mrs Challenger warns that her husband is "impossible" and tells Malone to leave if he gets angry. Then a roar and Challenger's annoying appearance build tension.
11. What does Mrs Challenger's behavior in the hallway suggest about her relationship with her husband?
– بماذا يوحي سلوك السيدة تشالنجر في الردهة بشأن علاقتها بزوجها؟
• She seems caring but also used to his temper مزاج. She warns Malone kindly, showing that she tries to protect visitors from her husband's anger.
12. Analyze how the setting of Challenger's study reflects his character.
– حلل كيف يعكس مكتب تشالنجر شخصيته.
• The messy desk full of books, maps, and fossils shows that Challenger is a serious, busy scientist. It also reflects his big, intense personality.

13. Evaluate Malone's decision to use deception in order to get an interview with Challenger. Was his method acceptable? Give reasons.
[SB]
– قيّم قرار مالون باستخدام الخداع للحصول على مقابلة مع تشالنجر. هل كانت طريقته مقبولة؟ اذكر الأسباب.
• Some may say it was fair since honest access was impossible; others may say lying is wrong even with good reason.
14. Evaluate Malone's desire for adventure at this stage. Does it show maturity, impulsiveness, or both? Explain.
[SB]
– قيّم رغبة مالون في المغامرة في هذه المرحلة. هل تدل على نضج، أم اندفاع، أم كليهما؟ اشرح.
• Both. Maturity, because he turns disappointment into action; impulsiveness, as he acts mainly to impress Gladys and rushes into a risky trick.
15. Evaluate the ending line, "I had entered looking for adventure. I had found it sooner than I expected." What does it suggest?
– قم بتقييم جملة النهاية: «دخلت أبحث عن المغامرة. لقد وجدتها في وقت أقرب مما كنت أتوقع». بماذا توحي هذه العبارة؟
• It hints that Malone's meeting with Challenger will be dramatic and difficult away, not calm or simple.

◆ JRE — نماذج إجابة | JRE Model Answers (Justify – Reason – Evidence)
Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.
1. Is Gladys a positive influence on Malone? SB – هل تُعتبر جلاديس مصدراً إيجابياً لتأثير مالون؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, mostly positive.
Reasoning : Explain how her words affect Malone's choices.
Answer : Her words push him to grow instead of just feeling hurt.
Evidence : Support your answer with at least two details from the chapter.
Answer : She says she could love "a man who has faced danger"; Malone feels "disappointed, but also strangely excited."
2. Is Malone truly ready for adventure? SB – هل مالون مستعد حقاً للمغامرة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : No, not fully.
Reasoning : Explain what his actions reveal about his readiness.
Answer : He acts on excitement, not careful planning.
Evidence : Use evidence from his behavior with McArdle, Tarp Henry, or Challenger.
Answer : He asks McArdle for danger almost casually; he tricks Challenger with a fake letter instead of weighing the risk Henry warns him about; and he ignores Mrs. Challenger's warning to leave "while you still can," admitting he felt excited rather than nervous.

3. Is Malone a determined person? – هل مالون شخص مصمم على تحقيق هدفه؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Malone is determined because he does not give up easily.
Reasoning : Explain how Malone works to achieve his goal.
Answer : He finds different ways to get an interview with Challenger.
Evidence : Use evidence from his actions with McArdle and Challenger.
Answer : He asks McArdle for a dangerous assignment and creates a plan to meet Challenger.
4. Is Malone's decision to hide the truth from Challenger justified? – هل يُعد قرار مالون بإخفاء الحقيقة عن تشالنجر مبرراً؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, it was understandable because Challenger hated journalists.
Reasoning : Explain why Malone chooses to pretend to be a student.
Answer : He knows that Challenger may refuse to meet him if he knows he is a reporter.
Evidence : Give at least two details that show why this plan was necessary.
Answer : Challenger often rejected journalists, and he had even thrown reporters out of his house.
5. Is Mr. McArdle helpful to Malone? – هل كان السيد مكاردل مفيداً لمالون؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, McArdle helps Malone by giving him a chance to find a real story.
Reasoning : Explain how McArdle helps Malone begin his adventure.
Answer : He tells Malone about Challenger and gives him a difficult assignment.
Evidence : Give at least two details from their conversation.
Answer : He tells Malone about Challenger's journey to South America and asks him to discover the truth.
6. Does Malone show good problem-solving skills? – هل يُظهر مالون مهارات جيدة في حل المشكلات؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Malone shows good problem-solving skills.
Reasoning : Explain how Malone solves the problem of getting an interview with Challenger.
Answer : He makes a clever plan and pretends to be a student.
Evidence : Use details about his letter, the scientific paper, and his plan.
Answer : He copies a difficult sentence from Challenger's paper and uses it in his letter.

◆ الفصل في نقاط | Chapter in Points
1. Professor Challenger questions Malone about a scientific article and quickly realizes that he knows very little about the subject.
— يستجوب البروفيسور تشالنجر مالون بشأن مقال علمي، وسرعان ما يدرك أنه لا يعرف الكثير عن الموضوع.
2. Malone admits that he is a reporter for the Daily Gazette.
— يعترف مالون بأنه مراسل لصحيفة ديلي جازيت.
3. Challenger becomes furious because Malone entered his house using a false name.
— يغضب تشالنجر بشدة لأن مالون دخل منزله مستخدما اسما مستعارا.
4. They struggle, and Malone is injured, but he decides not to press charges because he feels partly responsible.
— يتشاجران، ويُصاب مالون، لكنه يقرر عدم تقديم شكوى لأنه يشعر بمسؤولية جزئية.
5. Challenger respects Malone's honesty and courage and invites him back into his study.
— يحترم تشالنجر صدق مالون وشجاعته، ويدعوه للعودة إلى مكتبه.
6. Challenger shows him the sketchbook of Maple White, an American explorer who died in the Amazon.
— يُريه تشالنجر دفتر رسومات مابل وايت، وهو مستكشف أمريكي توفي في الأمازون.
7. The sketches show strange red cliffs, an isolated rock tower, and a huge animal resembling a stegosaurus.
— تُظهر الرسومات منحدرات حمراء غريبة، وبرجا صخريا معزولاً، وحيوانا ضخما يُشبه الستيجوصور.
8. Challenger explains that he followed Maple White's route and discovered the same mysterious plateau in South America.
— يشرح تشالنجر أنه اتبع مسار مابل وايت واكتشف الهضبة الغامضة نفسها في أمريكا الجنوبية.
9. He also shows Malone a damaged photograph and a piece of wing that he claims belonged to a pterodactyl.
— كما يُري مالون صورة فوتوغرافية تالفة وقطعة جناح يدعي أنها تعود إلى تيروداكتيل.
10. Challenger believes that prehistoric animals survived on the isolated plateau because it had been separated from the outside world for millions of years.
— يعتقد تشالنجر أن الحيوانات التي عاشت في عصور ما قبل التاريخ نجت على الهضبة المعزولة لأنها كانت منفصلة عن العالم الخارجي لملايين السنين.
11. Other scientists had laughed at Challenger's claims, but he remained completely certain that his discoveries were real.
— سخَر علماء آخرون من مزاعم تشالنجر، لكنه ظل على يقين تام بصحة اكتشافاته.
12. Malone gradually begins to believe Challenger's story.
— يبدأ مالون تدريجيا بتصدق رواية تشالنجر.
13. Challenger invites Malone to attend a public lecture at the Zoological Institute.
— دعا تشالنجر مالون لحضور محاضرة عامة في معهد علم الحيوان.
14. Malone leaves excited, realizing that a great adventure may be about to begin.
— غادر مالون متحمسا، مدركا أن مغامرة عظيمة قد تكون على وشك البدء.

◆ نص الفصل | Chapter Text
Professor Challenger began by asking me questions about the scientific article I had mentioned in my letter.
At first I tried to answer confidently, but the questions became more technical every minute.
"And what do you think of the recent evidence?" he asked. "Very interesting," I said carefully.
"Which evidence?" I hesitated.
His eyes narrowed. "You know nothing about this subject."
There was no point pretending any longer. "Very little," I admitted. "Then why are you here?"
I took a breath. "I am a reporter for the Daily Gazette."
For one silent second, nothing happened. Then Challenger rose from his chair like a storm. "A journalist! In my house!"
I moved towards the door. "I came because the public wants to understand what happened in South America."
"The public!" he roared. "You mean people who laugh before they listen. Out!"
He came towards me. I told him firmly not to touch me. He ignored the warning. A moment later we were struggling in the hallway, crashing into a chair and rolling through the open front door. We landed together on the steps outside.
A policeman hurried over. Several neighbors stopped to watch. "What happened?" the policeman asked.
My eye was beginning to swell, and Challenger's coat was torn. I could easily have made a complaint. Instead, I remembered that I had entered his house under a false name and had deliberately tricked him.

"It was partly my fault," I said. "I will not press charges."
The policeman looked surprised, but closed his notebook. Challenger stared at me. The anger in his face changed into something almost like respect.
"Come back inside," he said.
I followed him, wondering whether I was brave or simply foolish.
Back in the study, Challenger closed the door and became suddenly serious. "You behaved better than I would expect from a reporter," he said. "Perhaps I will show you something. But you must promise not to publish it without my permission."
I gave him my word. He opened a worn sketchbook. "Two years ago I traveled through the Amazon region to study animal life. In a remote village I found a dying American named Maple White. He had reached the village alone after terrible hardship. He died before I could question him."
Among White's belongings, Challenger had found the sketchbook. Most of the early pages showed ordinary river scenes, plants, people, and animals. Then the drawings changed.
One picture showed a line of high red cliffs rising above thick forest. The cliffs seemed to form an enormous natural wall. Another showed a separate rock tower standing near them. On top was a tree.
"A strange landscape," I said, "but hardly impossible." "Continue."
The next drawing stopped me. It showed a huge animal with a heavy body, long tail, and plates rising along its back.
"What is that?"
"That," said Challenger, "is the question. Maple White drew what he saw."
He opened a reference book and showed me an illustration of a prehistoric stegosaurus.
The similarity was remarkable.
"A man can draw anything," I said.
"Of course. Which is why I went to find the place myself."
Challenger explained that he followed White's route using clues in the sketchbook. After weeks of travel, he found the red cliffs. He could not climb onto the high plateau, but he saw extraordinary creatures there. He took photographs, though most were damaged later in a river accident.
He showed me one photograph. The image was unclear, but I could see the cliffs and the isolated rock tower. On the tree at the top appeared a large dark shape.
"A bird?" I suggested.

Challenger shook his head. "Look more closely."
He handed me a magnifying glass. The creature did have wings, but its outline was unlike any bird I knew.
Then Challenger opened a drawer and removed a long curved bone with a piece of dried membrane attached.
"This was part of the creature's wing," he said. "I shot it, but most of the body was lost in the river accident."
At first I thought it might come from a giant bat. Challenger pointed out the structure of the bone and compared it with a diagram in a zoology book. Slowly I understood what he was saying.
"A pterodactyl?" I whispered.

"Exactly. A flying reptile from a prehistoric age." I looked again at Maple White's drawing, the damaged photograph, and the piece of wing. One item alone might have been explained away. Together they were much harder to dismiss. "You really believe prehistoric animals are still alive on that plateau?" "I do not believe," Challenger said proudly. "I know."
He explained his theory. The plateau had been cut off from the surrounding world for an extremely long time. Its high cliffs formed a natural barrier. Plants and animals that disappeared elsewhere might have survived there in isolation.
"Then why not return with other scientists?" I asked.
His expression darkened. He had tried to speak publicly after returning to London, but people had laughed, accused him of creating false photographs, and demanded exact directions. Insulted, he had refused to say more.
"Yet truth does not become false because fools refuse to accept it," he said.
For the first time, I saw more than the rude, explosive man described by the newspapers. Challenger was proud and difficult, but he was also completely certain of what he had seen. I believed that he believed his story. More than that, I was beginning to believe it too.

Before I left, he told me about a public lecture at the Zoological Institute the following evening. A well-known speaker would discuss the history of life on Earth. Challenger expected his South American claims to be mentioned.
"Attend if you wish," he said. "You may find the evening educational."
As I stepped into the London street, the ordinary traffic seemed suddenly unreal. Somewhere beyond the Atlantic, perhaps, there was a high red wall of rock where creatures from millions of years ago still moved through the forest. I had wanted a great adventure. Now I knew where it might begin.

◆ الأسئلة والأجوبة | Questions and Answers
1. Why did Malone hesitate when Challenger asked him about "the recent evidence"?
- لماذا تردد مالون عندما سأله تشالنجر عن «الأدلة الحديثة»؟
• Malone had been giving unclear, cautious answers like "very interesting" to seem knowledgeable. When Challenger pressed him to name the specific evidence, Malone realized he had nothing real to say. His hesitation exposed that he had been tricking his way through the conversation from the start.
2. Explain why Challenger became furious when he learned Malone was a journalist.
- اشرح لماذا غضب تشالنجر عندما علم أن مالون صحفي.
• Challenger already hated journalists because he believed the press had mocked his earlier scientific claims. When Malone admitted he worked for the Daily Gazette, Challenger felt personally deceived, since Malone had entered his home under false pretenses. His anger reflected both his wounded pride and his deep distrust of the public's willingness to listen fairly.
3. Describe what happened during the struggle between Challenger and Malone, from the study to the front steps.
- صف ما حدث خلال الصراع بين تشالنجر ومالون، من غرفة الدراسة إلى الدرجات الأمامية.
• After ordering Malone out, Challenger advanced toward him despite being told not to touch him. The two men began fighting, crashing into a chair as the struggle carried them through the open front door. They ended up rolling together onto the front steps, where a policeman and neighbors witnessed the struggle.
4. Why did Malone decide not to press charges against Challenger, even though he was injured?
- لماذا قرر مالون عدم توجه الاتهامات ضد تشالنجر على الرغم من إصابته؟
• Malone remembered that he had entered the house dishonestly, using a false name to trick Challenger into speaking with him. He recognized that this deception had helped start the fight in the first place. Feeling he shared the blame, he told the policeman it was partly his own fault and refused to press charges.

5. Why does Challenger invite Malone back into the house after their fight? SB
- لماذا دعا تشالنجر مالون للعودة إلى المنزل بعد شجارهما؟
• Malone could have complained to the policeman but instead admitted the fault was partly his own and refused to press charges. This honesty impresses Challenger and turns his anger into respect.
6. Explain how Maple White's sketchbook, the damaged photograph, and the wing fragment support Challenger's claim. SB
- اشرح كيف تدعم كراسة رسم مابل وايت، والصورة التالفة، وشفرة الجناح ادعاء تشالنجر.
• The sketchbook shows a creature resembling a stegosaurus; the photograph shows the same cliffs with a strange winged shape; the wing bone matches both. Alone, each could be doubted, but together they point to the same real creature.
7. Explain Challenger's theory about how prehistoric animals could survive on the isolated plateau. SB
- اشرح نظرية تشالنجر حول كيفية بقاء حيوانات ما قبل التاريخ على الهضبة المعزولة.
• The plateau's high cliffs cut it off from the rest of the world for a very long time, so animals that died out elsewhere could have survived there in isolation.
8. Analyze how Challenger's difficult personality may make people doubt a story that may actually be true. SB
- حلل كيف يمكن لشخصية تشالنجر الصعبة أن تجعل الناس يشككون في قصة قد تكون صحيحة بالفعل.
• His temper and pride make him react with anger and refusal instead of calmly presenting proof, so people focus on his behavior rather than fairly considering his evidence.
9. Analyze how Malone's attitude changes from scepticism to growing belief during the chapter. SB
- حلل كيف يتغير موقف مالون من الشك إلى الإيمان المتزاید خلال الفصل.
• Malone starts doubtful, arguing that a drawing proves nothing. As Challenger shows the photograph and wing bone and explains his theory, Malone's doubt changes, and by the end he admits he is starting to believe.
10. Why are the different pieces of evidence more convincing together than any one piece would be alone? SB
- لماذا تعتبر الأدلة المختلفة مجتمعة أكثر إقناعا من أي دليل منفرد؟
• Any single item could be faked or misunderstood, but since the drawing, photo, and bone all match and support each other, it proves to be likely real.
11. Evaluate Challenger's decision to keep the exact location of the plateau secret. Was he justified? SB
- قيّم قرار تشالنجر بالحفاظ على الموقع الدقيق للهضبة سرا. هل كان مُبررا؟
• He may be justified since he was laughed at and accused of lying before, so secrecy protects him from further ridicule. But it also stops others from believing his claim, which keeps the mystery unresolved.

12. Evaluate the effect of Challenger's violent behavior on his scientific credibility. Does it weaken his case?
[SB]
- قيّم تأثير سلوك تشالنجر العنيف على مصداقيته العلمية. هل يُضعف حجته؟
• It weakens how he is understood, making him seem unstable and giving people an easy reason to disbelieve him. But his passion seems driven by genuine belief, not dishonesty, so it hurts his reputation more than it disproves his evidence.
13. Explain what condition Challenger set before showing Malone the sketchbook.
- اشرح الشرط الذي وضعه تشالنجر قبل أن يُري مالون دفتر الرسم.
• Challenger insisted that Malone give his word not to publish anything about the sketchbook without permission. This condition reflected Challenger's fear of being laughed at again by the press.
14. Analyze why Malone's decision to lie about his identity to gain entry was risky, given how the meeting turned out.
- حلل لماذا كان قرار مالون بالكذب بشأن هويته للدخول محفوفاً بالمخاطر، بالنظر إلى كيفية انتهاء الاجتماع.
• Malone's lie could easily have destroyed any chance of gaining Challenger's trust, as Challenger already hated dishonest journalists. If Malone had pressed charges or acted selfishly after the fight, Challenger would have shut him out completely. Instead, his honesty afterward turned a risky deception into, a trusting relationship.
15. Analyze the significance of the phrase "truth does not become false because fools refuse to accept it." What does it reveal about Challenger's character?
- حلل دلالة عبارة «الحقيقة لا تصبح زيفاً لأن الحمقى يرفضون قبولها». ماذا تكشف هذه العبارة عن شخصية تشالنجر؟
• This statement reveals Challenger's confidence in what he personally witnessed, regardless of public opinion. It shows that he values factual truth far more than social approval or reputation.
16. Evaluate Malone's choice to attend Challenger's suggested lecture at the Zoological Institute. What does this decision suggest about his transformation as a character?
- قيّم قرار مالون بحضور محاضرة تشالنجر المقترحة في معهد علم الحيوان. إلى ماذا يُشير قرارُهُ كشخصية؟
• Earlier in the chapter, Malone questioned and doubted much of Challenger's evidence. By agreeing to attend the lecture, he actively chooses to continue discovering the mystery rather than walking away. This decision suggests that his scepticism has shifted into real curiosity and a growing belief in Challenger's claims.

◆ JRE — نماذج إجابة | JRE Model Answers (Justify – Reason – Evidence)
Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.
1. Is Malone justified in believing Challenger by the end of the chapter? SB
هل كان مالون محقّاً في تصديق تشالنجر في نهاية الفصل؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Malone is right to start believing him.
Reasoning : Explain why the evidence changes, or fails to change, Malone's view.
Answer : At first, Malone doubts him, saying anyone could draw a strange animal. But Challenger shows more than one clue, and they all match. One drawing could be fake, but a matching photo and a real bone too make it hard to doubt. This is what changes Malone's mind.
Evidence : Use specific evidence Challenger shows Malone.
Answer : Malone sees the drawing that looks like a stegosaurus, the damaged photo of the same cliffs, and the actual wing bone. By the end, he says he is "beginning to believe" the story.
2. Can Challenger be considered a trustworthy scientist despite his rude and aggressive behavior? SB
هل يمكن اعتبار تشالنجر عالماً جديراً بالثقة رغم سلوكه الفظ والعدواني؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, even though he is rude, his science can still be trusted.
Reasoning : Separate his personality from the quality of his evidence.
Answer : Being bad-tempered doesn't mean his work is wrong. He gets angry because people laughed at him before, not because he is lying. His actual methods are careful and scientific.
Evidence : Support your answer with at least two details from the chapter.
Answer : He traveled to the Amazon himself to check if the sketchbook was true. He also compared his wing bone to a picture in a science book to prove it was real.
3. Do you think Malone's decision to trust Challenger by the end of the chapter is based on solid evidence or on Challenger's strong personality?
هل تعتقد أن قرار مالون بالوثوق في تشالنجر في نهاية الفصل يستند إلى أدلة قوية أم إلى شخصية تشالنجر القوية؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Malone's growing belief is based mainly on evidence, not personality.
Reasoning : Explain the difference between being persuaded by evidence and being persuaded by personality.
Answer : Being persuaded by evidence means being convinced by facts and proof, while being persuaded by personality means being affected by confidence or force of character alone, which is not real proof.

Evidence : Use specific evidence from the chapter.
Answer : Malone remains sceptical even while Challenger is being passionate and forceful ("A man can draw anything," he says). His belief only grows after seeing the photograph and wing bone match the sketchbook.
4. Was Challenger justified in physically confronting Malone once he discovered the deception?
هل كان تشالنجر محقّاً في مواجهة مالون جسدياً بمجرد اكتشافه للخداع؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : No, not fully justified, even though his anger is understandable.
Reasoning : Consider Challenger's past experiences with journalists and how they may have shaped his reaction.
Answer : Challenger had good reason to feel upset, since Malone lied to get into his house. But being angry does not mean it was right to attack him. Using violence instead of just asking Malone to leave made things worse than they needed to be.
Evidence : Use specific details from the chapter about Challenger's history with the press and his behavior during the fight.
Answer : The text says Challenger had already been laughed at and called a liar by reporters before. This made him hate journalists. So when he found out Malone was one, he got extremely angry and moved toward him, starting the fight—even after Malone warned him not to touch him.
5. Did Malone deserve Challenger's trust and respect after their fight?
هل استحق مالون ثقة واحترام تشالنجر بعد نزالهما؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Malone earned Challenger's trust.
Reasoning : Consider Malone's choice not to press charges and what this reveals about his character.
Answer : Even though Malone had lied earlier, he acted honestly right after the fight. Instead of blaming Challenger to get him in trouble, Malone admitted that the fight was partly his own fault. This honest behavior showed Challenger that Malone had good character, even if his first move was dishonest.
Evidence : Use details about the fight, the policeman's reaction, and Malone's own words.
Answer : The policeman was surprised when Malone said, "It was partly my fault," and refused to press charges. Right after this, the text says Challenger's anger "changed into something almost like respect," showing that Malone's honesty directly caused Challenger to trust him.

◆ JRE — تمارين | JRE Exercises (Justify – Reason – Evidence)
Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.
1. Was Challenger right to stay silent in London instead of trying to convince his critics with patience?
Justify : State your judgment.
Reasoning : Explain how his choice affected how others saw him.
Evidence : Use at least two details from the text.
2. Was Malone right to lie about his identity in order to meet Challenger?
Justify : State your judgment.
Reasoning : Weigh Malone's journalistic purpose against the dishonesty of his method.
Evidence : Support your answer with details about how Malone gained entry and how he behaved afterward.
3. Was Challenger right to keep the plateau's exact location a secret from the public?
Justify : State your judgment.
Reasoning : Consider both the risks of revealing the location and the risks of concealing it.
Evidence : Use details about Challenger's past humiliation and his reasons for secrecy.
4. Should Malone have left the house immediately after Challenger told him to get out?
Justify : State your judgment.
Reasoning : Consider what Malone actually did compared to what Challenger did next.
Evidence : Use details about the moment right before the fight began.
5. Was Maple White's sketchbook enough on its own to prove Challenger's story?
Justify : State your judgment.
Reasoning : Think about why a drawing alone might not be enough proof.
Evidence : Use Malone's own reaction to the sketchbook drawings.
6. Did Malone's job as a journalist help or hurt his search for the truth in this chapter?
Justify : State your judgment.
Reasoning : Think about both the problems and the benefits his job caused.
Evidence : Use details from both the fight and the later conversation.

◆ الفصل في نقاط | Chapter in Points
1. The Zoological Institute was crowded with scientists, students, journalists, and members of the public.
- كان معهد علم الحيوان مزدحما بالعلماء والطلاب والصحفيين والأفراد.
2. Professor Challenger defended the idea that prehistoric creatures might still exist.
- دافع البروفيسور تشالنجر عن فكرة أن مخلوقات ما قبل التاريخ قد تكون ما زالت موجودة.
3. Professor Summerlee challenged Challenger and demanded scientific proof.
- تحدى البروفيسور سمرلي تشالنجر وطلب دليلا علميا.
4. Edward Malone volunteered to join the expedition as a journalist.
- تطوع إدوارد مالون للانضمام إلى الرحلة الاستكشافية بصفته صحفيا.
5. Lord John Roxton, a famous explorer, also volunteered to join the expedition.
- تطوع اللورد جون روكسون، وهو مستكشف مشهور للانضمام إلى الرحلة أيضا.
6. Challenger chose Malone, Summerlee, and Roxton to investigate the mysterious evidence.
- اختار تشالنجر مالون وسمرلي وروكسون للتحقيق في الأدلة الغامضة.
7. Roxton told Malone about his experience in the Amazon and his knowledge of the region.
- أخبر روكسون مالون عن خبرته في منطقة الأمازون ومعرفته بالمنطقة.
8. The team prepared carefully for the dangerous journey to South America.
- استعد الفريق جيدا للرحلة الخطرة إلى أمريكا الجنوبية.
9. Summerlee remained doubtful about Challenger's claims, but he agreed to investigate them.
- ظل سمرلي مشككا في ادعاءات تشالنجر، لكنه وافق على التحقيق فيها.
10. The explorers traveled to Brazil and hired local helpers, including Zambo, Gomez, and Manuel.
- سافر المستكشفون إلى البرازيل واستأجروا مساعدين محليين، من بينهم زامبو وجوميز ومانويل.
11. They were told to open Challenger's sealed envelope in Manaos at exactly noon on 15 July.
- طلب منهم فتح مظروف تشالنجر المختوم في ماناوس في تمام الظهيرة يوم 15 يوليو.
12. To their surprise, the envelope contained a completely blank sheet of paper.
- ولدهشتهم، احتوى المظروف على ورقة بيضاء تماما.
13. Challenger suddenly appeared and explained that he had secretly traveled to Brazil.
- ظهر تشالنجر فجأة وشرح أنه سافر سرا إلى البرازيل.
14. He decided to guide the expedition himself because written instructions were not reliable enough.
- قرر أن يقود الرحلة بنفسه لأن التعليمات المكتوبة لم تكن موثوقة بما يكفي.
15. The four men were now ready to explore the unknown Amazon and discover whether a lost world really existed.
- أصبح الرجال الأربعة الآن مستعدين لاستكشاف منطقة الأمازون المجهولة ومعرفة ما إذا كان هناك عالم مفقود بالفعل.

◆ نص الفصل | Chapter Text
The lecture hall of the Zoological Institute was crowded. Scientists, students, journalists, and members of the public filled every seat. Professor Challenger sat near the front, impossible to miss because of his great black beard and powerful build.
At first the lecture was calm. The speaker described how life had changed over millions of years. Then he mentioned stories of prehistoric creatures surviving in remote regions. There was laughter from part of the audience.
Challenger stood at once.
"There is nothing amusing about evidence," he said loudly.
The chairman asked him to sit down, but the room was already excited. Professor Summerlee, a thin, elderly scientist with a sharp face and an even sharper voice, challenged him directly.
"If Professor Challenger has really found living prehistoric animals," Summerlee said, "Let him tell us where they are and provide proof that can be tested."
Challenger faced him. "Would you be willing to test it yourself?" The room became silent.
Summerlee lifted his chin. "Yes."
"Then I will give a properly chosen committee enough information to reach the place. You may examine it with your own eyes."
The audience exploded in cheers and questions.
Challenger raised a hand. "Professor Summerlee should not travel alone. The journey will be difficult and dangerous. He will require younger companions. Who volunteers?"
I do not remember deciding to stand. One moment I was seated; the next I was on my feet. "Edward Malone, Daily Gazette," I called. "I volunteer."
Another man stood at almost the same time. He was tall, thin, and sun-browned, with calm blue eyes.

"Lord John Roxton," he said. "I know the Amazon country and have traveled there before."

Everyone recognized the name. Lord John was famous as an explorer, sportsman, and hunter. Challenger immediately proposed that Summerlee, Roxton, and I should form the investigating party.

The motion was accepted. In a few astonishing minutes, my future had changed completely.

Outside the hall Lord John caught up with me. "So we are to be traveling companions, Mr Malone. Come to my rooms. We should get to know one another."

His home was full of maps, books, equipment, and trophies from earlier journeys. Yet he spoke without showing off. He wanted to know whether I could shoot, climb, swim, and keep calm under pressure.

"I can learn what I do not know," I told him.

"Good answer. South America is not a place for a man who thinks he already knows everything."

Lord John told me that during one of his earlier journeys, he had become involved in a serious dispute with a powerful rubber-trading group led by a man named Pedro Lopez. Local workers were being treated unfairly and forced to work under terrible conditions. Lord John helped them escape from the group's control and brought an end to Lopez's influence in the area.

"It made me some loyal friends," he said, "and some dangerous enemies. But I know the rivers, the language used by many local traders and guides, and the kind of men we may need. That could keep us alive."

Over the next weeks we prepared for the expedition. Professor Summerlee remained openly doubtful about Challenger's claims, but he was determined to investigate them properly. I respected his honesty. He might be sceptical, but he was willing to risk a long journey to discover the truth.

Before we left England, I visited Gladys.

"I am going to South America," I told her. "There may be real danger."

For a moment her eyes brightened with interest. "Then you have found your chance."

I wanted to say that I was doing it for her, but I remembered her earlier words. A brave act done only to win approval was not true courage. So I simply said, "I hope I can make good use of it."

We sailed across the Atlantic and reached Brazil. At Para we hired several men to help us. The most important was Zambo, a huge, cheerful man who was strong, loyal, and practical. We also employed two experienced river men, Gomez and Manuel, and several Indigenous boatmen who knew the upper waterways far better than any European traveler.

Lord John chose the team carefully. Summerlee spent much of his time studying insects and plants. I kept notes for the Gazette. Every day the expedition felt more real.

Our written instructions from Challenger were to be opened at Manaos at exactly noon on 15 July. The envelope remained sealed during the journey.

At last the day arrived. We sat around a table in a shaded house outside Manaos. Lord John placed his watch beside the envelope.

"Noon," he said, and cut it open. Inside was a single sheet of paper. It was blank. Summerlee stared at it. "This is ridiculous."

Before anyone could answer, a deep familiar voice came from the doorway. "On the contrary, Professor Summerlee. It is perfectly sensible."

Professor Challenger walked into the room.

He had secretly traveled to Brazil and planned to guide us himself. No written directions, he explained, could be as reliable as his own memory. He had kept his decision private because he did not want arguments, reporters, or curious travelers following him.

Summerlee looked horrified. "You mean I must spend the whole journey with you?"

"The scientific value of my company will more than compensate for any personal discomfort," Challenger replied. Lord John laughed.

"Then our team is complete."

Challenger spread a map across the table, though the final destination was not marked.

"We leave tomorrow," he said. "From this point onwards, we are going into a country that very few outsiders have seen. Later we will go beyond even that."

I looked around the table at the four of us—Challenger, Summerlee, Lord John, and myself.

We were different in age, character, and purpose. Yet we now shared one question. Was there truly a lost world waiting beyond the Amazon?

◆ الأسئلة والأجوبة | Questions and Answers

1. Why does Professor Challenger believe that the evidence of prehistoric creatures should be taken seriously?

– لماذا يعتقد البروفيسور تشالنجر أنه ينبغي أخذ الأدلة المتعلقة بالكائنات ما قبل التاريخ على محمل الجد؟

• Challenger believes that there is evidence that prehistoric creatures may still exist in remote regions. He becomes angry because he thinks scientific evidence should be examined seriously, not laughed at.

2. Explain why Professor Summerlee challenges Challenger during the lecture.

– اشرح لماذا يتحدى البروفيسور سمرلي تشالنجر أثناء المحاضرة.

• Summerlee is doubtful about Challenger's claims and wants convincing scientific proof. He challenges him because he believes that extraordinary claims must be tested carefully.

3. Explain why Professor Summerlee agrees to test Challenger's claims in person. SB

– اشرح لماذا وافق البروفيسور سمرلي على اختبار مزاعم تشالنجر شخصياً.

• Summerlee is sceptical of Challenger's claims and wants to see scientific evidence for himself. He agrees to join the expedition because he believes that personal investigation is the best way to discover the truth.

4. How do Malone and Lord John Roxton become members of the expedition? SB

– كيف أصبح مالون واللورد جون روكستون عضوين في البعثة؟

• Challenger asks for younger companions to accompany Summerlee on the dangerous journey. Malone volunteers publicly, and Lord John Roxton also volunteers because of his experience in the Amazon.

5. How does Malone's decision to volunteer change his future?

– كيف سيؤثر قرار مالون بالتطوع على مستقبله؟

• Malone's decision suddenly makes him a member of an exciting and dangerous expedition to South America. His life changes from that of a journalist in England to that of an explorer and investigator.

6. What qualities of Lord John Roxton make him suitable for the expedition?

– ما هي الصفات التي تجعل اللورد جون روكستون مناسباً للبعثة؟

• Roxton is experienced, brave, calm, and familiar with the Amazon. His knowledge of the region and survival skills make him an important member of the team.

7. Explain why Challenger sends a blank sheet of paper and then appears in Manaos himself. SB

– اشرح لماذا يرسل تشالنجر ورقة بيضاء فارغة ثم يظهر في ماناوس.

• Challenger sends a blank sheet because he does not want his written instructions to reveal the secret location. He then appears in Manaos because he plans to guide the expedition himself using his own knowledge and memory.

8. Analyze the contrast between Challenger and Summerlee. How might their different personalities affect the expedition? SB

– حلل التباين بين تشالنجر وسامرل. كيف يمكن أن تؤثر شخصياتهما المختلفة على الرحلة الاستكشافية؟

• Challenger is confident and bold, while Summerlee is sceptical and careful. Their differences may cause arguments, but they can also help the expedition by combining Challenger's confidence with Summerlee's scientific judgment.

9. How do the four main members of the expedition bring different strengths to the team? SB

– كيف يُضيف الأعضاء الأربعة الرئيسيون في البعثة نقاط قوة مختلفة للفريق؟

• Challenger provides scientific knowledge, while Summerlee offers careful scientific investigation. Roxton brings exploration and survival experience, and Malone contributes youth, courage, and his skills as a journalist.

10. Analyze how Malone's final conversation with Gladys before leaving England shows a change in his motivation. SB

– حلل كيف تُظهر محادثة مالون الأخيرة مع جلاديس قبل مغادرة إنجلترا تغيراً في دوافعه.

• At first, Malone wants to prove himself to Gladys and gain her approval. However, he realizes that true courage should come from his own desire to do something worthwhile, not simply from wanting to impress her.

11. Evaluate Malone's decision to volunteer publicly for the journey. Was it courageous, impulsive, or both? SB

– قيّم قرار مالون بالتطوع علناً في هذه الرحلة. هل كان قراراً شجاعاً، أم متهوراً، أم كليهما؟

• Malone's decision was both courageous and impulsive because he immediately stood up without really thinking about it. However, his willingness to face a dangerous journey shows that he is brave and determined.

12. Evaluate Challenger's decision to hide his plan to join the expedition. Was his secrecy sensible or unnecessary? SB

– قيّم قرار تشالنجر بإخفاء خطته للانضمام إلى البعثة. هل كان تكتيكه منطقياً أم غير ضروري؟

• Challenger's secrecy was sensible because he wanted to prevent reporters and strangers from discovering or following the expedition. However, it also surprised Summerlee and created some tension within the group.

13. Why does Malone respect Professor Summerlee even though Summerlee doubts Challenger?
- لماذا يحترم مالون البروفيسور سمرلي رغم أن سمرلي يشك في تشالنجر؟
• Malone respects Summerlee because he is honest about his doubts but is still willing to investigate the claims. He values the professor's scientific attitude and courage.
14. Analyze the importance of Zambo, Gomez, Manuel, and the Indigenous boatmen to the expedition.
- حل أهمية زامبو، وجوميز، ومانويل، والحارة الأصلين بالنسبة للرحلة الاستكشافية.
• They provide practical help and valuable knowledge of the rivers and local environment. Their experience makes the difficult journey safer and more manageable.
15. How does Challenger's unexpected appearance in Manaos surprise the other members of the expedition?
- كيف فاجأ ظهور تشالنجر غير المتوقع في ماناوس أعضاء البعثة الآخرين؟
• The team expects to find written instructions, but instead they discover that the paper is blank. Challenger then suddenly appears and reveals that he has secretly traveled to Brazil to guide them himself.

◆ JRE — نماذج إجابة | JRE Model Answers (Justify – Reason – Evidence)

Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.

1. Was forming a team with such different personalities a wise decision? SB
هل كان تشكيل فريق بشخصيات مختلفة كهذه قرارًا حكيمًا؟

Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, forming the team was a wise decision because each member had different skills and qualities that could help the expedition.
Reasoning : Explain how their differences could help or harm the expedition:
Answer : Their differences could help because they could solve problems in different ways. However, their strong personalities might also cause arguments and disagreements.
Evidence : Refer to details about at least two team members.
Answer : Lord John Roxton is brave, experienced, and skilled in dealing with dangerous situations, while Professor Summerlee is scientific and sceptical. Malone is young and determined to prove himself. Together, these qualities could make the expedition stronger.

2. Is Summerlee's scepticism valuable to the expedition? SB
هل تشكك سمرلي ذو قيمة للرحلة الاستكشافية؟

Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Summerlee's scepticism is valuable because it encourages the team to look for facts instead of simply believing Challenger's claims.
Reasoning : Explain why questioning Challenger may help or hinder the search for truth.
Answer : Questioning Challenger can help the team discover the truth because they must examine evidence carefully. However, too much doubt could slow the expedition or create conflicts with Challenger.
Evidence : Use evidence from the meeting or the preparations for the journey.
Answer : Summerlee does not easily accept Challenger's claims about the unknown land and prehistoric creatures. He wants convincing evidence before believing them. His questioning makes the expedition more scientific and careful.

3. Is Lord John Roxton a suitable leader for the expedition?
هل اللورد جون روكستون قائد مناسب لهذه الرحلة الاستكشافية؟

Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Lord John Roxton is a very suitable leader because he is experienced, calm, brave, and knowledgeable about the Amazon.
Reasoning : Explain how his experience and personality could help the team.
Answer : His experience could help the team deal with dangerous situations and unfamiliar places. His calm personality could also help the other members remain confident.
Evidence : Refer to his experience in the Amazon and the qualities he looks for in Malone.
Answer : Roxton had traveled through the Amazon before and knew its rivers and local traders. He also asked Malone whether he could shoot, climb, swim, and keep calm under pressure.

4. Was Challenger justified in secretly traveling to Brazil instead of sending written instructions?
هل كان من المصير لتشالنجر السفر سرًّا إلى البرازيل بدلًا من إرسال تعليمات مكتوبة؟

Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Challenger was justified because he believed that traveling with the team would be safer and more reliable than giving them written directions.
Reasoning : Explain the advantages and disadvantages of Challenger's decision.
Answer : His decision could help the team because he knew the route from memory. However, keeping his plan secret surprised the others and caused tension, especially with Summerlee.

Evidence : Refer to the blank sheet of paper and Challenger's explanation when he arrived in Manaos.
Answer : When the envelope was opened, it contained only a blank sheet of paper. Challenger then appeared and explained that no written directions could be as reliable as his own memory.

5. Does Challenger's decision to keep the destination secret show wisdom or selfishness?
هل يُظهر قرار تشالنجر بإبقاء الوجهة سرية حكمة أم أنانية؟

Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : It mainly shows wisdom because Challenger wanted to protect the expedition from unnecessary attention.
Reasoning : Explain why keeping the information secret could protect or harm the expedition.
Answer : Keeping the destination secret could prevent reporters and curious travelers from following them. However, it also made the team uncertain and annoyed Summerlee.
Evidence : Refer to Challenger's concern about reporters, curious travelers, and written directions.
Answer : Challenger explained that he did not want reporters or curious travelers to follow them. He also believed that keeping the information private would make the expedition more secure.

6. Is Malone motivated by true courage when he decides to join the expedition?
هل كان مالون مدفوعًا بالشجاعة الحقيقية عندما قرر الانضمام إلى البعثة؟

Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Malone shows true courage because he accepts the danger even though he knows he may face serious difficulties.
Reasoning : Explain the difference between doing something brave for yourself and doing it only to impress someone else.
Answer : True courage means facing a challenge because it is the right thing to do, not simply to gain someone's approval. Malone begins to understand this difference.
Evidence : Refer to Malone's conversation with Gladys and his thoughts about courage.
Answer : Malone tells Gladys that there may be real danger. He remembers that a brave act done only to win her approval would not be true courage, so he decides to prove himself through his actions.
