◆ الفصل في نقاط | Chapter in Points
1. Lord John hires the steam launch Esmeralda to travel up the Amazon, while Challenger refuses to reveal the exact route to protect the site.
— يستأجر اللورد جون الزورق البخاري «إيميرالدا» للسفر عبر نهرالأمازون، بينما يرفض تشالنجرالكشف عن المسارالدقيق لحماية الموقع.
2. At a small village, the launch is sent back and the group continues by canoe up a narrowing tributary, guided by two local men who had been on Challenger's earlier trip.
— عند قرية صغيرة، أُعيد الزورق، وواصلت المجموعة رحلتها بالقوارب الصغيرة عبر رافد ضيق، بمساعدة رجلين محليين كانا قد رافقا تشالنجرفي رحلته السابقة.
3. When the water becomes too shallow, they hide the canoes and hike inland through forest, palms, bamboo, and tree-ferns, while Challenger and Summerlee argue over navigation.
— عندما أصبحت المياه ضحلة جدًا، أخفوا القوارب وساروا ريًا عبر الغابة والنخيل والخيزران والسراخس الشجرية، بينما استمر الجدال بين تشالنجروسمرلي حول الاتجاه.
4. On the ninth day, they spot a large flying creature that Challenger identifies as a pterodactyl, though Summerlee dismisses it as a large bird.
— في اليوم التاسع، شاهدوا مخلوقًا طائرًا ضخمًا اعتمره تشالنجرحيوان طائر مجنحًا (تيرووداكتيل)، بينما اعتمره سمرلي مجرد طائر كبير.
5. They reach the massive red cliffs matching Maple White's drawings, along with a separate rock tower topped by a tree; Summerlee admits the cliffs are real but nothing more.
— وصلوا إلى الجروف الحمراء الضخمة المطابقة لرسومات مايبل وايت، إلى جانب برج صخري منفصل تعلوه شجرة؛ وأقرّ سمرلي بوجود الجروف فقط لكن دون أن دليل إضافي.
6. After failing to find a path up the sheer cliff, they climb the rock tower and cut down its tree so it falls across the chasm, forming a bridge to the plateau.
— بعد فشلهم في إيجاد طريق للصعود عبر الجرف شديد الانحدار، تسلقوا البرج الصخري وقطعوا الشجرة التي سقطت عبر الهوة لتشكل جسرًا نحو الهضبة.
7. Challenger crosses first in triumph, followed by Summerlee, the narrator, and Lord John, until all four finally stand on the plateau together.
— عبر تشالنجرأولًا بفخر، تلاه سمرلي، ثم الراوي، وأخيرًا اللورد جون، حتى وقف الأربعة جميعًا على الهضبة.
8. Shortly after entering the bushes, they hear cracking and rush back to find the bridge destroyed; Gomez appears on the far tower, revealing he pushed it deliberately to avenge his brother, Pedro Lopez, whom Lord John had killed.
— بعد دخولهم الأدغال بوقت قصير، سمعوا صوت تكسر وعادوا مسرعين ليجدوا الجسر قد دُمر؛ وظهر جوميز على البرج المقابل كاشفًا أنه دفع الشجرة عمدا انتقامًا لأخيه بيدرو لويز على يد اللورد جون.
9. Lord John fires at Gomez, who vanishes amid shouting and silence.
— أطلق اللورد جون النار على جوميز الذي اختفى وسط صراخ ثم صمت.
10. Zambo, still loyal, climbs the opposite tower and helps pass food and ammunition across via rope, promising to stay and assist.
— تسلق زامبو، الذي بقي وفيا، البرج المقابل وساعد في تمرير الطعام والذخيرة عبر حبل، واعدًا بالبقاء والمساعدة.
11. As night falls, the four explorers camp at the cliff's edge, now trapped on the plateau with the unexplored lost world stretching out behind them.
— مع حلول الليل، خيم المستكشفون الأربعة عند حافة الجرف، محاصرين الآن على الهضبة، والعالم المفقود غير المستكشف يمتد خلفهم.

◆ نص الفصل | Chapter Text
Lord John had hired a steam launch called the Esmeralda to carry us up the Amazon. The river was so wide that, even far inland, the opposite bank often looked like a distant shadow. Day after day we moved through heat, rain, brilliant sunlight, and walls of green forest.
Challenger refused to reveal our exact route. He made all of us promise that, even if we returned safely, we would not publish enough information for strangers to locate the plateau. He said careless visitors could destroy a place that science had barely discovered.
After several days we left the main river and entered a large tributary. It gradually became narrower. At a small riverside village, Challenger ordered the Esmeralda back to Manaos.
"From here we use canoes," he said. "Soon there will be rapids. A steam launch cannot continue."
We hired two local guides who had accompanied Challenger during his earlier journey. They were clearly frightened by the idea of returning, but they knew the route.
For several days our canoes moved through a beautiful green waterway. Monkeys watched us from branches. Bright birds stood on fallen logs. Once we saw a puma disappear into the forest. The animals seemed less afraid of humans the farther we traveled.
Then the water became too shallow. We hid the canoes and continued on foot, carrying food, rifles, blankets, instruments, and medical supplies.
The land rose steadily. Thick tropical forest gave way to palms, rocky slopes, orchids, bamboo, and wide areas of tree-ferns. Progress was exhausting. Sometimes we spent an entire day cutting a path through bamboo so dense that we could see only a few meters ahead.

Challenger and Summerlee argued constantly.
"We should follow the compass," Challenger insisted one morning.
"We should follow the men who have actually walked through this country," Summerlee replied.
Lord John usually prevented the arguments from becoming serious. "Save your energy, gentlemen. You may need it for something with bigger teeth."
On the ninth day after leaving the canoes, Challenger suddenly stopped and pointed. A huge grey shape rose from the plain ahead. It flapped slowly, flew low across the tree-ferns, and disappeared behind the forest.
"A pterodactyl!" Challenger cried.
Summerlee gave a short laugh. "A large bird."
Lord John had been watching through field glasses. "I cannot tell you what it was," he said, "but I can tell you I have never seen a bird shaped like that."
The next day we crossed a ridge and saw the cliffs.
They were exactly as Maple White had drawn them: a vast red wall rising high above the plain, with thick vegetation along the top. Near one section stood a separate rocky tower with a tree growing on its summit.
Even Summerlee became quiet. "Well?" Challenger asked proudly.
"The cliffs exist," Summerlee said. "That proves the cliffs exist. Nothing more."
We spent several days searching for a way up. The main wall was almost vertical.
At one point we found the entrance of an old cave, but the passage was blocked. We climbed surrounding hills and examined cracks and ledges. Nothing reached the plateau.
Then Challenger noticed that the separate rock tower stood close to the main cliff. If we could climb the tower and somehow cross the gap, we might reach the top.
The climb was difficult but possible. We carried a rope up and reached the tree that grew on the tower. From there we could see the green edge of the plateau only a short distance away—but between us and it was a deep chasm.
Lord John looked at the tree. "If we cut it down in the right direction, it could fall across the gap."
For hours we worked with axes. At last the tree began to move. It fell slowly, then crashed across the chasm with its upper branches landing on the plateau.
We had a bridge.

Challenger insisted on going first. He sat astride the trunk and moved carefully across.
When he reached the far side, he climbed onto the plateau and raised both arms.
"At last!" he shouted.
Summerlee crossed next, carrying two rifles. I followed. I tried not to look down at the enormous drop beneath the tree. Lord John came last, walking with a balance and calm that I could hardly believe.
For the first time, all four of us stood inside Maple White Land.
The air was warm and still. Thick bushes grew near the cliff, while taller trees formed a dark forest beyond. Strange calls came from somewhere in the distance.
Challenger's face shone with triumph.
"Professor Summerlee," he said, "we can now begin the investigation you demanded." Summerlee adjusted his glasses. "We can begin it. I have not yet said how it will end."
We moved about fifty meters into the bushes.
Then we heard a terrible cracking sound behind us. We ran back. The tree bridge was gone. Far below, its broken trunk lay at the foot of the cliff.
At first we thought it had slipped. Then a face appeared on the opposite rock tower. It was Gomez.
His calm expression had disappeared. He looked across the gap at Lord John with open hatred.
"You are trapped now!" he shouted. Lord John stepped forward. "Why?"
Gomez laughed. "Think of Pedro Lopez. He was my brother. You killed him. I have waited years for this."

So the past had followed us into the unknown.
Gomez had pushed the tree from the tower after we crossed. The four of us were now on a plateau surrounded by cliffs, with no known road down.
"Your bones will stay up there," Gomez called.
A moment later Lord John raised his rifle. Gomez disappeared behind the rock. We heard shouting and then silence.
Below us, however, Zambo had remained loyal. He climbed the opposite tower as soon as Gomez and the others were gone.
"Tell me what to do!" he shouted.
We used a thin rope to pull food and ammunition across the gap. Zambo promised to stay below and help us in any way he could.
As night came, we camped near the cliff with only a small lantern. Behind us stretched the unexplored forest of the plateau.
We had reached the lost world. And now we could not leave it.

◆ الأسئلة والأجوبة | Questions and Answers
1. Explain why the explorers leave the Esmeralda and continue by canoe and later on foot.
[SB]
– اشرح لماذا غادر المستكشفون سفينة إزميرالدا واستمروا في رحلتهم بالزورق ثم سيرًا على الأقدام.
• The steam launch can only travel on the wide main river. As the tributary narrows, they switch to canoes; when the water becomes too shallow even for canoes, they hide them and continue on foot with their supplies.
2. Why does Challenger refuse to reveal the exact route to the plateau, even to his own companions and future readers?
– لماذا يرفض تشالنجر الكشف عن المسار الدقيق إلى الهضبة، حتى لرفاقه وقراء المستقبل؟
• He fears that if the location becomes known, careless visitors could flood the site and destroy a place that science had barely begun to discover.
3. Describe the disagreement between Challenger and Summerlee during the overland trek, and how Lord John responds to it.
– صف الخلاف بين تشالنجر وسامرلي خلال الرحلة البرية، وكيف رد اللورد جون على ذلك.
• Challenger wants to navigate by compass, while Summerlee trusts the experienced local guides. Lord John relieves the tension with humor, telling them to save their energy for something "with bigger teeth."

4. What evidence first convinces the group that Maple White's account might be true, and how does Summerlee react to it?
– ما هي الأدلة التي أقنعت المجموعة أولًا بأن رواية مابل وايت قد تكون صحيحة، وكيف كان رد فعل سامرلي على ذلك؟
• They find the massive red cliffs and rock tower exactly as Maple White had drawn them. Summerlee admits only that the cliffs exist, insisting this proves nothing more.
5. Explain how the explorers use the tree on the rock tower to reach the plateau. [SB]
– اشرح كيف استخدم المستكشفون الشجرة الموجودة على البرج الصخري للوصول إلى الهضبة.
• Unable to find a path up the cliff, they climb a nearby rock tower with a tree on top. They cut the tree down so it falls across the chasm, creating a bridge to the plateau, which they cross one by one.
6. Why does Gomez destroy the tree bridge after the explorers cross it? [SB]
– لماذا قام جوميز بتدمير جسر الأشجار بعد أن عبره المستكشفون؟
• He seeks revenge. Lord John had killed Gomez's brother, Pedro Lopez, years earlier. Gomez pushes the tree down to trap the explorers on the plateau with no way back.
7. How does the behavior of the wildlife change as the group travels farther into the region?
– كيف يتغير سلوك الحيوانات البرية كلما توغلت المجموعة في المنطقة؟
• The animals—monkeys, birds, a puma—appear steadily less afraid of humans the deeper the explorers travel, showing that they are entering territory rarely or never visited by people.
8. What does Summerlee's comment "We can begin it. I have not yet said how it will end" reveal about his character?
– ماذا يكشف تعليق سامرلي "يمكننا أن نبدأ. لم أقل بعد كيف ستنتهي" عن شخصيته؟
• It shows his persistent scientific caution and scepticism—even after reaching the plateau, he refuses to assume the investigation will confirm Challenger's claims.
9. Why does Challenger insist on crossing the tree bridge first?
– لماذا يصر تشالنجر على عبور جسر الأشجار أولًا؟
• Likely driven by pride and eagerness to claim the achievement, Challenger insists on going first, and once across he raises both arms in triumph, shouting "At last!"—emphasizing this moment as the fulfillment of his earlier claims being proved.
10. How are Zambo and Gomez presented as contrasting examples of loyalty and betrayal? [SB]
– كيف يتم تقديم زامبو وجوميز كمثالين مت_tailقضين للولاء والخيانة؟
• Gomez betrays the group, secretly destroying their only way down out of hatred. Zambo, by contrast, stays loyal, climbing the tower to check on them and helping pass food and ammunition across by rope.

11. Analyze how the sighting of the strange flying creature develops the conflict between evidence and scepticism. SB
– حل كيف يؤدي رصد المخلوق الطائر الغريب إلى تطور الصراع بين الأدلة والشك.
• Challenger confidently calls it a pterodactyl, proof of prehistoric life; Summerlee dismisses it as just a bird. Lord John takes a middle position, unsure but struck by its strangeness. This exchange highlights the story's tension: physical evidence exists, but its meaning stays disputed until proven.
12. Evaluate the explorers' decision to cross the tree bridge. Was the scientific goal worth the physical risk? Give reasons. SB
– قيّم قرار المستكشفين بعبور جسر الأشجار. هل كان الهدف العلمي يستحق المخاطرة الجسدية؟ اذكر الأسباب.
• On the one hand, the discovery was scientifically huge, and they'd come too far to turn back. However, crossing left them with only one fragile escape route, and they failed to consider being tricked by an enemy like Gomez, turning a risk into a trap.
13. Explain the sequence of events that leads to the explorers being trapped on the plateau.
– اشرح تسلسل الأحداث التي أدت إلى وقوع المستكشفين في مأزق على الهضبة.
• After crossing the tree bridge onto the plateau, they walk a short distance into the bushes. They hear cracking, rush back, and find the bridge destroyed—Gomez had pushed it down out of revenge, leaving them stranded.
14. Analyze how Lord John's character is revealed through his actions during the climb and the confrontation with Gomez.
– حل كيف تتجلى شخصية اللورد جون من خلال أفعاله أثناء التساق والمواجهة مع جوميز.
• He shows calmness and courage, crossing the log bridge steadily last, and certain action when threatened, immediately firing at Gomez. His character combines coolness under pressure with readiness for danger.
15. Evaluate Challenger's decision to keep the expedition's location secret. Was this justified, or did it create unnecessary danger for the group?
– قيّم قرار تشالنجر بإبقاء موقع البعثة سراً. هل كان هذا القرارمبرراً. أم أنه خلق خطأً غير ضروري على المجموعة؟
• For: Protecting a new scientific place from people who might harm or use it is a sensible decision. It is part of a scientist's responsibility to protect important discoveries.
• Against: Keeping the place secret was also dangerous. If something went wrong, nobody outside the group knew where they were, so they could not get help or be rescued.

◆ JRE — نماذج إجابة | JRE Model Answers (Justify – Reason – Evidence)
Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.
1. Does Zambo deserve to be considered one of the heroes of the expedition? SB
هل يستحق زامبون أن يُعتبر أحد أبطال الرحلة الاستكشفية؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Zambo deserves to be considered one of the heroes because he is strong, loyal, and dependable, especially when the expedition faces danger.
Reasoning : Explain what makes his actions important after the betrayal.
Answer : Zambo's loyalty becomes especially important after the betrayal because the explorers need someone they can trust. His strength and practical skills help protect the members of the expedition and give them hope in a difficult situation.
Evidence : Support your answer with details from the chapter.
Answer : Zambo is described as a huge, cheerful, strong, loyal, and practical man. He is also chosen carefully by Lord John to help the expedition. His loyalty contrasts with Gomez's betrayal and shows why he is an important member of the team.
2. Do Lord John's past actions help explain Gomez's betrayal? SB
هل تساعد أفعال اللورد جون السابقة في تفسير خيانة جوميز؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Lord John's past actions help explain Gomez's betrayal because his conflict with Pedro Lopez created enemies who might want revenge against him.
Reasoning : Explain how Lord John's actions against Pedro Lopez's group may have influenced Gomez.
Answer : Lord John helped workers escape from Pedro Lopez's control and ended Lopez's influence in the area. As a result, people connected with Lopez's group may have regarded Lord John as an enemy and wanted to take revenge.
Evidence : Use evidence about Pedro Lopez, Gomez, and the trapped explorers.
Answer : Lord John explains that he had a serious dispute with Pedro Lopez and that his actions made him "some loyal friends, and some dangerous enemies." Gomez later betrays the explorers and helps put them in danger. This suggests that the conflict with Lopez's group may be connected to Gomez's actions.

3. Is Summerlee's attitude still useful after they reach the plateau?
هل لا يزال موقف سمرلي مفيداً بعد وصولهم إلى مرحلة الاستقرار؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Summerlee's careful attitude is still useful.
Reasoning : Explain why the team needs someone who asks questions.
Answer : The team needs to examine what they see carefully instead of believing every claim immediately.
Evidence : Use details from the chapter.
Answer : When Challenger celebrates reaching the plateau, Summerlee says that they can begin the investigation, but he has not yet said how it will end.
4. Did seeing the cliffs prove that Challenger was telling the truth?
هل أثبتت رؤية المنحدرات أن تشالنجركان يقول الحقيقة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : It proved that the cliffs were real, but it did not prove all of Challenger's claims.
Reasoning : Explain the difference between proving one fact and proving everything.
Answer : Finding the cliffs confirmed one part of the story. It did not prove that prehistoric animals lived there.
Evidence : Use details from the chapter.
Answer : The cliffs were exactly like Maple White's drawing, but Summerlee said that this only proved that the cliffs existed.
5. Does Lord John show courage and self-control?
هل أظهر اللورد جون شجاعة وضبطاً للنفس؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Lord John shows great courage and self-control.
Reasoning : Explain how his behaviour shows these qualities.
Answer : He stays calm in dangerous situations and helps the team instead of becoming frightened.
Evidence : Use details from the chapter.
Answer : He crossed the tree last and walked with great balance and calm. Later, when Gomez threatened them, Lord John calmly raised his rifle.

◆ الفصل في نقاط | Chapter in Points
1. The group find water and build a rough shelter, humorously named "Fort Challenger" by Lord John, in a clearing surrounded by thorny branches.
— عُثِرت المجموعة على الماء وبنت مأوى بسيطًا، أطلق عليه اللورد جون اسم «حصن تشالنجر» بشُكل ساخر، في منطقة مفتوحة محاطة بأغصان شائكة.
2. They observe a herd of iguanodons feeding peacefully, finally confirming Challenger's claims and even convincing Summerlee, though Challenger remains smugly unsurprised.
— شاهدوا قطيعًا من الإجوانودونات يتغذى بسلام، مما أكد أخيرًا صحة ادعاءات تشالنجر وأقنع حتى سمرلي، رغم أن تشالنجر بقي متعجرفًا وغير مفتاجٍ.

3. On a return visit, they find one of the iguanodons torn apart, revealing that a large carnivorous dinosaur, possibly an allosaurus, lives nearby.
— في زيارة لاحقة، وجدوا أحد الإجوانودونات ممزقًا، مما كشف عن وجود ديناصور مفترس كبير، ربما من نوع الألوصور، يعيش بالقرب منهم.
4. The group discovers a swamp filled with hundreds of pterodactyls, as well as their eggs and young. While the scientists are thrilled, the creatures suddenly attack, wounding the group before they escape into the trees.
— اكتشفت المجموعة مستنقعًا مليئًا بمئات من التيريوداكتيل، بما في ذلك البيض والصغار؛ وبينما كان العلماء متحمسين، هاجمتهم المخلوقات فجأة عند اكتشافهم، فأصابتهم بجروح قبل أن يهربوا إلى الأشجار.
5. Despite the attack, Summerlee remains fascinated, calling the pterodactyls "remarkable," to Lord John's irritation.
— رغم الهجوم، بقي سمرلي مفتونًا، واصفًا التيريوداكتيل بأنها «مذهلة»، مما أثار استياء اللورد جون.
6. They return to camp to find it ransacked by an unknown, strong, and clever creature that entered without breaking the thorn wall, likely by climbing a nearby branch.
— عادوا إلى المعسكر ليجدوه منهوبًا من قِبل مخلوق مجهول قوي وذكي دخل دون كسر الجدار الشائك، على الأرجح عبر تسلق غصن قريب.
7. They begin keeping fires burning at night and taking turns on watch for safety.
— بدأوا بإبقاء النيران مشتعلة ليلًا وتناوب الحراسة للحماية.
8. Over the following days, the group continues encountering strange animals and unidentified tracks, while Challenger and Summerlee's arguments shift from doubting the plateau's existence to debating classification.
— في الأيام التالية، واصلت المجموعة مواجهة حيوانات غريبة وآثارًا قدمًا مجهولة، بينما تحولت جدالات تشالنجر وسمرلي من الشك في وجود الهضبة إلى الجدال حول تصنيف ما داخلها.
9. Lord John concentrates on ensuring the group's survival, while Malone tries to balance observation with survival.
— يركز اللورد جون على ضمان بقاء المجموعة، بينما يحاول مالون الموازنة بين الملاحظة والبقاء على قيد الحياة.
10. They maintain daily contact with the loyal Zambo below the cliff, hoping for rescue, while some guides head back toward the river carrying letters requesting rope.
— حافظوا على تواصل يومي مع زامبو الوفي أسفل الجرف، على أمل الإنقاذ، بينما توجه بعض المرشدين نحو النهر حاملين رسائل تطلب حبلًا.
11. Signs suggest the group is being watched—vanishing food, a strange clicking call, and a broad, heavy, human-like footprint—though Summerlee cautions against forming theories without evidence.
— ظهرت علامات على أن المجموعة مراقبة — اختفاء الطعام، وصوت نقر غريب، وأثر قدم عريض وثقيل يشبه أثر الإنسان — رغم أن سمرلي حذر من بناء نظريات دون أدلة.
12. Climbing a tree for a better view, Malone spots a large lake and distant cliffs with cave openings, then notices smoke rising near the caves, suggesting the plateau is inhabited by someone—or something.
— عند تسلقه شجرة للحصول على رؤية أفضل، رصد مالون بحيرة كبيرة وجروفًا بعيدة بها فتحات كهوف، ثم لاحظ دخانًا يتصاعد بالقرب من الكهوف، مما يشير إلى أن الهضبة مأهولة بشخص ما — أو شيء ما.

◆ نص الفصل | Chapter Text
Our first task was simple: find water and a safe place to camp. We followed a small stream into the forest and built a rough shelter in an open area surrounded by thorny branches. Lord John called it "Fort Challenger". Professor Challenger pretended to dislike the joke, but I noticed that he did not suggest a different name.
The plateau was unlike any place I had ever seen. Some plants looked familiar, while others seemed larger or older in form. Insects of brilliant colors moved through the air. Every distant sound made us stop and listen.
We did not have to wait long for proof that Challenger had been right.
One morning we entered a broad glade and saw several enormous animals feeding among the trees. They stood on powerful back legs, using shorter front legs to pull down branches.
Their heads were small compared with their heavy bodies and long tails.
"Iguanodons," Challenger whispered.
Even Summerlee forgot to argue. His face changed from doubt to amazement.
There were adults and young ones. For several minutes we watched them in silence. "You were right," Summerlee said at last.
Challenger looked almost disappointed. "Of course I was right."
Their peace did not last. The next time we returned, one of the great plant-eaters had been torn apart. Deep claw and tooth marks showed that a far more dangerous animal lived nearby.
"A large carnivorous dinosaur," Summerlee said. "Possibly an allosaurus," Challenger replied.
Lord John checked his rifle. "Call it what you like. I prefer not to meet it at close range." Another discovery was even more disturbing.

We heard a constant harsh crying from a rocky hollow and crawled forward to investigate.
Below us lay a swampy basin filled with pterodactyls. Hundreds of them crowded around pools of green water. Young animals moved between leathery eggs, while large adults rested on rocks with folded wings.
For Challenger and Summerlee, it was a scientific treasure. They whispered excitedly about wings, teeth, nesting behavior, and species. Lord John and I were more interested in the animals' sharp beaks and watchful eyes.
We tried to withdraw quietly, but one creature saw us. Its cry was answered by others. In seconds the sky seemed full of wings.
"Run!" Lord John shouted.

We raced toward the trees. Beaks snapped near our heads. Wings struck our shoulders.
One pterodactyl stabbed me in the neck with its beak. Lord John's coat was torn.
Challenger was knocked down. At last Lord John fired his rifle and the attack weakened enough for us to escape beneath thicker branches.
Summerlee, bleeding from a cut on his forehead, looked back toward the swamp.
"Remarkable," he said.
"Remarkable?" Lord John replied. "They nearly killed us." "That does not make them less remarkable."
The scientists collected every fact they could, even when the facts had teeth.
When we returned to Fort Challenger, we found another problem. Something had entered the camp while we were away. Food tins had been crushed. A wooden ammunition box had been broken. Supplies were scattered across the ground.
There were no clear footprints. "Whatever did this was strong," I said.
"And clever enough to enter without breaking the thorn wall," Lord John added.
A large branch hung over one side of the camp. Something could have climbed through the tree.

That night we kept two fires burning and took turns on watch. In the following days we explored carefully. We saw more iguanodons, giant insects, strange birds, and tracks we could not identify. Once, at dusk, a huge shadow moved between the trees. It was too large to be any modern animal I knew.
Despite the danger, Challenger was happier than I had ever seen him. The plateau was proving his story in every direction. Summerlee, now convinced that prehistoric life had survived, became just as energetic. Their arguments continued, but the subject had changed. They no longer argued about whether the lost world existed; they argued about how to classify everything inside it.
Lord John focused on survival. He chose safe paths, watched the wind, studied animal tracks, and never allowed us to walk carelessly into thick cover.
I tried to do both jobs: observe and survive. I was a reporter, and I knew that if we ever returned to London, people would want a clear record of what we had seen.
But returning was still the great question.
Every day we went to the cliff and called to Zambo. He remained at his little camp below, faithful to his promise. Some of the Indigenous guides had gone back toward the river, carrying letters from me and requests for strong rope. We could only hope help would eventually arrive.
Meanwhile, the plateau held another mystery.
More than once we had the feeling that we were being watched.
A shadow moved high in a tree and disappeared. We heard a clicking call unlike any bird.
Food vanished from places we thought were secure. Once Lord John found a footprint that looked disturbingly human, but broader and heavier.
"There may be people here," I said.
"Or something close to people," Challenger replied.
Summerlee frowned. "Let us not create a theory before we have evidence." It was good advice. Yet the forest seemed to be giving us a warning.
One afternoon I climbed a high tree at the edge of a clearing. From the upper branches I hoped to see more of the plateau and perhaps discover a possible escape route.
The view was astonishing.
The plateau was far larger than I had imagined. Forest covered most of it, but in the center lay a broad lake shining in the sun. On the far side I could make out a line of cliffs with dark openings that might be caves.
Then I noticed something else.
A thin line of smoke rose near those caves. Smoke meant fire.
And fire usually meant people.
I climbed down with my mind racing.
The lost world was not only a land of prehistoric animals. Someone—or something—was living there too.

◆ الأسئلة والأجوبة | Questions and Answers

1. Explain how the iguanodons provide strong evidence that Challenger's story is true.
[SB]
- اشرح كيف تُقدّم حيوانات الإيجوانودون دليلاً قوياً على صحة قصة تشالنجر.
• The explorers see enormous iguanodons with their own eyes. This proves that prehistoric animals really survived on the plateau and confirms Challenger's claims.
2. What happens at the pterodactyl swamp, and why are the explorers forced to escape quickly?
[SB]
- ماذا يحدث في مستنقع التيروداكتيل، ولماذا يضطر المستكشفون إلى الهروب بسرعة؟
• The explorers discover hundreds of pterodactyls around a swamp. When one sees them, the creatures attack in large numbers, forcing the explorers to run quickly toward the trees.
3. Explain what the damaged supplies at Fort Challenger suggest about another danger on the plateau.
[SB]
- اشرح ما تشير إليه الإمدادات المتضررة في حصن تشالنجر بشأن خطر آخر على الهضبة.
• The damaged supplies suggest that a strong and intelligent creature entered their camp while they were away. It may be able to move through the trees without being noticed.
4. Analyze how Summerlee's reaction to the prehistoric animals shows a change in his role as the expedition's sceptic.
[SB]
- حلل كيف يُظهر رد فعل سامرلي تجاه الحيوانات التي عصورا ما قبل التاريخ تغيّراً في دوره كمتشكك في البعثة.
• At first, Summerlee doubts Challenger's claims. After seeing the iguanodons, however, he admits that Challenger was right. He changes from a sceptic into an enthusiastic scientist who wants to study the prehistoric animals.
5. How does the chapter present the lost world as both wonderful and dangerous?
[SB]
- كيف يقدّم هذا الفصل العالم المفقود على أنه رائع وخطر في آن واحد؟
• The lost world is wonderful because it contains rare plants, giant insects, dinosaurs, and pterodactyls. However, it is also dangerous because the animals can attack the explorers, their camp is damaged, and mysterious creatures may be watching them.
6. Analyze the contrast between the scientists' excitement and Lord John's practical response during the pterodactyl encounter.
[SB]
- حلل التناقض بين حماس العلماء ورد فعل اللورد جون العملي أثناء مواجهة التيروداكتيل.
• Challenger and Summerlee are excited about the scientific importance of the pterodactyls. Lord John, however, focuses on survival. He notices their danger and uses his rifle when they attack, showing his practical and protective nature.

7. Evaluate the explorers' decision to continue studying the plateau while they are trapped instead of focusing only on escape.
[SB]
- قم بتقييم قرار المستكشفين بمواصلة دراسة الهضبة أثناء اعتقالهم بدلاً من التركيز فقط على الهروب.
• Their decision is useful because they can learn more about the lost world and record important discoveries. However, they must balance scientific curiosity with safety because the plateau contains many dangerous creatures.
8. Evaluate their decision to approach the pterodactyl rookery. Were they careful enough for the level of danger? Explain.
[SB]
- قيّم قرارهم بالاقتراب من مستعمرة التيروداكتيل. هل كانوا حذرين بما يكفي بالنظر إلى مستوى الخطر؟ اشرح.
• They were not careful enough. They approached a place containing hundreds of large, dangerous creatures without realizing how easily they could be attacked. Their curiosity was understandable, but they should have kept a safer distance and planned an escape route.
9. Evaluate Challenger's reaction after Summerlee admits that he was right.
[SB]
- قم بتقييم رد فعل تشالنجر بعد أن اعترف سامرلي بأنه كان على حق.
• Challenger's reaction shows his confidence and pride. Instead of being surprised, he simply says that he knew he was right. This shows how strongly he believes in his own knowledge and discoveries.
10. Explain why Summerlee's acceptance of Challenger's theory is important to the expedition.
[SB]
- اشرح لماذا يعد قبول سامرلي لنظرية تشالنجر أمراً مهماً للرحلة الاستكشافية.
• Summerlee has been the main sceptic, so his acceptance makes Challenger's theory much more convincing. It also allows the scientists to work together more positively and focus on studying the creatures.
11. Analyze the contrast between the scientists and Lord John during the pterodactyl attack.
[SB]
- حلل التناقض بين العلماء واللورد جون أثناء هجوم التيروداكتيل.
• The scientists focus on the scientific value of the creatures, while Lord John focuses on survival. This contrast shows that scientific curiosity and practical thinking are both important during the expedition.
12. Evaluate the explorers' decision to continue exploring despite the dangers.
[SB]
- قم بتقييم قرار المستكشفين بمواصلة الاستكشاف على الرغم من المخاطر.
• Their decision is understandable because they are making extraordinary discoveries. However, they should remain cautious because the plateau contains dangerous animals and unknown threats.
13. Explain how the damaged supplies increase the mystery of the lost world.
[SB]
- اشرح كيف تزيد الإمدادات التالفة من غموض العالم المفقود.
• The damaged supplies show that something strong entered their camp while they were away. Since there are no clear footprints, the explorers cannot identify the creature, making the danger more mysterious.

14. Analyze how Lord John's actions contribute to the group's survival.
- حلل كيف تساهم تصرفات اللورد جون في بقاء المجموعة.
• Lord John carefully studies tracks, chooses safe paths, watches the surroundings, and keeps his weapon ready. His practical decisions help protect the group from the dangers of the plateau.
15. Evaluate Summerlee's change from a sceptic to an enthusiastic scientist.
- قيّم تحول سمرلي من شخص متشكك إلى عالم متحمس.
• His change is reasonable because he bases his opinion on direct evidence. Instead of refusing to accept new information, he changes his mind when the evidence proves Challenger correct.
16. Explain why the discovery of smoke is more mysterious than the discovery of dinosaurs.
- اشرح لماذا يُعد اكتشاف الدخان أكثر غموضًا من اكتشاف الديناصورات.
• The dinosaurs prove that prehistoric life survived, but smoke suggests that an intelligent being may be living on the plateau. This raises new questions about who is making the fire and why.

◆ JRE — نماذج إجابة | JRE Model Answers (Justify – Reason – Evidence)

Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.
1. By the end of this chapter, has Challenger's scientific claim been fully proved to the other explorers? SB
- بنهاية هذا الفصل، هل تم إثبات الأدعاء العلمي لتشانجر بشكل كامل للمستكشفين الآخرين؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes. Challenger's scientific claim has been strongly proved to the other explorers, especially to Summerlee.
Reasoning : Explain why the discoveries are or are not enough.
Answer : The explorers see living prehistoric animals with their own eyes. This direct evidence is much stronger than Challenger's original story, so Summerlee can no longer deny that prehistoric life has survived on the plateau.
Evidence : Use at least two discoveries from the chapter.
Answer : First, they discover enormous iguanodons, which makes Summerlee admit that Challenger was right. Second, they find hundreds of pterodactyls, providing further evidence that prehistoric creatures are living on the plateau.

2. Is scientific curiosity shown as a strength or a danger in this chapter? SB
هل يُصوَّر الفضول العلمي في هذا الفصل على أنه قوة أم خطر؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Scientific curiosity is shown as both a strength and a danger, but it becomes dangerous when the explorers take unnecessary risks.
Reasoning : Explain how curiosity affects the explorers' choices.
Answer : Their curiosity helps them make important discoveries and understand the lost world. However, it also encourages them to approach dangerous animals and investigate places that may not be safe.
Evidence : Support your answer with details from the iguanodons, pterodactyls, or the camp.
Answer : Their curiosity leads them to observe the iguanodons, giving them important scientific evidence. However, they approach the pterodactyls and are attacked. They also investigate the strange damage at Fort Challenger, showing that their desire to understand the unknown sometimes puts them in danger.
3. Does Summerlee's change of opinion show that he is a good scientist?
هل يُظهر تغيير رأي سمرلي أنه عالم جيد؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Summerlee's change of opinion shows that he is a good scientist.
Reasoning : Explain how Summerlee responds to new evidence.
Answer : A good scientist should accept reliable evidence even when it proves that his earlier opinion was wrong.
Evidence : Use at least two details from the chapter.
Answer : Summerlee initially doubts Challenger, but after seeing the iguanodons, he admits that Challenger was right. Later, he becomes enthusiastic about studying the prehistoric animals.
4. Was the explorers' decision to study the pterodactyls reasonable despite the danger?
هل كان قرار المستكشفين بدراسة التيرووداكتيل معقولًا رغم الخطر؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Their decision was understandable but too risky.
Reasoning : Explain the benefits and risks of their decision.
Answer : Studying the pterodactyls could provide valuable scientific information, but the explorers did not fully consider the danger of approaching hundreds of wild creatures.
Evidence : Use details from the pterodactyl encounter.
Answer : They entered the area to observe the pterodactyls closely, but the animals attacked them. They had to run to the trees, and Lord John had to use his rifle to help them escape.

5. Does the damaged camp suggest that the explorers face a greater danger than they realize?
هل يشير المخيم المتضرر إلى أن المستكشفين يواجهون خطرًا أكبر مما يدركون؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, the damaged camp suggests that the explorers face a greater danger than they realize.
Reasoning : Explain what the damaged supplies reveal about the unknown creature.
Answer : Something strong and possibly intelligent entered the camp while they were away without being clearly detected. This means that the explorers may be watched even when they think they are safe.
Evidence : Use at least two details from the camp.
Answer : Food tins were crushed and an ammunition box was broken. There were no clear footprints, suggesting that the creature may have entered through a tree.
6. Does the discovery of smoke change the meaning of the explorers' expedition?
هل يُغيّر اكتشاف الدخان معنى رحلة المستكشفين الاستكشافية؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, the discovery of smoke changes the meaning of the expedition.
Reasoning : Explain why the smoke is different from the other discoveries.
Answer : Until this point, the explorers mainly believe they are studying prehistoric animals. Smoke suggests that intelligent beings may also live on the plateau.
Evidence : Use details from the final part of the chapter.
Answer : Malone sees smoke rising near the caves, and he realizes that fire usually means people. He concludes that the lost world may contain people or human-like creatures.

◆ الفصل في نقاط | Chapter in Points
1. Malone draws a rough map of the plateau and marks important places such as the forest, the pterodactyl swamp, and the lake.
- يرسم مالون خريطة تقريبية للهضبة ويحدد عليها أماكن مهمة مثل الغابة ومستنقع التيروداكتيل والبحيرة.
2. The explorers allow Malone to name the lake, so he calls it Lake Gladys after the woman who inspired his desire for adventure.
- يسمح المستكشفون لمالون بتسمية البحيرة. فيسميها بحيرة جلاديس نسبةً إلى المرأة التي أوهمته حب المغامرة.
3. Malone becomes restless because he wants to prove that he can contribute more to the expedition.
- يشعرمالون بالقلق لأنه يريد أن يثبت أنه يستطيع تقديم المزيد للبعثة.
4. He decides to explore the far side of the lake alone during the night to look for signs of human life.
- يقرراستكشاف الجانب البعيد من البحيرة بمفرده ليلاً بحثاً عن علامات تدل على وجود حياة بشرية.
5. At the lake, Malone sees a huge stegosaurus drinking from the water.
- عند البحيرة، يرى مالون ديناصوراً ضخماً من نوع ستيجوصورس يشرب من الماء.
6. Malone is amazed because the stegosaurus is living proof of a world that should have disappeared millions of years ago.
- يشعرمالون بالدهشة لأن الديناصوردليل حي على وجود عالم كان من المفترض أن يختفي منذ ملايين السنين.
7. Suddenly, a dangerous dinosaur with powerful legs and huge teeth appears behind Malone.
- فجأة يظهر خلف مالون ديناصور خطير ذو أرجل قوية وأسنان ضخمة.
8. The dinosaur chases Malone, so he runs through the forest and fires his rifle.
- يطارد الديناصورمالون، فيركض عبر الغابة ويطلق النارمن بندقيته.
9. Malone falls into a deep pit, and the dinosaur cannot reach him.
- يسقط مالون في حفرة عميقة، ولا يستطيع الديناصورالوصول إليه.
10. He discovers that the pit was a human-made trap with sharp stakes, proving that intelligent people live on the plateau.
- يكتشف أن الحفرة كانت فخاً صنعه البشرو بها أوتاد حادة، مما يثبت أن أشخاصاً أذكياء يعيشون على الهضبة.
11. Malone climbs out of the pit and returns to Fort Challenger at dawn.
- يخرج مالون من الحفرة ويعود إلى حصن تشالنجرعند الفجر

12. When he returns, he finds the camp silent and discovers that Challenger, Summerlee, and Lord John have disappeared.
- عندما يعود، يجد المعسكر صامتاً ويكتشف أن تشالنجروسمرلي ولورد جون قد اختفوا.
13. Lord John suddenly returns and tells Malone that the three men were captured by ape-men.
- يعود لورد جون فجأة ويخبر مالون أن الرجال الثلاثة تم أسرهم بواسطة رجال القردة.
14. The ape-men are strong and intelligent creatures that walk upright and use clubs, stones, and vines.
- رجال القردة مخلوقات قوية وذكية تمشي مستقيمة الظهروتستخدم العصي والحجارة والكروم.
15. The leader of the ape-men looks very similar to Professor Challenger, so they treat Challenger differently.
- يشبه زعيم رجال القردة البروفيسور تشالنجربدرجة كبيرة، ولذلك يعاملون تشالنجربطريقة مختلفة.
16. Lord John manages to escape and returns to Malone to get weapons and rescue the prisoners.
- يتمكن لورد جون من الهروب ويعود إلى مالون للحصول على الأسلحة وإنقاذ الأسرى .
17. Malone learns that true courage means facing danger to help others, not taking risks just to prove yourself.
- يتعلم مالون أن الشجاعة الحقيقية تعني مواجهة الخطرلمساعدة الآخرين، وليس تعريض نفسك للخطرلمجرد إثبات نفسك .
18. Malone decides to join Lord John and rescue the captured prisoners.
- يقررمالون الانضمام إلى لورد جون وإنقاذ الأسرى .

◆ نص الفصل | Chapter Text

My companions were pleased with what I had seen from the tree. That evening, by the light of our fire, I drew a rough map of the plateau. We marked the cliff where we had entered, the forest, the pterodactyl swamp, and the large lake in the center. "The man who first saw the lake should name it," Lord John said. I thought of Gladys and the reason I had first wanted adventure. "Lake Gladys," I said. Summerlee gave me a dry look, but Challenger nodded. "Lake Gladys it is." I should have been satisfied with the day's work. Instead, I became restless. I wanted to prove that I could contribute more than notes and maps. I also wanted to examine the signs of human life near the far side of the lake.
That night, after the others were asleep, I took a rifle, some food, and a small electric lamp.
I left Fort Challenger alone.
At first the forest was peaceful. Moonlight appeared through gaps in the leaves. I followed the stream, using the map in my head. The farther I traveled, the more foolish my decision seemed, but pride kept me moving.
After several hours I reached the lake.
Its surface reflected the moon. For a few minutes I forgot the danger and simply watched.
Then a heavy shape moved near the water.
An enormous stegosaurus stepped into the open.
I recognized it immediately from Maple White's drawing. Plates rose along its back, and spikes extended from its tail. It lowered its head to drink, completely unaware of me.
I wanted to shout with excitement, but I remained still. This was living proof of a world that should have disappeared millions of years ago.

Then the stegosaurus suddenly lifted its head. It had heard something. So had I.
A deep movement came from the bushes behind me. I turned and saw two shining eyes.
The creature that stepped into the moonlight was built for killing. It stood on powerful back legs with a long tail for balance. Its huge head was filled with teeth. Whether it was an allosaurus or another great hunter, I did not care. It had seen me. I ran.

Branches struck my face as I pushed through the forest. Behind me I heard heavy feet crashing closer. I fired once over my shoulder. The shot did not stop it.
The ground suddenly vanished. I fell several meters into a deep pit and landed painfully on soft earth.
For a moment I could not breathe. I expected the dinosaur to fall after me, but it stopped at the edge. Its head appeared against the sky. It snapped its jaws and roared, unable to reach me. Eventually it moved away.
I examined the pit. Its sides were too straight to be natural. Sharp stakes had once been placed at the bottom, though many were broken or rotten. This was a trap made by intelligent hands.
The smoke I had seen from the tree had not been an accident. People really did live on the plateau.
It took me a long time to climb out using roots and cracks in the wall. By dawn I was exhausted, covered in mud, and far from camp.
When I finally returned to Fort Challenger, something was wrong. The camp was silent.
Challenger, Summerlee, and Lord John had disappeared.

There were signs of struggle. Equipment had been thrown aside. Some food was missing.
On the ground I found broken branches and several broad footprints. I called until my throat hurt. No one answered.
For the first time since we reached the plateau, I felt completely alone.
I spent the day searching nearby paths, but found no clear trail. At sunset I returned to camp and prepared for a lonely night. I had almost fallen asleep when a hand touched my arm.
I reached for my rifle - then stopped. Lord John Roxton knelt beside me. He looked terrible. His clothes were torn, his face scratched, and he was breathing hard.
"Quickly, Malone," he whispered. "Take the rifles, cartridges, and some food. We cannot stay here."
I followed him into thick bushes where he had chosen a hiding place. "Where are the professors?" I asked.
"Captured." "By whom?"
Lord John's face became hard. "Ape-men."
He told me what had happened. Before dawn, while the others were waking, large creatures dropped from the branches above the camp. They were covered with reddish hair, but they walked upright. They carried clubs and stones, communicated with one another, and used strong vines to tie their prisoners.
Lord John had fired at them, but they attacked too quickly. The three men were overwhelmed.
"They are stronger than ordinary men," he said. "And they are clever. Not human as we are, but not simply animals either."
The ape-men had carried Challenger, Summerlee, and Lord John through the forest. Their leader was a powerful older creature.
Then Lord John gave a tired smile.
"You will not believe this part. The chief looks like Challenger. Same broad shoulders, same beard, same angry expression. Even Summerlee laughed when he saw them together."
The strange similarity may have saved Challenger. The ape-men treated him differently, almost as if they considered him important. Summerlee and Lord John were handled far more roughly.
During the confusion, Lord John managed to escape. He had returned to find me and collect weapons.
"Can we rescue them?" I asked.

"We must try. But there is more. The ape-men have other prisoners - humans from another part of the plateau. I saw several of them being dragged towards the ape settlement."
He checked his rifle.
"If we move carefully, we may reach them before it is too late."
I looked at the dark forest. Only a few hours earlier I had been proud of my solo journey. Now it seemed childish. Courage was not wandering into danger to prove something about yourself.
Real courage was going back into danger because other people needed you. "Lead the way," I said.

◆ الأسئلة والأجوبة | Questions and Answers

1. Why did Malone decide to explore the lake alone at night?
- لماذا قرر مالون استكشاف البحيرة بمفرده ليلاً؟
• Because he wanted to prove that he could contribute more to the expedition, and he wanted to look for signs of human life.
2. Why did Malone name the lake "Lake Gladys"?
- لماذا أطلق مالون على البحيرة اسم «بحيرة جلاديس»؟
• Because Gladys was the woman who had inspired his desire for adventure.
3. Why was the pit important to Malone?
- لماذا كانت الحفرة مهمة بالنسبة لمالون؟
• Because he discovered that it was a human-made trap, which proved that intelligent people lived on the plateau.
4. Why did the ape-men treat Challenger differently from the others?
- لماذا عامل رجال القردة تشالنجر بطريقة مختلفة عن الآخرين؟
• Because their leader looked very similar to Challenger, they seemed to consider him important.
5. How would you evaluate Malone's decision to explore the plateau alone?
- كيف تقيّم قرار مالون باستكشاف الهضبة بمفرده؟
• It was brave but also dangerous and somewhat foolish because he put himself in unnecessary danger.
6. What does Malone learn about true courage at the end of the chapter?
- ماذا يتعلم مالون عن الشجاعة الحقيقية في نهاية الفصل؟
• He learns that true courage means facing danger to help others, not taking risks just to prove yourself.

7. Why did Lord John return to Malone after escaping from the ape-men?
– لماذا عاد لورد جون إلى مالون بعد هروبه من رجال القردة؟
• He returned to find Malone, collect weapons and food, and plan a rescue mission.
8. Do you think Malone became more mature by the end of the chapter? Why?
– هل تعتقد أن مالون أصبح أكثر نضجاً في نهاية الفصل؟ ولماذا؟
• Yes. He understood that courage is not about proving yourself but about helping others when they are in danger.
9. Analyze how pride influences Malone's decision to continue traveling alone even when he begins to feel unsafe. [SB]
– حلّل كيف يؤثر غرور مالون في قراره بالاستمرار في السفر بمفرده حتى عندما يبدأ في الشعور بعدم الأمان.
• Malone's pride makes him continue because he wants to prove that he is useful and brave. Although he realizes that his decision is becoming dangerous, his pride prevents him from turning back.
10. Analyze the significance of Lord John's return and the news that Challenger and Summerlee have been captured. [SB]
– حلّل أهمية عودة لورد جون والخبر الذي يفيد بأن تشالنجر وسميرلي قد تم أسرهما.
• Lord John's return changes the situation completely. Malone discovers that the camp is no longer safe and that his companions are in danger. This also gives him a new purpose: helping Lord John rescue the prisoners.
11. Evaluate Malone's decision to explore alone. Was it brave, reckless, or a mixture of both? Support your answer. [SB]
– قيّم قرار مالون بالاستكشاف بمفرده. هل كان شجاعاً أم متهوراً أم مزيجاً من الاثنين؟ ادعم إجابتك.
• It was a mixture of bravery and recklessness. Malone was brave because he wanted to discover the truth and contribute to the expedition. However, he was reckless because he ignored the danger and traveled alone at night.
12. Evaluate Malone's decision to name the lake after Gladys. What does it suggest about his motivation at this stage? [SB]
– قيّم قرار مالون بتسمية البحيرة باسم جلاديس. ماذا يشير ذلك عن دوافعه في هذه المرحلة؟
• Naming the lake "Lake Gladys" shows that Gladys is still an important motivation for Malone. It suggests that his desire for adventure is partly connected to his wish to prove himself worthy and successful.
13. Why did Malone remain still when he saw the stegosaurus near the lake?
– لماذا ظل مالون ساكناً عندما رأى الديناصور ستيجوصورس بالقرب من البحيرة؟
• He remained still because he did not want to frighten the dinosaur or attract its attention. He wanted to observe it safely.

14. What does the stegosaurus represent to Malone?
– ماذا يمثل ديناصور ستيجوصورس بالنسبة لمالون؟
• It represents living proof that an ancient world still exists on the plateau. It also shows the importance of their discovery.
15. What happened after Malone fired his rifle at the attacking dinosaur?
– ماذا حدث بعد أن أطلق مالون بنادقته على الديناصور المهاجم؟
• The shot did not stop the powerful creature, so Malone had to continue running until he accidentally fell into the pit.

◆ JRE — نماذج إجابة | JRE Model Answers (Justify – Reason – Evidence)
Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.
1. How does Malone's experience with the dinosaur affect his understanding of the dangers of exploring alone? كيف تؤثر تجربة مالون مع الديناصور في فهمه لمخاطر الاستكشاف بمفرده؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Malone becomes more aware that exploring alone can put his life in serious danger.
Reasoning : Explain how the experience changes his understanding.
Answer : He realizes that unexpected dangers can appear at any moment, and that courage does not mean ignoring those dangers.
Evidence : Refer to events from the chapter.
Answer : A dangerous dinosaur chases him, and he has to escape by falling into a deep pit.
2. Why is the discovery of the ape-men an important development in the story?
– لماذا يعد اكتشاف رجال القردة تطوراً مهماً في أحداث القصة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : The discovery is important because it introduces a new intelligent threat to the explorers.
Reasoning : Explain why the ape-men are different from the dinosaurs.
Answer : Unlike the dinosaurs, the ape-men can communicate, use tools, and capture and control people.
Evidence : Refer to their actions in the chapter.
Answer : They use clubs, stones, and vines and manage to capture Challenger, Summerlee, and Lord John.

3. Does Lord John's escape make him an important character in this chapter?
– هل يجعل هروب لورد جون شخصية مهمة في هذا الفصل؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Lord John becomes especially important because he brings information about the prisoners and plans their rescue.
Reasoning : Explain his role after escaping.
Answer : Instead of simply saving himself, he returns to Malone and prepares to face the danger again.
Evidence : Refer to his actions.
Answer : He returns to find Malone, collects weapons and food, and asks Malone to help rescue the prisoners.
4. What does Malone's reaction to the empty camp reveal about his feelings toward his companions? ماذا يكشف رد فعل مالون تجاه المعسكر الخالي عن مشاعره تجاه رفاقه؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : It shows that Malone cares deeply about his companions and is frightened by their disappearance.
Reasoning : Explain why his reaction is important.
Answer : He realizes that he is no longer simply an explorer looking for adventure; he now has to worry about the safety of others.
Evidence : Refer to his actions after finding the camp empty.
Answer : He searches nearby paths, calls for his companions, and continues looking for them until Lord John returns.
5. Why does Malone feel completely alone when he returns to the camp?
– لماذا يشعر مالون بالوحدة التامة عندما يعود إلى المعسكر؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Malone feels alone because all his companions have disappeared and there is no one to answer his calls.
Reasoning : Explain what makes the situation frightening.
Answer : The silent camp and signs of struggle make him realize that something serious has happened to the others.
Evidence : Refer to what Malone finds.
Answer : He finds broken equipment, missing food, broken branches, and footprints, but no sign of his companions.

◆ الفصل في نقاط | Chapter in Points
1. Malone and Lord John move carefully through the forest, avoiding open areas and hiding whenever they hear the strange calls of the ape-men.
- تحرك مالون ولورد جون بحذر عبر الغابة، وتختبئا الأماكن المفتوحة واختباء كلما سمعا الأصوات الغريبة لرجال القردة.
2. They discover a large clearing where the ape-men have built huts high in the trees and are guarding several prisoners.
- اكتشفا منطقة واسعة بنى فيها رجال القردة أكواخًا عالية فوق الأشجار وكانوا يحرسون عددًا من الأسرى.
3. Malone recognizes Professor Summerlee and Challenger among the prisoners, as well as several human prisoners from the Accala group.
- تعرف مالون على البروفيسور سامرلن وتشالنجر بين الأسرى، بالإضافة إلى عدد من الأسرى البشر من قبيلة الأكالا.
4. The ape-men perform a cruel ceremony in which human prisoners are forced toward the edge of a cliff and thrown over it.
- قام رجال القردة بطقس وحشي يُجبرون فيه الأسرى من البشر على التوجه نحو حافة جرف والقائهم من فوقه.
5. When Summerlee is brought forward as the next victim, Challenger tries to protect him, but the ape chief pushes him aside.
- عندما أُحضر سامرلن ليكون الضحية التالية، حاول تشالنجر حمايته، لكن رئيس رجال القردة دفعه جانبًا.
6. Lord John begins the rescue by shooting the ape chief, while Malone attacks the guards holding Summerlee.
- بدأ لورد جون عملية الإنقاذ بإطلاق النار على رئيس رجال القردة، بينما هاجم مالون الحراس الذين يحتجزون سامرلن.
7. Challenger rescues Summerlee, and Malone gives Challenger a loaded rifle before the group begins to escape.
- أنقذ تشالنجر سامرلن، وأعطى مالون تشالنجر بندقية محمّلة قبل أن تبدأ المجموعة في الهروب.
8. Lord John uses his accurate shooting to prevent the ape-men from getting close to the escaping group.
- استخدم لورد جون دقة تصويبه لمنع رجال القردة من الاقتراب من المجموعة الهاربة.
9. After reaching their hidden shelter, Summerlee thanks Malone and Lord John for saving his life.
- بعد وصولهم إلى مخبئهم، شكر سامرلن مالون ولورد جون لإنقاذ حياتهم.
10. The rescued prisoners introduce themselves as the Accala, a human group living in caves on the far side of Lake Gladys.
- عرف الأسرى الذين تم إنقاذهم أنفسهم بأنهم الأكالا، وهم جماعة بشرية تعيش في كهوف على الجانب الآخر من بحيرة جلاديس.
11. The Accala and the ape-men have been enemies for generations, and the ape-men often capture Accala hunters.
- ظل الأكالا ورجال القردة أعداء لأجيال، وكان رجال القردة كثيرًا ما يأسرون صيادي الأكالا.
12. The young Accala chief wants to return to his people, so the explorers decide to travel with him.
- يريد رئيس الأكالا الشاب العودة إلى شعبه، لذلك قرر المستكشفون السفر معه.

13. Challenger believes that the Accala caves may reveal how humans first reached the plateau, while Lord John believes the Accala can protect them.
- يعتقد تشالنجر أن كهوف الأكالا قد تكشف كيف وصل البشر إلى الهضبة لأول مرة. بينما يعتقد لورد جون أن الأكالا يستطيعون حمايتهم.
14. At Lake Gladys, dozens of Accala arrive in canoes to rescue their chief and the other prisoners.
- عند بحيرة جلاديس، وصل العشرات من أفراد الأكالا في زوارق لإنقاذ رئيسهم وبقية الأسرى.
15. The Accala warmly welcome their rescued people and show gratitude to the four explorers who helped them.
- رحب الأكالا بحرارة بأفراد شعوبهم الذين تم إنقاذهم وأظهروا امتنانهم للمستكشفين الأربعة الذين ساعدوهم.
16. The group crosses Lake Gladys and reaches the Accala village, which is located high in the cliffs for protection from dangerous animals.
- عبرت المجموعة بحيرة جلاديس ووصلت إلى قرية الأكالا التي تقع مرتفعة في الجروف لحمايتها من الحيوانات الخطرة.
17. Inside the caves, the explorers discover drawings of dinosaurs, flying reptiles, huge fish-like animals, and other creatures.
- داخل الكهوف، اكتشف المستكشفون رسومات للديناصورات والزواحف الطائرة والحيوانات الضخمة الشبيهة بالأسماك ومخلوقات أخرى.
18. Challenger and Summerlee are excited because they believe the drawings provide a natural record of the plateau's animal life and history.
- شعر تشالنجر وسامرلن بالحماس لأنهم يعتقدون أن الرسومات تقدم سجلًا طبيعيًا للحياة الحيوانية في الهضبة وتاريخها.
19. During a council, the young Accala chief makes it clear that he wants to fight the ape-men who have attacked his people for years.
- خلال اجتماع للقبيلة، أوضح رئيس الأكالا الشاب أنه يريد محاربة رجال القردة الذين هاجموا شعبه لسنوات.
20. Lord John argues that the ape-men will probably attack them anyway and that the Accala have already given them shelter and protection.
- يرى لورد جون أن رجال القردة سيهاجموهم على أي حال، وأن الأكالا سبق أن قدموا لهم المأوى والحماية.
21. Malone does not want more violence, but he realizes that the ape-men are already hunting them and remembers the prisoners who were killed.
- لا يريد مالون المزيد من العنف، لكنه يدرك أن رجال القردة يطاردونهم بالفعل ويتذكر الأسرى الذين قُتلوا.
22. The next morning, hundreds of Accala gather with spears and bows, while the four explorers prepare their rifles for battle.
- في صباح اليوم التالي، تجمع مئات من أفراد الأكالا بالرمح والأقواس. بينما جهز المستكشفون الأربعة بنادقهم للمعركة.
23. The expedition that began as a search for scientific knowledge has now become a battle for survival in the lost world.
- تحولت البعثة التي بدأت بمجرد عن المعرفة العلمية الآن إلى معركة من أجل البقاء في العالم المفقود.

◆ نص الفصل | Chapter Text

Lord John and I moved slowly through the forest. We avoided open ground and stopped whenever we heard the strange clicking calls of the ape-men. More than once we hid in bushes while a group passed close by.
Near the cliff we could see Zambo far below, still waiting at his camp. I wanted to call to him, but Lord John shook his head. A shout could reveal our position. After two hours he raised one hand and pointed ahead.
Through the leaves I saw a large clearing. Huts made from branches had been built high in the surrounding trees. Ape-men crowded the branches and the ground below. In the center stood several prisoners.
I recognized Professor Summerlee at once. His clothes were torn and his head was bowed.
Challenger stood nearby, guarded but not tied as tightly. Beside them were several small, strong-looking human prisoners - members of the group whose smoke I had seen near the lake.
Lord John pulled me back into cover.
"We wait until we have a clear shot," he whispered. What happened next was difficult to watch.
The ape-men led one of the human prisoners to the edge of the cliff. Far below, sharp bamboo canes grew from the ground. The prisoners were being forced over the edge as part of a cruel ceremony.
One man was thrown.
The ape-men shouted and jumped with excitement. Summerlee was brought forward next.
Challenger struggled against his guards. He shouted and tried to stop them, but the ape chief pushed him aside.

Lord John raised his rifle. "Now," he said. His first shot hit the chief. The creature fell.
I fired at the guards holding Summerlee. Lord John fired again. Confusion spread through the clearing. The ape-men had never faced rifles at close range. They did not understand where the sudden attack was coming from.
"Move!" Lord John shouted.
Challenger seized Summerlee and pulled him towards the trees. I ran out to meet them and pushed a loaded rifle into Challenger's hands. Lord John covered our retreat.

The remaining human prisoners followed us. Ape-men rushed from the bushes behind, but each time they came too close, Lord John's accurate shooting forced them back.
Summerlee was exhausted and could barely walk. Challenger and I supported him between us.
After a long, desperate retreat, we reached our hidden shelter in the thick bushes. For several minutes no one spoke. Then Summerlee looked at me. "Mr. Malone," he said weakly, "I believe I owe you and Lord John a considerable debt."
"You can repay it by staying alive," Lord John replied.
The rescued prisoners called themselves Accala. We could not understand their language, but one young man was clearly their leader. He had a shaved area at the front of his head and carried himself with quiet authority. The others treated him with great respect.
Using gestures, he explained that the Accala lived in caves on the far side of Lake Gladys. The ape-men controlled the forest on this side of the plateau. The two groups had fought for generations. The ape-men often captured Accala hunters and carried them away.

The young chief repeatedly pointed towards the lake, then at us, then toward the forest of the ape-men. He wanted to return to his people.
"We should go with them," Challenger said. "Their caves may reveal how humans first reached the plateau."
Summerlee looked at him in disbelief. "Our first task is to escape this place." "And how do you propose to do that without learning more about its inhabitants?" For once, Lord John ended the argument quickly. "The ape-men know our old camp. We cannot stay here. The Accala have friends, food, and a defensible position. We go with them."
The next afternoon we started across the plateau.
We were a strange group: four exhausted travelers carrying modern rifles, several rescued Accala, and a young chief guiding us through a land of dinosaurs. At the shore of Lake Gladys, the Accala suddenly began shouting with joy. A fleet of canoes was moving across the water towards us.
The young chief's people had come looking for him.
When the canoes reached shore, dozens of Accala rushed forward. They embraced the rescued men and surrounded their young leader. Many then turned toward us and bowed or touched our hands. It was clear that they understood we had helped save him.
We crossed the lake in their canoes. For the first time I saw the far cliffs closely. Many dark cave entrances opened high above the ground, reached by narrow stone steps.
The Accala village was protected from large animals by its height. Inside the caves were sleeping areas, stores, tools, and drawings of the plateau's creatures. The walls showed iguanodons, flying reptiles, huge fish-like animals, and many shapes we had not yet seen.
Challenger and Summerlee forgot their exhaustion. They examined everything. "A natural record of the animal life," Summerlee said.
"And perhaps of its history," Challenger added. That evening the Accala held a council. The young chief spoke passionately, pointing again and again toward the forest of the ape-men. We could not follow his words, but we understood the meaning.
He wanted to fight.

Lord John watched him carefully. "He thinks this is his chance. Their enemies have captured and killed his people for years. Now he has us and our rifles."
Summerlee frowned. "We did not come here to join a war."
"No," Lord John said, "but the ape-men will come after us anyway. And these people saved us by giving us shelter."
Challenger nodded. "If their victory also gives us safe access to the plateau, it may be necessary."
I thought of the prisoners at the cliff. I did not want more violence, but I knew the ape-men were already hunting us.
The next morning, hundreds of Accala gathered with spears and bows. The four of us checked our rifles.
The expedition had come to search for scientific truth. Now we were about to take part in a battle for the lost world.

◆ الأسئلة والأجوبة | Questions and Answers

1. Explain how Lord John and Malone rescue Summerlee, Challenger, and the Accala prisoners. [SB]
- اشرح كيف أنقذ لورد جون ومالون سمرلي وتشالنجر وأسرى الأكالا.
• Lord John and Malone rescue them by attacking the ape-men with their rifles. Lord John shoots the ape chief and the guards, while Malone helps free Summerlee and gives Challenger a loaded rifle. They then escape through the forest with the rescued prisoners while Lord John protects them.
2. Explain who the Accala are and what conflict has existed between them and the ape-men. [SB]
- اشرح من هم الأكالا وما الصراع الذي كان قائماً بينهم وبين حمال القردة.
• The Accala are a group of humans who live in caves on the far side of Lake Gladys. They have been fighting the ape-men for generations because the ape-men often capture and kill Accala hunters.
3. Why do the explorers decide to travel with the Accala and why does the cave settlement interest the scientists? [SB]
- لماذا قرر المستكشفون السفر مع الأكالا؟ ولماذا أثار مستوطنتهم داخل الكهوف اهتمام العلماء؟
• The explorers decide to travel with the Accala because their old camp is no longer safe and the Accala can provide food, shelter, and protection. The caves interest Challenger and Summerlee because they contain drawings and records of many creatures living on the plateau, which may reveal important information about its history.

4. How does the rescue scene show the value of teamwork and the different strengths of the explorers?
[SB]
- كيف يُظهر مشهد الإنقاذ أهمية العمل الجماعي ونقاط القوة المختلفة لدى المستكشفين؟
• The rescue shows that teamwork is essential because each explorer has a different role. Lord John uses his excellent shooting skills to protect the group, Malone helps the prisoners and supports Summerlee, while Challenger helps Summerlee escape and continues to think about the scientific importance of the plateau.
5. Analyze the disagreement between Summerlee and the others about becoming involved in the Accala conflict.
[SB]
- حلّل الخلاف بين سمرلي والآخرين بشار التورط في صراع الأكالا.
• Summerlee believes that their main goal is to escape the plateau and that they should not become involved in a war. However, Lord John and Challenger believe that the ape-men will attack them anyway and that helping the Accala may provide safety and access to more information about the plateau.
6. Analyze the importance of the drawings inside the Accala caves as a record of life on the plateau.
[SB]
- حلّل أهمية الرسومات الموجودة داخل كهوف الأكالا باعتبارها سجلاً للحياة على الهضبة.
• The drawings are important because they show many of the creatures that live or once lived on the plateau. They provide the scientists with valuable evidence about the animal life and history of this mysterious lost world.
7. Evaluate the explorers' decision to take part in the coming battle. Was it justified, or did it take them too far from their original mission?
[SB]
- قيّم قرار المستكشفين بالمشاركة في المعركة القادمة. هل كان القرار مبّراً، أم أنه أبعدهم كثيراً عن مهمتهم الأصلية؟
• The decision was partly justified because the ape-men were already hunting the explorers, and the Accala had given them shelter and helped rescue them. Although the explorers originally came to study the plateau scientifically, joining the battle became necessary for their protection and survival.
8. Evaluate Lord John's decision to leave the old camp and travel with the Accala. Was it the safest option available? Explain.
[SB]
- قيّم قرار لورد جون ترك المعسكر القديم والسفر مع الأكالا. هل كان هذا الخيار الأكثر أماناً؟ اشرح.
• Yes, Lord John's decision was the safest option available. The ape-men already knew the location of the old camp, so staying there would have put the explorers in danger. The Accala offered them food, shelter, protection, and a defensible position. Therefore, traveling with the Accala gave the explorers a better chance of surviving and escaping the ape-men.

9. Why did Lord John insist on waiting for a clear shot before attacking the ape-men?
- لماذا أصرّ لورد جون على الانتظار حتى تات له فرصة واضحة لإطلاق النار قبل مهاجمة رجال القردة؟
• Lord John wanted to make sure that his attack would be effective and would not put the prisoners in greater danger. He waited for the right moment so that he could shoot the ape chief and help the prisoners escape safely.
10. How did the ape-men's reaction to the rifles help the explorers during the rescue?
- كيف ساعد رد فعل رجال القردة تجاه البنادق المستكشفين أثناء عملية الإنقاذ؟
• The ape-men had never faced rifles at close range, so they were confused and did not know where the attack was coming from. This gave the explorers an advantage and allowed them to rescue the prisoners and escape.
11. What does Summerlee's reaction after the rescue reveal about his character?
- ماذا يكشف رد فعل سمرلي بعد عملية الإنقاذ عن شخصيته؟
• Summerlee's reaction shows that he was grateful to Malone and Lord John for saving his life. Although he was exhausted, he recognized their courage and felt that he owed them a great debt.
12. Why was the young Accala chief treated with great respect by his people?
- لماذا كان زعيم الأكالا الشاب يُعامل باحترام كبير من شعبه؟
• The young man was the leader of the Accala, so his people clearly respected his authority. When they found him, they surrounded him, embraced him, and showed great happiness at his safe return.
13. What information did the explorers learn about the history of the Accala and the ape-men?
ما المعلومات التي عرفها المستكشفون عن تاريخ الأكالا ورجال القردة؟
• They learned that the Accala lived in caves on the far side of Lake Gladys and had been fighting the ape-men for generations. The ape-men often captured Accala hunters and took them away.
14. Why were Challenger and Summerlee especially interested in the Accala caves?
- لماذا كانت تشالنجر وسمرلي مهتمين بشكل خاص بكهوف الأكالا؟
• The caves contained tools, stores, sleeping areas, and drawings of many creatures living on the plateau. The drawings could provide valuable information about the animal life and history of the Lost World.
15. What change had taken place in the explorers' mission by the end of the chapter?
- ما التغيير الذي طرأ على مهمة المستكشفين بحلول نهاية الفصل؟
• At first, the explorers had come to the plateau to search for scientific knowledge and study its unusual creatures. By the end of the chapter, they had become involved in the conflict between the Accala and the ape-men and were preparing to take part in a battle.

◆ JRE — نماذج إجابة | JRE Model Answers (Justify – Reason – Evidence)
Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.
1. Are the explorers right to support the Accala against the ape-men?
[SB]
- هل من الصواب أن يدعم المستكشفون قبيلة أكالا ضد رجال القردة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, the explorers are right to support the Accala because the ape-men are dangerous enemies who have captured and killed Accala people.
Reasoning : Explain the strongest reason for your view.
Answer : The strongest reason is that the ape-men have already attacked the explorers and treated their prisoners cruelly. Supporting the Accala can also help the explorers protect themselves.
Evidence : Use details about the prisoners, the danger to the explorers, or the Accala's help.
Answer : The ape-men captured Summerlee, Challenger, Lord John, and several Accala prisoners. They also tried to kill the prisoners in a cruel ceremony. In addition, the Accala gave the explorers shelter, food, and protection, so helping them is reasonable.
2. At this point, should the expedition make escape its first priority rather than further scientific exploration?
[SB]
- في هذه المرحلة، هل ينبغي أن تجعل البعثة الهروب أولويتها الأولى بدلاً من مواصلة الاستكشاف العلمي؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, the expedition should make escape its first priority because the explorers are in serious danger and the ape-men are still hunting them.
Reasoning : Explain which goal is more urgent and why.
Answer : Escape is more urgent because the explorers cannot continue their scientific work safely while they are being attacked and pursued. Their survival must come before scientific discoveries.
Evidence : Use evidence from Summerlee's argument and the group's situation.
Answer : Summerlee says that their first task is to escape the plateau. The group has already been captured and attacked by the ape-men, and their old camp is no longer safe. Even though the caves contain valuable scientific information, the explorers must first protect themselves.
3. Was Lord John right to risk his life to rescue the prisoners from the ape-men?
- هل كان لورد جون على صواب عندما خاطر بحياته لإنقاذ الأسرى من رجال القردة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Lord John was right to risk his life because the prisoners were in immediate danger and could have been killed.
Reasoning : Explain the strongest reason for your view.
Answer : Leaving the prisoners would have meant allowing the ape-men to continue their cruel treatment. Lord John had the skills and courage needed to give them a chance to escape.

Evidence : Use details from the rescue scene.
Answer : Lord John attacked the ape chief with his rifle, helped free Summerlee, and protected the group during their retreat. His accurate shooting prevented the ape-men from getting too close.
4. Was the Accala's decision to help the explorers important to their survival?
- هل كان قرار الأكالا بمساعدة المستكشفين مهماً لبقائهم على قيد الحياة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, the Accala's help was very important to the explorers' survival.
Reasoning : Explain the strongest reason for your view.
Answer : The explorers could no longer remain safely in their old camp, so they needed a safe place, food, and protection from the ape-men.
Evidence : Use details about the Accala village and the explorers' situation.
Answer : The Accala took the explorers across Lake Gladys and gave them shelter in their protected cave settlement. Their village provided a defensible position away from the dangerous animals and ape-men.
5. Was Challenger right to remain interested in scientific discoveries despite the danger around him؟ هل كان تشالنجر على صواب في الاستمرار في الاهتمام بالاكتشافات العلمية رغم الخطر المحيط به؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Challenger was right to remain interested in scientific discoveries, although he also needed to consider the group's safety.
Reasoning : Explain the strongest reason for your view.
Answer : The expedition's main purpose was to discover and study the lost world, and the caves contained valuable evidence that could not be found anywhere else.
Evidence : Use details about the cave drawings and Challenger's reaction.
Answer : The caves contained drawings of iguanodons, flying reptiles, huge fish-like animals, and other creatures. Challenger immediately became interested in them because they could reveal important information about the history of life on the plateau.
6. Was it reasonable for the explorers to prepare for the battle between the Accala and the ape-men؟ هل كان من المنطقي أن يستعد المستكشفون للمعركة بين الأكالا ورجال القردة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, it was reasonable for the explorers to prepare for the battle because the ape-men were already their enemies.
Reasoning : Explain the strongest reason for your view.
Answer : The explorers could not simply avoid the conflict because the ape-men knew about them and were already hunting them. Helping the Accala could also give them a better chance of survival.
Evidence : Use details from the end of the chapter.
Answer : The ape-men had captured the explorers before and attacked their camp. The Accala had protected the explorers, and by the end of the chapter hundreds of Accala were preparing for battle while the explorers checked their rifles.

الفصل 8 | Chapter 8: The Battle for the Plateau

◆ المفردات | Vocabulary

conflict (n) | صراع/نزاع | herd (n) | قطيع | prehistoric (adj) | من عصور ما قبل التاريخ
creature (n) | مخلوق/كائن | honored (adj) | مُكرم/ محل تقدير | pursuit (n) | مطاردة
defeat (n) (v) (ed) | هزيمة/يهزم | interfere (v) (d) | يتدخل | resistance (n) | مقاومة
demonstration (n) | عرض/ تجربة توضيحية | loyalty (n) | ولاء/اخلاص | sympathy (n) | تعاطف
