◆ التعبيرات والمصطلحات وحروف الجر | Expressions, Phrases & Prepositions
break the power of | يحطم/يقتضي على قوة | never waste a shot | لا يهدراي طلقة
carry valuable evidence | يحمل أدلة قيمة | remain loyal to someone | يظل مخلصا لشخص
come to someone's aid | ياتي لمساعدة شخص | search for a way out | يبحث عن مخرج
control a herd of animals | يسيطر على قطيع من الحيوانات | take control of | يسيطر على
fight for survival | يكافح من اجل البقاء | under Accala control | تحت سيطرة الأكالا
have scientific evidence | يمتلك ادلة علمية | use something as protection | يستخدم شيئاً كوسيله للحماية
hold someone under control | يضع شخصا تحت السيطرة | witness a remarkable event | يشهد حدثاً رائعا/ استثنائيا
make escape more urgent | يجعل الهروب اكثر الحاحا

◆ الفصل في نقاط | Chapter in Points
1. The Accala army moves toward the forest in a long line, with scouts in front and the main force behind them. Lord John and Summerlee take the right side, while Challenger and Malone move on the left.
- يتحرك جيش الأكالا نحو الغابة في صف طويل، حيث يتقدم الكشافة المجموعة الرئيسية. بينما يتخذ لورد جون وسمرلي الجانب الأيمن، ويتحرك تشالنجر ومالون في الجانب الأيسر.
2. The first attack begins suddenly when the ape-men rush from the trees, using heavy clubs and stones. The Accala respond quickly with arrows and spears and surround the attackers.
- يبدأ الهجوم الأول فجأة عندما ينفجر رجال القردة من الأشجار مستخدمين العصي الثقيلة والحجارة، ويرد الأكالا بسرعة بالسهام والرماح ويحاصرون المهاجمين.
3. The fighting becomes more dangerous inside the forest because the ape-men attack from the branches and bushes. One of them breaks Summerlee's rifle, but an Accala warrior saves him from another attack.
- يصبح القتال أكثر خطورة داخل الغابة لأن رجال القردة يهاجمون من فوق الأشجار ومن خلف الشجيرات. ويكسر أحدهم بندقية سمرلي، لكن محاربًا من الأكالا ينقذه من هجوم آخر.
4. The explorers' rifles give the Accala an important advantage, but Lord John uses his rifle carefully and never wastes a shot.
- تعطي بنادق المستكشفين الأكالا ميزة مهمة، لكن لورد جون يستخدم بندقيته بحذر ولا يهدر أي طلقة.
5. After more than an hour of fighting, the ape-men begin to retreat, and the Accala follow them to their tree settlement. The Accala eventually break the power and organized resistance of the ape-men.
- بعد أكثر من ساعة من القتال، يبدأ رجال القردة في الانسحاب، ويتبعهم الأكالا إلى مستوطنتهم في الأشجار. وفي النهاية يحطم الأكالا قوة رجال القردة ومقاومتهم المنظمة.
6. Many ape-men are killed during the battle and pursuit, while the surviving females and young are taken under Accala control. Malone feels little joy because the conflict has ended violently after lasting for generations.
- يُقتل العديد من رجال القردة أثناء المعركة والمطاردة. بينما توضع الإناث والصغار الناجون تحت سيطرة الأكالا. ولا يشعر مالون بفرحة كبيرة لأن الصراع انتهى بعنف بعد أن استمر لأجيال.
7. Challenger considers the victory an important turning point in the history of the plateau, while Summerlee simply wants to stop fighting, return to science, and eventually go back to London.
- يعتبر تشالنجر الانتصار نقطة تحول مهمة في تاريخ الهضبة، بينما يريد سمرلي ببساطة التوقف عن القتال والعودة إلى العلم ثم العودة في النهاية إلى لندن.
8. After the victory, the Accala treat the explorers as honored allies and invite them to stay in their caves. However, Lord John prefers to camp nearby because he does not completely trust people whose language they cannot understand.
- بعد الانتصار يعامل الأكالا المستكشفين كحلفاء مكرمين ويدعوهم للإقامة في كهوفهم. لكن لورد جون يفضل التخييم بالقرب منهم لأنه لا يثق تماماً في أشخاص لا يفهم لغتهم.

9. The explorers return to scientific research and discover creatures resembling plesiosaurs living in Lake Gladys. They are amazed to see living examples of animals known mainly from fossils.
— يعود المستكشفون إلى البحث العلمي ويكتشفون مخلوقات تشبه البليزوصورات تعيش في بحيرة جلاديس. ويشعرون بالدهشة لرؤية حيوانات حية كانت معروفة أساسا من خلال الحفريات.
10. The Accala treat some iguanodons like herds of cattle because they can guide and control these huge plant-eating dinosaurs more easily than expected.
— يعامل الأكلا بعض الإجواندونات مثل قطعان الماشية لأنهم يستطيعون توجيه هذه الديناصورات الضخمة آكلة النباتات والسيطرة عليها بسهولة أكبر مما كان متوقعا.
11. Two enormous meat-eating dinosaurs attack a group of hunters, forcing the Accala to run toward the caves. Lord John and Malone shoot at the creatures, and one of them is killed.
— يهاجم ديناصوران ضخمان آكلان للحوم مجموعة من الصيادين، مما يجبر الأكلا على الرخص نحو الكهوف. ويطلق لورد جون ومالون النار على المخلوقات، ويُقتل أحد ها.
12. Despite their scientific success, the explorers become increasingly worried about escaping the plateau because they have collected valuable evidence that could disappear if they die there.
— على الرغم من نجاحهم العلمي، يزداد قلق المستكشفين بشأن الهروب من الهضبة لأنهم جمعوا أدلة قيمة يمكن أن تختفي إذا ماتوا هناك.
13. The Accala explain that an old passage once connected the cliffs to the land below, but an earthquake had blocked the upper part about a year earlier. This explains how Maple White and possibly others had entered the plateau.
— يشرح الأكلا أن ممرا قديما كان يربط الجروف بالأرض الموجودة بالأسفل، لكن زلزالا أغلق الجزء العلوي منه منذ حوالي عام. وهذا يفسر كيف استطاع مايل وايت وربما آخرين دخول الهضبة.
14. The Accala refuse to help the explorers escape because they believe the explorers' weapons helped them win the battle. They want the explorers to remain as their protectors.
— يرفض الأكلا مساعدة المستكشفين على الهروب لأنهم يعتقدون أن أسلحة المستكشفين ساعدهم على الفوز بالمعركة. ويريدون من المستكشفين البقاء ليكونوا حماة لهم.
15. Challenger announces that he has a plan and asks the others not to interfere. At the same time, Lord John secretly travels to the pterodactyl swamp and builds a light protective cage.
— يعلن تشالنجر أن لديه خطة ويطلب من الآخرين ألا يتدخلوا. وفي الوقت نفسه، يذهب لورد جون سرالا إلى مستنقع التيروداكتيل ويبني قفصا خفيفا للحماية.
16. After surviving dinosaurs, betrayal, captivity, and battle, the explorers realize that their final enemy is not a creature but the enormous cliff that separates them from the outside world.
— بعد أن نجوا المستكشفون من الديناصورات والخيانة والأسر والمعركة، يدركون أن عدوهم الأخير ليس مخلوقا، بل الجرف الضخم الذي يفصلهم عن العالم الخارجي.

◆ نص الفصل | Chapter Text
The Accala force moved towards the forest in a long line. Scouts went ahead, while the main group followed with bows and spears. Lord John and Summerlee took the right side. Challenger and I moved on the left.
No one spoke much. Even Challenger seemed to understand that this was not a scientific demonstration.
The first attack came suddenly.
A group of ape-men rushed from the trees, swinging heavy clubs and throwing stones. The Accala were faster. They moved aside, fired arrows and spears, and surrounded the attackers before they could break the line.
The battle became more dangerous inside the forest.
Ape-men dropped from branches. Others charged from behind bushes. Their strength was enormous. One blow broke Summerlee's rifle. An Accala warrior pulled him away before another strike could reach him.
Our rifles gave the Accala an important advantage, but we used them only when the fighting came close. Lord John moved calmly from one position to another, never wasting a shot.
After more than an hour, the ape-men began to retreat. The Accala followed. We reached their tree settlement later that day. The fighting there was fierce but short. The power of the ape-men was broken, and their organized resistance ended. Many were killed during the battle and pursuit. The surviving females and young were taken under Accala control. It was a harsh ending to a conflict that had lasted for generations, and I found little joy in it.
Challenger, however, viewed the event as a turning point in the history of the plateau.

Summerlee had a simpler response.
"I have seen enough battles for one lifetime," he said. "Now let us return to science - and then, if possible, to London."
The victory changed our position completely. The Accala now treated us as honored allies. We could travel more safely through much of the plateau, and they invited us to stay in their caves.
We chose to camp nearby instead. Lord John did not want to depend completely on people whose language we still could not understand.
For the next several days our attention returned to exploration.
At Lake Gladys we saw creatures with long necks and powerful swimming bodies. Challenger and Summerlee agreed that they resembled plesiosaurs, marine reptiles known from fossils. Seeing them alive in fresh water was almost impossible to accept, even after everything else we had witnessed.
We also learned that the Accala treated some iguanodons like herds of cattle. Marks on the animals showed different owners. The huge plant-eaters could be guided and controlled far more easily than their size suggested.
But the plateau still contained predators that no one could control. One afternoon people began racing toward the caves. A warning cry spread through the camp. Two enormous meat-eating dinosaurs burst from the edge of the forest, chasing a group of hunters.
They moved with frightening speed.
Lord John and I fired. One turned toward us, and for a moment I saw its open jaws and powerful legs coming directly across the grass. More shots followed. The creature fell only a short distance away.
The Accala did not treat the event as unusual. To them, the dinosaurs were part of daily life. For us, every new creature expanded the list of discoveries we hoped to carry home.

Summerlee collected insects and plants. Challenger made notes about reptiles and geology. Lord John preserved the skull of a huge flightless predatory bird called a phorusrhacos after we encountered one near the lake. We also observed a large animal resembling an enormous prehistoric relative of a guinea pig, which the professors identified as a notoungulate.
Yet our scientific success made the problem of escape more urgent. We had evidence that could change what people understood about natural history. If we died on the plateau, most of that knowledge would disappear with us.
We asked the Accala how they had reached the plateau. Through signs and drawings, they showed us that an old passage had once connected the cliffs with the land below. An earthquake had blocked the upper part about a year earlier.
That explained why Maple White and perhaps others had been able to enter in the past. "So there was a road," I said.
"There was," Summerlee replied. "That is not the same as saying there is one now." The Accala were surprisingly unwilling to help us leave. At first we thought they did not understand. Then the reason became clearer. They believed our weapons had brought them victory. They wanted us to stay as protectors.
Whenever we asked for long ropes, strong leather strips, or timber for an escape bridge, they smiled and refused.
Only the young chief we had rescued - whose name sounded to us like Maretas - showed sympathy. He watched our failed attempts and seemed troubled. Meanwhile we continued to communicate with Zambo below the original cliff whenever we could reach that side of the plateau. He had not abandoned us. "Help will come," he called up. "I stay here!"
His loyalty gave us hope, but a rescue party could do little if we had no safe way down from the top.
One evening Challenger announced that he had a plan.
"I require several days," he said, "and I require that none of you interfere." Summerlee sighed. "That sentence has never led to anything peaceful." Challenger only smiled.
At almost the same time, Lord John began making private journeys towards the pterodactyl swamp. He built a light cage from bent canes and carried it around himself as protection from attacking beaks.
"What are you doing there?" I asked. "Studying," he said.
"Studying what?"
"You will find out when I am ready."
So, while the professors searched for knowledge, Lord John followed a secret of his own, and Challenger worked on an even stranger idea.
We had survived dinosaurs, betrayal, captivity, and battle. Our final enemy was the cliff itself.

◆ الأسئلة والأجوبة | Questions and Answers
1. Explain how the battle changes the explorers' position on the plateau. [SB]
– اشرح كيف غيّرت المعركة موقع المستكشفين على الهضبة.
• The battle changes the explorers' position because the Accala now regard them as honored allies. They can travel more safely through much of the plateau and are invited to stay in the Accala caves.
2. What new scientific discoveries do the explorers make after the battle? [SB]
– ما الاكتشافات العلمية الجديدة التي يقوم بها المستكشفون بعد المعركة؟
• They discover creatures resembling plesiosaurs in Lake Gladys and learn that the Accala control some iguanodons like herds of cattle. They also collect insects, plants, and study prehistoric animals such as the phorusrhacos and the notougulate.
3. Explain why the Accala are unwilling to help the explorers leave the plateau. [SB]
– اشرح لماذا لا يرغب الأكالا في مساعدة المستكشفين على مغادرة الهضبة.
• The Accala believe that the explorers' weapons helped them defeat the ape-men. Therefore, they want the explorers to stay as protectors and refuse to give them the materials they need to build a safe way down.
4. What is the difference between Malone's and Challenger's reactions to the end of the battle? What does this reveal about their values? [SB]
– ما الفرق بين رد فعل مالون وتشالنجر تجاه نهاية المعركة؟ وما الذي يكشفه ذلك عن قيم كل منهما؟
• Malone feels little joy because many ape-men were killed, showing that he values human life and dislikes unnecessary violence. Challenger sees the battle as a turning point in the history of the plateau, showing that he gives greater importance to scientific and historical significance.
5. Analyze why Lord John chooses to keep the explorers' camp separate from the Accala caves. [SB]
– حلّل لماذا يختار لورد جون إبقاء معسكر المستكشفين منفصلاً عن كهوف الأكالا.
• Lord John wants to remain independent because the explorers still cannot understand the Accala's language completely. He does not want them to depend entirely on people whose intentions and plans they cannot fully understand.
6. Analyze why the explorers' growing collection of scientific evidence makes escape more urgent, not less. [SB]
– حلّل لماذا تجعل الأدلة العلمية المتراكمة التي يجمعها المستكشفون الهروب أكثر إلحاحاً وليس أقل.
• Their discoveries could change people's understanding of natural history. If the explorers die on the plateau, this valuable knowledge and evidence could disappear with them, so they need to escape and take their discoveries home.
7. Evaluate Lord John's decision to keep the group independent from the Accala. Was this a wise precaution? Explain. [SB]
– قيّم قرار لورد جون بإبقاء المجموعة مستقلة عن الأكالا. هل كان هذا إجراءً حكيمًا؟ اشرح.
• Yes, it was a wise precaution because the explorers could not fully understand the Accala's language or intentions. Staying independent allowed them to protect themselves and make their own decisions about exploration and escape.

8. Evaluate the decision to continue exploring after the battle even though dangerous predators still live on the plateau. [SB]
– قيّم قرار الاستمرار في الاستكشاف بعد المعركة رغم وجود حيوانات مفترسة خطيرة على الهضبة.
• The decision was reasonable because the plateau contained unique animals and scientific evidence that could be extremely valuable. However, the explorers had to remain careful because dangerous predators could still threaten their lives.
9. Why does Summerlee want to return to London after the battle?
– لماذا يريد سمرلي العودة إلى لندن بعد المعركة؟
• Summerlee feels that he has experienced enough battles and danger. He wants to return to scientific work and, if possible, go back to London.
10. How does the Accala's treatment of the explorers change after their victory?
– كيف تتغيّر معاملة الأكالا للمستكشفين بعد انتصارهم؟
• The Accala begin to treat the explorers as honored allies. They allow them to travel more safely through the plateau and invite them to stay in their caves.
11. Why are the living plesiosaurs in Lake Gladys especially important to the scientists?
– لماذا تعتبر البليزيوصورات الحية في بحيرة جلاديس مهمة بشكل خاص للعلماء؟
• They are important because plesiosaurs were known mainly from fossils. Seeing living examples provides extraordinary evidence about prehistoric life and could change scientific knowledge.
12. How does the Accala's attitude toward the dinosaurs differ from that of the explorers?
– كيف يختلف موقف الأكالا تجاه الديناصورات عن موقف المستكشفين؟
• The Accala see dinosaurs as a normal part of their daily life, while the explorers see every new creature as a valuable scientific discovery that they hope to document and take home.
13. Why does the young chief, Maretas, show sympathy toward the explorers?
– لماذا يُظهر الشيف الصغير ماريتاس تعاطفاً مع المستكشفين؟
• Maretas understands that the explorers want to leave the plateau and seems troubled when he sees their attempts fail. Unlike the other Accala, he is willing to show sympathy for their difficult situation.
14. What does Zambo's behavior reveal about his character? ماذا يكشف سلوك زامبو عن شخصيته؟
• Zambo's decision to remain below the cliff and continue waiting for the explorers shows that he is loyal, patient, and determined not to abandon them even when helping them is difficult.
15. Why is the cliff described as the explorers'"final enemy"?
– لماذا يوصف الجرف بأنه «العدو الأخير» للمستكشفين؟
• The explorers have survived dinosaurs, betrayal, captivity, and battle, but they still cannot leave the plateau. The cliff is the final obstacle separating them from the outside world and preventing their safe escape.

◆ JRE — نماذج إجابة | JRE Model Answers (Justify – Reason – Evidence)
Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.
1. Does victory in the battle solve the explorers' main problem? [SB]
– هل يحل الانتصار في المعركة المشكلة الرئيسية التي تواجه المستكشفين؟
Justify : State your judgment: yes, no, or partly.
Answer : Partly. The victory solves some of their problems, but it does not solve their biggest problem: escaping from the plateau.
Reasoning : Explain what the victory changes and what it does not change.
Answer : The victory makes the explorers safer because the ape-men's power is broken and the Accala treat them as honored allies. However, they still cannot find a safe way down from the cliffs.
Evidence : Support your answer with details about safety, discoveries, and the cliffs.
Answer : After the battle, the explorers can travel more safely and continue making important scientific discoveries. However, the old passage through the cliffs is blocked, and the Accala refuse to give them materials to build an escape bridge.
2. Are the Accala justified in wanting the explorers to remain on the plateau? [SB]
– هل يحق للأكالا أن يرغبوا في بقاء المستكشفين على الهضبة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Partly, but ultimately no. The Accala have a reasonable reason for wanting the explorers to stay, but the explorers have the right to return home.
Reasoning : Consider the Accala's need for protection and the explorers' right to return home.
Answer : The Accala depend on the explorers' weapons for protection after defeating the ape-men. However, the explorers have their own mission, valuable scientific discoveries to take home, and a need to return safely to their families and world.
Evidence : Use evidence from the chapter to support both sides before giving your final view.
Answer : The Accala believe the explorers' weapons brought them victory and therefore want them to remain as protectors. On the other hand, the explorers repeatedly try to build an escape bridge and need to return home with evidence that could change people's understanding of natural history.

3. Is Lord John right to remain cautious about depending on the Accala?
– هل كان لورد جون محقًا في توخي الحذر من الاعتماد على الأكالا؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes. Lord John is right to remain cautious about depending completely on the Accala.
Reasoning : Explain why Lord John may not completely trust the Accala.
Answer : The explorers still cannot understand the Accala's language completely, and the Accala have their own interests. They want the explorers to remain on the plateau as protectors.
Evidence : Use details about the language barrier, the Accala's refusal to help them escape, and Lord John's decision to camp separately.
Answer : Lord John chooses to camp near the Accala rather than inside their caves because he does not want to depend completely on people whose language he cannot understand. Later, the Accala refuse to provide ropes, leather, and timber to help the explorers escape.
4. Is Malone's reaction to the end of the battle more reasonable than Challenger's?
– هل كان رد فعل مالون على نهاية المعركة أكثر عقلانية من رد فعل تشالنجر؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, in terms of human feelings. Malone's reaction is more sympathetic because he focuses on the suffering and deaths caused by the battle.
Reasoning : Compare Malone's feelings about the deaths with Challenger's view of the battle as a historical turning point.
Answer : Malone feels little joy because many creatures were killed, while Challenger focuses on the historical importance of the victory. Malone's reaction shows greater concern for life and the human cost of violence.
Evidence : Use details about Malone's lack of joy and Challenger's scientific interest in the event.
Answer : Malone says that he found little joy in the harsh ending of the conflict. Challenger, however, considers the battle a turning point in the history of the plateau.

5. Should the explorers have stopped exploring after encountering the dangerous predators?
- هل كان يجب على المستكشفين التوقف عن الاستكشاف بعد مواجهتهم للحيوانات المفترسة الخطيرة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : No, but they should have explored more carefully. The scientific value of the plateau was too important to ignore.
Reasoning : Consider the danger of the predators and the scientific value of the plateau.
Answer : The predators could seriously threaten their lives, but the plateau contains unique species and evidence about prehistoric life. Therefore, continuing the research was reasonable as long as they took necessary precautions.
Evidence : Use details about the dinosaur attack and the discoveries made by the scientists.
Answer : Two enormous meat-eating dinosaurs attack a group of hunters, proving the danger. Despite this, the explorers discover plesiosaurs, iguanodons, a phorusrhacos, and a notougulate, making their continued research scientifically valuable.
6. Is the blocked passage the most serious obstacle facing the explorers?
- هل يُعد الممر المغلق أَخطر عقبة تواجه المستكشفين؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes. The blocked passage is their most serious obstacle because it prevents them from returning to the outside world.
Reasoning : Compare the blocked passage with the other dangers and problems they have faced.
Answer : The explorers have survived dinosaurs, ape-men, captivity, and battle, but the blocked passage directly prevents their escape. Without another route down the cliffs, even their scientific success cannot help them return home.
Evidence : Use details about the dinosaurs, ape-men, Accala, the cliffs, and their attempts to escape.
Answer : The explorers have already survived attacks from dinosaurs and ape-men and gained the Accala's protection. However, the old passage has been blocked by an earthquake, and the Accala refuse to give them materials for an escape bridge, leaving the cliffs as their final major obstacle.

◆ الفصل في نقاط | Chapter in Points
1. Professor Challenger reveals his plan to escape from the plateau by using hydrogen gas from a mud pool to fill a huge prehistoric animal membrane and create a balloon.
— يكشف البروفيسور تشالنجر عن خطته للهروب من الهضبة باستخدام غاز الهيدروجين الموجود في بركة طينية لملء غشاء ضخم من جلد حيوان من عصور ما قبل التاريخ وصنيع مناطد.
2. Challenger tests the balloon by attaching a heavy stone to it, but the experiment becomes dangerous when the balloon suddenly rises and almost carries Challenger and the others into the air.
— يختبر تشالنجر المنطاد بربط حجر ثقيل به، لكن التجربة تصبح خطيرة عندما يرتفع المنطاد فجأة ويكاد يحمل تشالنجر والأخرين إلى الهواء.
3. Although the experiment nearly causes an accident, Challenger considers it a success because it proves that the balloon can lift weight. However, the others are not completely confident about using it.
— على الرغم من أن التجربة كادت أن تسبب حادثًا، فإن تشالنجر يعتبرها ناجحة لأنها أثبتت أن المنطاد يستطيع رفع وزن. ومع ذلك، لم يكن الآخرون واثقين تمامًا من استخدامه.
4. Malone continues visiting the original cliff to communicate with Zambo, who tells him that a messenger has gone to find stronger ropes and more men to help rescue them.
— يواصل مالون زيارة الجرف الأصلي للتواصل مع زامبو، الذي يخبره أن أحد الرسل ذهب للبحث عن حبال أقوى والمزيد من الرجال للمساعدة في إنقاذهم.
5. Lord John continues making secret trips to the pterodactyl swamp and refuses to explain what he is studying there.
— يواصل لورد جون القيام برحلات سرية إلى مستنقع التيروداكتيل ويرفض أن يوضح ما الذي يدرسه هناك.
6. Maretas secretly takes Malone to a hidden part of the Accala caves and reveals a small opening behind some stones.
— يأخذ ماريتاس مالون سرًا إلى جزء مخفي من كهوف الأكالا ويكشف عن فتحة صغيرة خلف بعض الحجارة.
7. Maretas shows Malone that the opening leads downward, giving the explorers hope that they have discovered a way out of the plateau.
— يوضح ماريتاس لمالون أن الفتحة تؤدي إلى الأسفل، مما يمنح المستكشفين الأمل في أنهم اكتشفوا طريقًا للخروج من الهضبة.
8. Malone brings Challenger, Summerlee, and Lord John to the hidden passage. They travel through a narrow tunnel and overcome a collapsed section filled with loose rocks.
— يحضر مالون تشالنجر وسومرلي ولورد جون إلى الممر السري. ويسيرون عبر نفق ضيق ويتجاوزون جزءًا منه انهار وامتلأ بالصخور المتحركة.
9. After moving through the darkness, the explorers reach a larger natural cave and see a red glow ahead. They discover that it is the moon shining through a second exit in the cliff.
— بعد التجرك وسط الظلام، يصل المستكشفون إلى كهف طبيعي أكبر ويرون ضوءًا أحمر أمامهم. ويكتشفون أنه ضوء القمر الذي يمر من خلال مخروج ثانٍ في الجرف.
10. The explorers realize that the new exit is suitable for escape because it is difficult to see from below and they can reach the ground using a rope.
— يدرك المستكشفون أن المخرج الجديد مناسب للهروب لأنه من الصعب رؤيته من الأسفل، كما يمكنهم الوصول إلى الأرض باستخدام حبل.

11. The explorers decide to leave secretly without telling the Accala leaders because they are no longer certain that the Accala would allow them to leave.
— يقرر المستكشفون الرحيل سرًا دون إخبار قادة الأكالا لأنهم لم يعودوا متأكدين من أن الأكالا سيسمحون لهم بالمغادرة.
12. Before leaving, they take only essential supplies, including rifles, ammunition, notebooks, specimens, and other necessary equipment. Challenger insists on carrying a heavy wooden case containing secret scientific material.
— قبل الرحيل، يأخذون فقط المؤن الأساسية، بما في ذلك البنادق والذخيرة ودفاتر الملاحظات والعينات والمعدات الضرورية الأخرى. ويصر تشالنجر على حمل صندوق خشبي ثقيل يحتوي على مواد علمية سرية.
13. After dark, the explorers enter the hidden passage. Malone looks back at the plateau and reflects on the dangers, arguments, and experiences that have changed them.
— بعد حلول الظلام، يدخل المستكشفون الممر السري. وينظر مالون إلى الهضبة ويتأمل المخاطر والخلافات والتجارب التي غيرتهم.
14. The explorers have learned to depend on one another after surviving fear, injuries, captivity, disagreements, and many dangerous situations together.
— تعلم المستكشفون الاعتماد على بعضهم البعض بعد أن نجوا معًا من الخوف والإصابات والأسر والخلافات والعديد من المواقف الخطيرة.
15. Lord John goes down the cliff first using the rope, followed by Malone, Summerlee, and finally Challenger, who continues to protect his heavy wooden case.
— ينزل لورد جون من الجرف أولًا باستخدام الحبل، يليه مالون ثم سمرلي وأخيرًا تشالنجر، مع حرصه على حماية صندوقه الخشبي الثقيل.
16. When Malone reaches the ground, he feels an overwhelming sense of relief and realizes how much he has missed ordinary life outside the plateau.
— عندما يصل مالون إلى الأرض، يشعر براحة شديدة ويدرك مدى اشتياقه إلى الحياة العادية خارج الهضبة.
17. The explorers travel through the night toward Zambo's camp, and at dawn they see many fires because a rescue party has arrived with twenty men, ropes, and equipment.
— يسافر المستكشفون طوال الليل نحو معسكر زامبو، وعند الفجر يرون العديد من النيران لأن فريق إنقاذ وصل ومعه عشرون رجلًا وحبال ومعدات.
18. Zambo welcomes the explorers happily, and for the first time in weeks they know that they are truly safe.
— يرحب زامبو بالمستكشفين بسعادة، ولأول مرة منذ أسابيع يعرفون أنهم أصبحوا آمنين حقًا.
19. The journey back to England is long but feels easy compared with their experiences on the plateau. They travel downriver, reach Para, and finally sail for England.
— تكون الرحلة إلى إنجلترا طويلة، لكنها تبدو سهلة مقارنة بتجاربهم على الهضبة. ويسافرون عبر النهر ويصلون إلى بارا، ثم يبحرون أخيرًا إلى إنجلترا.
20. On the journey home, Challenger protects his mysterious wooden case, Lord John guards a secret small box, Summerlee organizes his scientific specimens, and Malone writes down the story of their adventure.
— في رحلة العودة، يحافظ تشالنجر على صندوقه الخشبي الغامض، ويحرس لورد جون صندوقًا صغيرًا سريًا، وينظم سمرلي عيناته العلمية، ويكتب مالون قصة مغامرتهم.
21. Malone thinks about Gladys and remembers that he originally left England hoping to become a man she would admire. After everything he has experienced, he wonders what she will think when she sees him again.
— يفكر مالون في جلاديس ويتذكر أنه غادر إنجلترا في الأصل على أمل أن يصبح الرجل الذي ستعجب به. وبعد كل ما مر به، يتساءل عما ستذكر فيه عندما تراه مرة أخرى.

◆ نص الفصل | Chapter Text
Professor Challenger eventually led us to a small clearing where hot gas bubbled through a mud pool. Around it he had arranged bamboo tubes, leather straps, and a huge dried membrane made from part of a prehistoric animal. Summerlee stopped in disbelief. "What is that?""Our way down," Challenger said.
The gas from the ground, he explained, contained hydrogen. He had collected it inside the large membrane to create a balloon. If the balloon could lift one person at a time, perhaps we could descend from the plateau. Lord John was delighted by the idea. Summerlee called it madness.
Challenger attached a heavy stone to the balloon to test its lifting power. The experiment worked far too well. The balloon shot upwards, pulled Challenger off his feet, and almost carried him into the air. I grabbed Challenger, Lord John grabbed me, and for one ridiculous second all of us were being dragged across the ground together. Then the rope snapped.
The balloon and stone disappeared into the sky. Challenger stood up, brushed dirt from his clothes, and looked pleased. "An excellent demonstration," he said. Summerlee stared at him. "You nearly killed us." "A small technical problem. The principle is proved." He promised to build another balloon, but none of us felt completely confident about becoming its passenger. During these same days I traveled twice to the original cliff to speak with Zambo. He told me that one of our messengers had gone to search for stronger ropes and more men. Help should arrive soon.
On my second return journey I met Lord John near the pterodactyl swamp. He was inside his strange cane cage.
"Still studying?" I asked.
"Very seriously," he said with a smile. He would tell me no more.

When I reached the Accala camp, I noticed that Maretas was waiting for me. He looked around to make sure no one was watching, then touched my arm and pointed towards one of the caves.
I followed him.
He led me up a narrow stair and into a passage deeper than the others. At the back wall he moved several stones and revealed a small opening.
Then he pointed at me, at the opening, and finally downwards. My heart began to beat faster.
"A way out?" I asked, though he could not understand the words. Maretas nodded. That night I brought Challenger, Summerlee, and Lord John to the place. We carried lamps and rope. Maretas was gone, perhaps to avoid being seen helping us. The hidden passage was narrow at first, then widened. We moved through darkness for hundreds of metres. At one point part of the tunnel had collapsed, and we had to crawl over loose rock. Beyond it we found a larger natural cave.
Ahead, a red glow seemed to block the passage. We hurried towards it. "The moon!" Lord John cried.
A round opening in the cliff showed the full moon outside. We had found a second exit from the plateau.
The opening was too small to have been noticed easily from below, and the cliff curved over it. We tested the distance to the ground. With our rope, the descent would be possible.
Summerlee sat down on a rock and laughed with relief.
"At last," he said. "A plan that does not involve flying inside a dinosaur."We returned quietly to camp. The next day we prepared to leave without telling the Accala leaders. They had treated us generously, but we were no longer sure they would allow us to go.
We took only what we could carry: rifles, ammunition, notebooks, a few specimens, and essential supplies. Challenger insisted on bringing one large wooden case. "What is inside?" Summerlee asked. "Scientific material."
"That answer usually means trouble." Challenger refused to explain.
After dark we climbed to the hidden passage. Before entering, I looked back across the plateau.
Small fires shone near the Accala caves. Beyond them stretched the forest. Lake Gladys reflected the moon. Somewhere in the distance an unknown animal gave a long cry.
I had dreamed of adventure in London, but I had never imagined anything like this place. We had been frightened, injured, trapped, and sometimes divided by argument. Yet the lost world had also changed us. We had learned to depend on one another.

We entered the cave.
Two hours later we reached the opening in the outer cliff. Lord John went first, using the rope. I followed, then Summerlee. Challenger came last, still protecting his heavy case.
When my feet touched the ground below, I wanted to shout. I had not realized how strongly I had missed ordinary earth.
We made our way through the night towards Zambo's camp. At dawn we saw smoke ahead—not one fire, but many.
A rescue party had arrived with twenty men, ropes, and equipment. Zambo ran towards us with a smile so wide that it seemed to light his whole face.
"You come back! I knew you would come back!"
For the first time in weeks, we were truly safe.
The return journey to the Amazon was long, but it felt simple after the plateau. We traveled downriver, reached Para, and finally sailed for England.
Challenger guarded his wooden case as if it contained the future of science. Lord John also protected a small box that he refused to discuss.
Summerlee organized his insect and plant specimens. I wrote as much of our story as I could remember.
There was one thing I still thought about often: Gladys.
I had left England hoping to become the kind of man she might admire. I was returning after facing more danger than either of us could have imagined. I wondered what she would say when she saw me again.

◆ الأسئلة والأجوبة | Questions and Answers
1. Explain Challenger's balloon plan and why the first test fails. [SB]
- اشرح خطة تشالنجر للبالون المانطاد، ولماذا فشلت التجربة الأولى؟
• Challenger plans to use hydrogen gas from a mud pool to fill a huge prehistoric animal membrane and create a balloon. The first test becomes dangerous because the balloon rises too quickly and almost carries Challenger and the others into the air. The rope eventually snaps, and the balloon disappears.
2. How does Maretas help the explorers discover a hidden way out of the plateau? [SB]
- كيف يساعد ماريتاس المستكشفين في اكتشاف طريق سري للخروج من الهضبة؟
• Maretas secretly takes Malone to a hidden part of the Accala caves and moves some stones to reveal a small opening. He shows Malone that the passage leads downward, allowing the explorers to discover a second exit from the plateau.
3. Analyze what the hidden passage suggests about the limits of Challenger's scientific plans and the value of local knowledge. [SB]
- حلّل ما الذي يشير إليه الممر السري بشأن حدود خطط تشالنجر العلمية وقيمة خبرة أهل المنطقة.
• The hidden passage shows that even brilliant scientific plans may not always be the best solution. Challenger's balloon is dangerous and uncertain, while Maretas's knowledge of the caves provides a safe and practical way out. This proves that local knowledge can be extremely valuable.
4. To what extent does the chapter show that the explorers have learned to depend on one another? [SB]
- إلى أي مدى يوضح الفصل أن المستكشفين تعلموا الاعتماد على بعضهم البعض؟
• The chapter shows that they have become strongly dependent on one another. During their escape, they work together, use the rope carefully, help each other through the passage, and share responsibilities. Their difficult experiences have taught them that cooperation is essential for survival.
5. Analyze the significance of Malone looking back at the plateau before entering the hidden passage. [SB]
- حلّل أهمية نظر مالون إلى الهضبة قبل دخوله الممر السري.
• Malone's final look at the plateau shows that the experience has deeply affected him. He remembers the fear, injuries, arguments, and adventures they experienced there. It also shows that although the plateau was dangerous, it changed the explorers and taught them important lessons about courage, friendship, and cooperation.

6. Evaluate the explorers' decision to leave the Accala secretly. Was secrecy necessary, even after the Accala had helped them? [SB]
- قيّم قرار المستكشفين بمغادرة الأكالا سراً. هل كانت السرية ضرورية رغم أن الأكالا ساعدهم؟
• Yes, secrecy was necessary because the explorers were no longer certain that the Accala would allow them to leave. The Accala had helped them and treated them generously, but they had previously refused to provide materials for their escape. Therefore, leaving secretly was a precaution to ensure their freedom and safety.
7. Evaluate Challenger's insistence on carrying the heavy wooden case during the escape. Was this scientifically responsible or unnecessarily risky? [SB]
- قيّم إصرار تشالنجر على حمل الصندوق الخشبي الثقيل أثناء الهروب. هل كان ذلك تصرفاً علمياً مسؤولًا أم مخاطرة غير ضرورية؟
• It was scientifically responsible because the case contained important scientific material that Challenger believed was valuable to the future of science. However, carrying such a heavy case during a dangerous escape was also risky. His decision shows both his strong dedication to science and his tendency to take unnecessary risks.
8. Why does Challenger consider the balloon experiment successful despite its dangerous result? [SB]
- لماذا يعتبر تشالنجر تجربة المنطاد ناجحة رغم نتيجتها الخطيرة؟
• Challenger considers the experiment successful because it proves that the hydrogen-filled membrane has enough lifting power to rise into the air. Although the test almost causes an accident, it confirms that his basic scientific principle works.
9. What does Zambo's continued support reveal about his character? [SB]
- ماذا يكشف استمرار زامبو في دعم المستكشفين عن شخصيته؟
• Zambo's actions reveal that he is loyal, patient, and dependable. He remains near the original cliff, arranges for help, and continues waiting for the explorers even when their situation is uncertain.
10. Why is the discovery of the second exit more practical than Challenger's balloon plan? [SB]
- لماذا يعد اكتشاف المخرج الثاني أكثر عملية من خطة تشالنجر الخاصة بالمنطاد؟
• The second exit provides a direct route down the cliff that can be used with ropes. Unlike the balloon, it does not depend on an uncertain experiment or create the same risk of being carried into the air.
11. How does the hidden passage change the explorers' chances of returning home? [SB]
- كيف يغير الممر السري فرص المستكشفين في العودة إلى وطنهم؟
• The hidden passage gives them a realistic and safe way to leave the plateau. Before discovering it, their escape depended on difficult plans and outside rescue, but now they can descend the cliff themselves.
12. Why does Maretas help the explorers even though his people want them to stay? [SB]
- لماذا يساعد ماريتاس المستكشفين رغم أن شعبه يريد منهم البقاء؟
• Maretas seems to understand that the explorers have their own lives and need to return home. His sympathy leads him to secretly show Malone the passage, even though helping them may go against the wishes of the Accala leaders.

13. How does the final journey from the plateau show how much the explorers have changed? [SB]
- كيف توضح الرحلة الأخيرة من الهضبة مدى التغيير الذي حدث للمستكشفين؟
• The explorers leave the plateau with a stronger sense of teamwork and dependence on one another. Their experiences with danger, captivity, conflict, and survival have made them more united and mature.
14. Why is Malone's memory of Gladys important at the end of the chapter? [SB]
- لماذا تعد ذكرى مالون لجلاديس مهمة في نهاية الفصل؟
• His thoughts about Gladys connect his original reason for seeking adventure with the person he has become. He first wanted to prove himself to her, but after the expedition he has gained real courage and experience that have changed his understanding of himself.
15. What does the rescue party's arrival reveal about the importance of Zambo's loyalty? [SB]
- ماذا يكشف وصول فريق الإنقاذ عن أهمية ولاء زامبو؟
• The arrival of the rescue party proves that Zambo kept his promise to help the explorers. His loyalty gives them hope and ultimately contributes to their safe return to the outside world.

◆ JRE — نماذج إجابة | JRE Model Answers (Justify – Reason – Evidence)
Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.
1. Is Maretas the key person in the explorers' escape? [SB]
هل يعد ماريتاس الشخص الأساسي في هروب المستكشفين؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, Maretas is one of the key people in the explorers' escape.
Reasoning : Compare his contribution with the help given by Challenger, Zambo, or the rest of the team.
Answer : Maretas provides the explorers with the most important discovery: a hidden passage leading to a second exit from the plateau. Challenger's balloon plan is risky, while Zambo provides support and a rescue party. However, Maretas gives the explorers a direct way out from inside the plateau.
Evidence : Use specific events from the chapter.
Answer : Maretas secretly takes Malone to the Accala caves, moves some stones, and reveals the hidden opening. He then shows Malone that the passage leads downward. The explorers later use this passage and a rope to descend safely from the plateau.

2. Is Zambo's loyalty as important to the success of the expedition as the scientists' knowledge?
هل يعد ولاء زامبو مهما لنجاح الرحلة الاستكشافية بقدر أهمية معرفة العلماء؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes. Zambo's loyalty is as important as the scientists' knowledge because both contribute in different ways to the explorers' survival and return.
Reasoning : Explain how practical loyalty and scientific intelligence contribute in different ways.
Answer : The scientists' knowledge helps them understand the strange creatures and develop possible ways to escape, while Zambo's loyalty provides practical support from outside the plateau. His continued waiting and search for help ensure that the explorers have assistance when they finally escape.
Evidence : Use evidence from Zambo's actions and the group's escape.
Answer : Zambo stays near the original cliff and promises that help will come. He arranges for a messenger to find stronger ropes and more men. When the explorers finally reach his camp, a rescue party of twenty men has arrived with ropes and equipment, proving the importance of his loyalty.
3. Was Challenger right to continue developing the balloon plan despite the danger of the first test?
هل كان تشالنجر محقا في الاستمرار في تطوير خطة المنطاد رغم خطورة التجربة الأولى؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Partly. Challenger was right to recognize the scientific possibility, but he should have been more cautious about the danger.
Reasoning : Explain why the idea was useful but also risky.
Answer : The experiment proved that the hydrogen-filled membrane could lift a heavy weight, so the basic idea had scientific value. However, the balloon rose unexpectedly and almost carried the explorers into the air.
Evidence : Use details from the experiment.
Answer : The balloon suddenly shot upward and pulled Challenger off his feet. Malone and Lord John had to hold on to one another before the rope finally snapped. This shows that the plan was promising but dangerous.

4. Did the explorers make the right decision by leaving the Accala secretly?
هل اتخذ المستكشفون القرار الصحيح عندما غادروا الأكالا سرا؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes. Leaving secretly was a reasonable decision because their freedom was uncertain.
Reasoning : Explain why they could not simply ask the Accala for permission.
Answer : The Accala had treated them generously, but they had previously refused to help them escape because they wanted them to stay as protectors. Therefore, asking openly might have prevented the explorers from leaving.
Evidence : Use details about the Accala's behavior and the escape.
Answer : The Accala refused to provide ropes, leather, and timber for an escape bridge. For this reason, the explorers waited until dark and entered the hidden passage without informing the Accala leaders.
5. Was Maretas's decision to help Malone an act of courage?
هل كان قرار ماريتاس بمساعدة مالون عملا من أفعال الشجاعة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes. Maretas shows courage by helping the explorers secretly.
Reasoning : Explain the risks Maretas takes by helping them.
Answer : He risks being discovered by his own people, who want the explorers to remain on the plateau. Despite this, he chooses to help them because he understands their desire to return home.
Evidence : Use details about how Maretas helps them.
Answer : Maretas waits until no one is watching, secretly takes Malone to the cave, and reveals the hidden opening. He then allows the explorers to use the passage to escape.
6. Did the explorers become stronger as a team during their time on the plateau?
هل أصبح المستكشفون فريقا أقوى خلال الوقت الذي قضوه على الهضبة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes. Their experiences made them more cooperative and dependent on one another.
Reasoning : Explain how their difficult experiences changed their relationships.
Answer : They faced dangerous animals, captivity, battles, injuries, and disagreements together. These experiences taught them that cooperation was necessary for survival.
Evidence : Use details from their escape.
Answer : During the final escape, they travel through the hidden passage together and use the rope to descend the cliff. Each member follows the others and helps the group complete the journey safely.

◆ الفصل في نقاط | Chapter in Points
1. London welcomes the explorers as heroes, but many scientists still doubt their story because the photographs, drawings, insects, and bones could be questioned.
- تستقبل لندن المستكشفين كأبطال، لكن كثيرًا من العلماء ما زالوا يشككون في قصتهم لأن الصور والرسومات والحشرات والعظام يمكن التشكيك فيها.
2. A special meeting is held at the Zoological Institute, where Professor Summerlee gives a detailed report about the expedition without revealing the exact location of the plateau.
- يُعقد اجتماع خاص في معهد علم الحيوان، حيث يقدم البروفيسور سمرلن تقريرًا مفصلاً عن الرحلة الاستكشافية دون الكشف عن الموقع الدقيق للهضبة.
3. Summerlee describes the many creatures and plants they discovered, including iguanodons, stegosaurs, pterodactyls, giant predators, strange birds, and enormous reptiles.
- يصف سمرلن العديد من الكائنات والنباتات التي اكتشفوها، بما في ذلك الإيجوانودون والستيجوصور التيروداكتيل والحوانات المفترسة العملاقة والطيور الغريبة والزواحف الضخمة.
4. Some scientists remain sceptical and argue that the photographs could have been changed, the specimens could have come from other places, and the explorers could have made mistakes.
- يظل بعض العلماء متشككين ويجادلون بأن الصور ربما تم تعديلها، وأن العينات ربما جاءت من أماكن أخرى، وأن المستكشفين ربما ارتكبوا أخطاء.
5. Challenger asks what evidence would satisfy the sceptics. When one scientist says that he needs to see the actual animal, Challenger prepares to provide dramatic proof.
- يسأل نشال للتجرة عن الدليل الذي يمكن أن يفتع المشككين. وعندما يقول أحد العلماء إنه يحتاج إلى رؤية الحيوان نفسه، يستعد نشال لتقديم دليل مذهل.
6. Malone and Zambo bring Challenger's heavy wooden case into the meeting hall. Challenger opens it and reveals a living pterodactyl.
- يحضر مالون وزامبو صندوق نشال الخشبي الثقيل إلى قاعة الاجتماع. ويفتح نشال الصندوق ويكشف عن تيروداكتيل حي.
7. The pterodactyl flies around the hall and escapes through an open window. Its appearance proves that the explorers' story is true.
- يطير التيروداكتيل حول القاعة ويهرب من نافذة مفتوحة. ويثبت ظهوره أن قصة المستكشفين حقيقية.
8. Malone visits Gladys expecting her to admire him, but he discovers that she has married a man named William Potts.
- يزور مالون جلاديس متوقعًا أن تُعجب به، لكنه يكتشف أنها تزوجت رجلاً يُدعى ويليام بوتس.
9. Gladys explains that she has learned that she wants a quieter life rather than the adventurous life Malone had imagined she wanted.
- توضح جلاديس أنها أدركت أنها تريد حياة أكثر هدوءًا بدلاً من حياة المغامرات التي ظن مالون أنها تريدها.

10. Malone initially feels foolish and hurt, but his disappointment gradually becomes less painful as he realizes that his life does not need Gladys's approval to have value.
- يشعر مالون في البداية بالسخافة والألم، لكن خيبة أمله تصبح أقل إيلامًا تدريجيًا عندما يدرك أن حياته لا تحتاج إلى موافقة جلاديس لتكون لها قيمة.
11. Malone understands that the expedition changed him. He faced danger, helped his friends, worked as part of a team, and experienced things that most people could never imagine.
- يدرك مالون أن الرحلة غيّرته. فقد واجه الخطر، وساعد أصدقاءه، وعمل كجزء من فريق، وعاش أشياء لا يستطيع معظم الناس تخيلها.
12. Lord John reveals another secret: he had discovered valuable blue volcanic clay in the pterodactyl swamp and suspected that it contained diamonds.
- يكشف لورد جون سرًا آخر؛ فقد اكتشف طينا بركانيًا أزرق الثمن في مستنقع التيروداكتيل، وكان يعقد أنه يحتوي على ماس.
13. The stones are tested after their return to England, and Lord John reveals that they are valuable diamonds worth at least two hundred thousand pounds.
- يتم فحص الأحجار بعد عودتهم إلى إنجلترا، ويكشف لورد جون أنها ماسات ثمينة تبلغ قيمتها ما لا يقل عن مائتي ألف جنيه إسترليني.
14. The four explorers agree to divide the money into four equal shares.
- يتفق المستكشفون الأربعة على تقسيم المال إلى أربعة أجزاء متساوية.
15. Challenger decides to use his share to build a private museum for scientific research.
- يقرر نشال لجور استخدام نصيبه في بناء متحف خاص للأبحاث العلمية.
16. Summerlee plans to retire from teaching and complete the work he has postponed for years.
- يخطط سمرلن للتقاعد من التدريس وإنهاء الأعمال التي أجّلها لسنوات.
17. Lord John plans to organize another expedition to the plateau, this time with better preparation.
- يخطط لورد جون لتنظيم رحلة استكشافية أخرى إلى الهضبة، ولكن هذه المرة باستعداد أفضل.
18. When Lord John asks Malone about his future, Malone says that he would rather join another expedition than settle down and marry.
- عندما يسأل لورد جون مالون عن مستقبله، يقول مالون إنه يفضل الانضمام إلى رحلة استكشافية أخرى بدلاً من الاستقرار والزواج.
19. Lord John accepts Malone's decision and shakes his hand, showing that their friendship and partnership will continue.
- يقبل لورد جون قرار مالون ويصافحه، مما يدل على أن صداقتهما وشراكتهما ستستمران.
20. Malone reflects on what the lost world has taught him about courage. He realizes that courage is not about appearing heroic or winning admiration.
- يتأمل مالون ما علّمه العالم المفقود عن الشجاعة. ويدرك أن الشجاعة لا تتعلق بالظهور كبطلاً أو الحصول على إعجاب الآخرين.
21. Malone finally understands that true courage means making brave choices when the future is uncertain, fear is real, and other people depend on you.
- يفهم مالون أخيرًا أن الشجاعة الحقيقية تعني اتخاذ قرارات شجاعة عندما يكون المستقبل غير مؤكد، والخوف حقيقي، ويعتمد عليك الآخرون.
22. The expedition ends with Malone returning home as a changed person who understands himself better and is ready for another adventure.
- تنتهي الرحلة بعودة مالون إلى وطنه كشخص مختلف يفهم نفسه بشكل أفضل ومستعد لمغامرة أخرى.

◆ نص الفصل | Chapter Text
London welcomed us as if we had returned from another planet. Newspapers printed rumors about the expedition, but many scientists still doubted our story. Damaged photographs, drawings, unusual insects, and bones could all be questioned.
A special meeting was arranged at the Zoological Institute.
The hall was even more crowded than the one where our journey had begun. Professor Summerlee gave the main report. He described our route without revealing the exact location of the plateau. Then he spoke about the animals we had seen: iguanodons, stegosaurs, pterodactyls, giant predators, strange birds, enormous reptiles in the lake, and many new species of insects and plants.
This time Summerlee was not the sceptic. He was a witness. Yet some members of the audience remained unconvinced.
One scientist stood and argued that photographs could be altered, specimens could come from other places, and even honest travelers could make mistakes. The hall filled with angry voices. Challenger rose slowly.
"What evidence would satisfy you?" he asked. "The animal itself," the sceptic replied.
Challenger smiled. I knew that smile.
He signaled to me. With Zambo's help, I brought the heavy wooden case from behind the platform. The same case Challenger had protected during our escape now stood in front of hundreds of people.
The room became silent. Challenger opened the lid.
For a moment nothing happened. Then a leathery head appeared.
A living pterodactyl climbed onto the edge of the box.

The audience froze. Some people screamed. Others stood on chairs to see. The animal spread its wings, beat the air, and rose above the platform. It circled the hall once.
Then it flew towards an open window and disappeared into the London night. No one laughed at Professor Challenger after that.
Our story was proved in the most dramatic way possible.
There was one private matter I had to face after the meeting. I went to Gladys's house expecting - I am embarrassed to admit - a scene of admiration and happiness. She was surprised to see me. "Edward! You are back.""Yes."
Something in the room felt different. Then a small man stood from an armchair. Gladys looked uncomfortable. "Edward, this is my husband, William Potts." For several seconds I could say nothing.
She had written to me while I was away, but the letter had never reached me. "I thought you wanted a man of adventure," I said at last.
Gladys gave a small, uncertain smile. "Perhaps I learned that I wanted a quieter life."
William Potts was not an explorer. He was not famous. He had never crossed the Amazon or faced a dinosaur. He was simply the man she had chosen.
I left feeling foolish and hurt.
During the following week, however, my disappointment became less painful. I began to understand something important. I had started the expedition because I wanted to prove myself to Gladys. But the journey had become much larger than that. I had faced danger for my friends, worked as part of a team, and seen things no newspaper reporter had ever expected to see.
My life no longer needed Gladys's approval to have value.

A few evenings later, the four explorers met again in Lord John's rooms. After dinner he placed an old box on the table.
"I have one final surprise," he said.
He poured out a group of rough stones. Challenger and Summerlee looked puzzled. "Remember the blue clay in the pterodactyl swamp?" Lord John asked. "I have seen blue volcanic clay like that before - in a diamond mine. That is why I kept going back."
He had collected the stones secretly and had one tested after our return. It was a valuable diamond.
"The whole group is worth at least two hundred thousand pounds," he said. "Four equal shares."
For once, even Challenger had no immediate reply.
Lord John turned to him. "What will you do with your share?"
"Build a private museum," Challenger said. "A proper home for scientific research." Summerlee smiled. "I will retire from teaching and finish work I have postponed for years."
Lord John nodded. "I intend to organize another expedition to the plateau. Better prepared this time."
Then he looked at me. "And you, Malone? I suppose you will marry and settle down."
I thought of Gladys, then of the red cliffs, the moon over the lake, the dark forest, and the thrill of stepping into the unknown.
"Not yet," I said. "If you will take me, I would rather go with you." Lord John reached across the table and shook my hand.
So, my first great adventure ended with the promise of another.
When I had asked Gladys what kind of man she could admire, I believed courage was something a person could win like a prize. The lost world taught me otherwise. Courage is a choice made again and again - when the road is uncertain, when fear is real, and when someone else is depending on you.
I had gone to South America hoping to become a hero.
I came home understanding that adventure was not about looking heroic.
It was about discovering what you were capable of when the world became larger than your plans.
The End

◆ الأسئلة والأجوبة | Questions and Answers
1. Why does the living pterodactyl convince the audience when the other evidence does not? SB
- لماذا يُقنع التيريوداكتيل الحي الحضور بينما لا تفعلهم الأدلة الأخرى؟
• The living pterodactyl provides direct and undeniable proof that the explorers' story is true. Unlike photographs, drawings, or bones, which could be changed, mistaken, or taken from another place, the audience can see the real animal flying inside the hall. Therefore, they can no longer deny the explorers' claims.
2. Explain how Gladys's marriage changes Malone's understanding of the purpose of his adventure. SB
- اشرح كيف يُغيّر زواج جلاديس فهم مالون لهدف مغامرته.
• Malone originally began the expedition because he wanted to prove himself to Gladys and become the kind of man she would admire. After discovering that she had married William Potts, he realizes that his adventure has become more important than winning her approval. He understands that his courage, experiences, and personal growth have value on their own.
3. How did Lord John discover the diamonds and what the four explorers plan to do with their shares? SB
- كيف اكتشف لورد جون الماس، وماذا يخطط المستكشفون الأربعة أن يفعلوا بأنصيبتهم؟
• Lord John remembered seeing blue volcanic clay in the pterodactyl swamp and recognized it as similar to clay he had seen in a diamond mine. He secretly collected some stones and had them tested after returning to England. The diamonds were worth at least two hundred thousand pounds, so the four explorers decided to divide the money into four equal shares. Challenger planned to build a private museum, Summerlee planned to retire and finish his postponed work, and Lord John planned another expedition.
4. Analyze the significance of Lord John's diamonds and Malone's decision to join another expedition. SB
- حلّل أهمية ماسات لورد جون وقرار مالون بالانضمام إلى رحلة استكشافية أخرى.
• The diamonds show that the expedition brought not only scientific discoveries but also a great financial reward. However, Malone's decision to join another expedition shows that money is not his main motivation. He has developed a love for adventure and exploration and wants to continue discovering the unknown.
5. Analyze the importance of Summerlee's changing from Challenger's strongest sceptic to a public witness supporting the discovery. SB
- حلّل أهمية تحول سامرلي من أقوى المشككين في اكتشافات شالمر إلى شاهد علني يؤيد الاكتشافات.
• Summerlee's change is important because he was originally one of Challenger's strongest critics and doubted his claims. After personally witnessing the dinosaurs and other discoveries, he becomes a reliable witness who can support Challenger's story. His change gives the expedition greater credibility because a former sceptic now confirms that the discoveries are real.

6. To what extent does Malone achieve his original goal, even though he does not marry Gladys? SB
- إلى أي مدى يحقق مالون هدفه الأصلي رغم أنه لا يتزوج جلاديس؟
• Malone achieves his deeper goal even though he does not marry Gladys. He originally wanted to become a courageous man whom Gladys could admire. During the expedition, he faces danger, helps his friends, works as part of a team, and proves his courage. In the end, he realizes that he does not need Gladys's approval to feel valuable.
7. Evaluate Challenger's decision to use a living pterodactyl as proof at the public meeting. Was it effective and responsible? Explain. SB
- قيّم قرار تشالنجر باستخدام تيريوداكتيل حي كدليل في الاجتماع العام. هل كان القرار فعالاً ومسؤولاً؟ اشرح.
• The decision was extremely effective because the living pterodactyl gave the audience direct proof that could not easily be questioned. It immediately silenced the sceptics and proved the explorers' story. However, it was somewhat risky because bringing a wild prehistoric animal into a crowded hall could have endangered the people there. Overall, it was effective, but it involved considerable risk.
8. Why does Professor Summerlee become an important witness at the Zoological Institute meeting?
- لماذا يصبح البروفيسور سامرلي شاهداً مهماً في اجتماع معهد علم الحيوان؟
• Summerlee is important because he personally experienced the expedition and witnessed the strange animals and plants on the plateau. Since he had originally doubted Challenger, his decision to support the discoveries makes his testimony especially convincing.
9. What does the scientists' scepticism at the meeting reveal about the importance of evidence?
- ماذا يكشف تشكك العلماء في الاجتماع عن أهمية الدليل؟
• Their scepticism shows that extraordinary claims require strong and reliable evidence. They do not accept photographs, drawings, or specimens easily because these could be questioned. The living pterodactyl finally provides direct evidence that is difficult to deny.
10. Why is Challenger's smile significant when the sceptic asks to see the actual animal?
- لماذا تُعد ابتسامة تشالنجر مهمة عندما يطلب المشكك رؤية الحيوان نفسه؟
• The smile shows that Challenger already has a surprising way to prove the expedition's story. He knows that the living pterodactyl in the wooden case will completely change the audience's attitude. His confidence also reflects his strong belief in his discoveries.
11. What does William Potts's character reveal about Gladys's new priorities?
- ماذا تكشف شخصية ويليام بوتس عن أولويات جلاديس الجديدة؟
• William Potts is an ordinary man who is not an explorer or a famous adventurer. His character shows that Gladys has changed her priorities and now prefers stability and a quieter life rather than excitement and adventure.

12. Why does Malone's disappointment about Gladys eventually become less painful?
- لماذا تصبح خيبة أمل مالون بشأن جلاديس أقل إيلاما في النهاية؟
• Malone gradually realizes that his life has gained value through his own experiences and achievements. He no longer depends on Gladys's admiration to feel important. The expedition has helped him discover his courage, abilities, and independence.
13. What does Lord John's decision to organize another expedition reveal about his character?
- ماذا يكشف قرار لورد جون بتنظيم رحلة استكشافية أخرى عن شخصيته؟
• His decision shows that Lord John is adventurous, courageous, and unwilling to stop exploring the unknown. Even after facing great dangers, he remains excited about discovering more of the plateau and wants to return better prepared.
14. How do the different plans for the diamond money reflect the explorers' personalities?
- كيف تعكس خطط المستكشفين المختلفة لاستخدام أموال الماس شخصياتهم؟
• Each explorer chooses something that reflects his interests. Challenger wants to build a museum for scientific research, Summerlee wants to retire and complete his unfinished work, and Lord John wants to finance another expedition. Their choices show their different priorities and personalities.
15. How has Malone's idea of a hero changed by the end of the novel?
- كيف تغيرت فكرة مالون عن البطل في نهاية الرواية؟
• At first, Malone believes that a hero is someone who looks brave and earns other people's admiration. By the end, he understands that true heroism means making brave choices despite fear and uncertainty, especially when other people depend on you.

◆ JRE — نماذج إجابة | JRE Model Answers (Justify – Reason – Evidence)
Answer the following questions. Justify your answers with clear reasoning and evidence.
1. By the end of the novel, is Malone a hero according to the lesson he has learned about courage?
[SB]
في نهاية الرواية، هل يُعد مالون بطلاً وفقاً للدرس الذي تعلمه عن الشجاعة؟
Justify : State your final judgment.
Answer : Yes, Malone is a true hero by the end of the novel. He proves his courage through his actions and his willingness to face danger and protect others.
Reasoning : Explain what kind of courage the story values.
Answer : The story values real courage, which means making brave choices even when fear is real and the situation is uncertain. True courage is not about looking heroic or gaining fame; it is about helping others and taking responsibility.
Evidence : Support your answer with events from the final chapter and at least one earlier chapter.

Answer : In the final chapter, Malone realizes that he no longer needs Gladys's approval to feel valuable. He also chooses to join Lord John on another expedition. Earlier, he faced dangerous dinosaurs, helped rescue Summerlee and the Accala prisoners, and worked with the others to escape the plateau. These actions show that he has developed genuine courage.
2. Does the ending suggest that personal growth is more valuable than fame, love, or wealth?
[SB]
هل توحي نهاية الرواية بأن التطور الشخصي أكثر قيمة من الشهرة أو الحب أو الثروة؟
Justify : State your judgment.
Answer : Yes, the ending strongly suggests that personal growth is more valuable than fame, romantic love, or wealth.
Reasoning : Explain what Malone gains from the expedition compared with what he first wanted.
Answer : At the beginning, Malone wants to prove himself to Gladys and become a man she can admire. By the end, he gains something deeper: courage, independence, confidence, friendship, and a better understanding of himself. He realizes that his value does not depend on another person's approval or on material success.
Evidence : Use evidence from Gladys, the public success, the diamonds, and Malone's final decision.
Answer : Gladys has married another man, so Malone does not achieve the romantic goal he originally wanted. Although the expedition becomes famous after the living pterodactyl proves their story, fame does not become his main goal. The explorers also receive a large financial reward from the diamonds, but Malone chooses neither fame nor wealth as his greatest achievement. Instead, he decides to join Lord John on another expedition, showing that he values adventure, discovery, and personal growth.
3. Does Gladys's rejection help Malone become a more independent person?
هل يساعد رفض جلاديس مالون في أن يصبح شخصا أكثر استقلالا؟
Justify : State your final judgment.
Answer : Yes, Gladys's rejection helps Malone become more independent and confident.
Reasoning : Explain how Malone changes after discovering that Gladys is married.
Answer : At first, Malone feels hurt because he expected Gladys to admire him. Later, he realizes that his value does not depend on her opinion and that he can be proud of his own experiences and achievements.
Evidence : Support your answer with details from the final chapter.
Answer : Malone realizes that he faced danger, helped his friends, and became part of a strong team. He understands that his life no longer needs Gladys's approval to have value.

4. Does Lord John's decision to plan another expedition show that the first expedition was a failure?
هل يدل قرار لورد جون بالتخطيط لرحلة استكشافية أخرى على أن الرحلة الأولى كانت فاشلة؟
Justify : State your final judgment.
Answer : No, the first expedition was not a failure. Lord John's desire to return shows that there are still discoveries to be made.
Reasoning : Explain what the explorers achieved during the first expedition.
Answer : They discovered a hidden world, proved the existence of prehistoric animals, collected scientific evidence, survived great dangers, and learned important lessons about courage and teamwork.
Evidence : Support your answer with events from the final chapter.
Answer : The living pterodactyl proves their discoveries to the scientific world. In addition, Lord John finds valuable diamonds and decides to return to the plateau better prepared.
5. Is the diamond discovery less important to Malone than the experiences he gained?
هل يُعد اكتشاف الماس أقل أهمية بالنسبة لمالون من الخبرات التي اكتسبها؟
Justify : State your final judgment.
Answer : Yes, the experiences Malone gained are more important to him than the financial reward.
Reasoning : Explain what Malone learns from the expedition.
Answer : Malone learns courage, responsibility, teamwork, independence, and how to face uncertainty. These lessons change his understanding of himself and of true courage.
Evidence : Use details about the diamonds and Malone's final decision.
Answer : Although the explorers receive a large amount of money from the diamonds, Malone does not focus on the wealth. Instead, he chooses to join Lord John on another expedition, showing that adventure and personal growth matter more to him.
6. Does the ending show that Malone no longer needs to prove himself to others?
هل تُظهر النهاية أن مالون لم يعد بحاجة إلى إثبات نفسه للآخرين؟
Justify : State your final judgment.
Answer : Yes, Malone becomes confident in his own abilities and no longer needs other people's approval.
Reasoning : Explain the difference between Malone at the beginning and at the end of the novel.
Answer : At the beginning, Malone wants to become an adventurous man mainly because he wants Gladys to admire him. At the end, he understands that courage and personal value come from his own choices and actions.
Evidence : Support your answer with events from the final chapter.
Answer : After discovering Gladys's marriage, Malone eventually accepts the situation and realizes that his life has value without her approval. He then confidently chooses another adventure with Lord John.

الجزء 1 | Part 1: Reading Comprehension — قطع الفهم (القراءة والاستيعاب)
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

◆ قطع الفهم | Reading Comprehension — الطريقة العامة وأنماط الأسئلة

أولاً: الطريقة العامة لحل قطعة الفهم

١- اقرأ الأسئلة أولاً بسرعة
اقرأ الأسئلة والاختيارات قبل قراءة القطعة؛ حتى تعرف المعلومات التي تبحث عنها أثناء القراءة.

٢- حدد نوع قطعة الفهم

• Fiction:
• قصة، حكاية، رسالة أو إيميل شخصي، وتركز غالبًا على الشخصيات والأحداث والمشكلة والنهاية والدرس المستفاد.

• Non-fiction:
• قطعة معلوماتية عن موضوع طبي أو علمي أو بيئي أو تعليمي، وتركز على الحقائق والأسباب والنتائج والمقارنات.

٣- اقرأ القطعة قراءة سريعة للفكرة العامة
حاول معرفة:

من يتحدث؟ | Who is talking?
ما موضوع القطعة؟ | What is the subject of the passage?
ما المشكلة أو الفكرة الأساسية؟ | What is the basic problem or idea?
ما النتيجة أو الرسالة التي يريد الكاتب توصيلها؟ | What result or message does the writer want to convey?

٤- ارجع الى القطعة عند الإجابة
لا تعتمد على ذاكرتك فقط. ابحث عن الكلمات المفتاحية في السؤال، ثم ابحث عن مرادفاتها أو معناها داخل القطعة.
مع الوضع في الاعتبار أن الأسئلة من الممكن أن تأتي على ثلاثة أشكال:

اختيار من متعدد | Multiple Choice
مقالي | Open-ended
صح أم خطأ وعلي إجابتك | True or False then Justify your answer

٥- انتبه الى كلمات مهمة في السؤال ... مثل:

وفقًا للنص | according to the text
بشكل أساسي | mainly / mainly about
يشير أو يستنتج | suggests / implies
باستثناء | except
أنسب عنوان | best title
أعطِ مبررات | give reasons
عالل | justify
وضع الدليل | show evidence
عبّر عن رأيك | express your opinion

٦- استبعد الإجابات الخطأ فضلًا لتوجيه اختياراتك:

عكس ما ورد في النص. | غير مذكورة في قطعة الفهم.
صحيحة جزئيًا لكنها لا تجيب عن السؤال بدقة.

ثانيًا: أنماط أسئلة قطعه الفهم

وهي متعددة الأنواع ... مثل:

Literal | Main Idea | Inference | Opinion | Summarizing
حرفي | الفكرة الرئيسية | إستنتاج | رأي | تلخيص

Paraphrasing | Sequencing | Synonym / Antonym | Compare and Contrast | Writer's Purpose / Tone
إعادة الصياغة | تسلسل | مرادف / مضاد | المقارنة والتباين | هدف الكاتب / لهجته

Reference | Moral / Lesson | Cause and Effect | Conclusion
إشارة | العبرة / الدرس المستفاد | السبب والنتيجة | خُلاصة / ختام

1. Literal / Factual Question: سؤال نصي مباشر
• سؤال يحتاج إلى معلومة مذكورة بشكل واضح داخل القطعة ، وللإجابة عنه ابحث عن الكلمات المفتاحية في السؤال أو عن مرادفاتها داخل النص.

2. Main Idea Question: سؤال الفكرة الأساسية
• للإجابة عن هذا السؤال، اقرأ القطعة كاملة ثم حدد الموضوع الرئيسي الذي تتحدث عنه، وليس تفصيلة صغيرة وردت فيها.

3. Best Conclusion Question: سؤال أفضل استنتاج أو خاتمة
• اقرأ الفكرة العامة للقطعة، ثم اختر النتيجة أو الخاتمة المنطقية التي تتوافق مع جميع المعلومات المذكورة.

4. Summarizing Question: سؤال التلخيص
• اختر الجملة التي تشمل أهم الأفكار في القطعة دون الدخول في تفاصيل كثيرة أو معلومات جزئية.

5. Synonym / Antonym Question: سؤال المرادف أو المضاد
• حدد معنى الكلمة من خلال سياقها داخل القطعة ، ثم اختر المرادف أو المضاد المناسب لها.

6. Compare and Contrast Question: سؤال المقارنة والاختلاف
• ابحث عن أوجه التشابه والاختلاف بين شخصين أو مكانين أو فكرتين وردتا في القطعة.

Useful expressions: تعبيرات مفيدة
• Both ... and ... : كل من ... و ...
• Unlike ...: على عكس
• However, ...: ومع ذلك / ولكن
• While ...: بينما

7. Inference Question: سؤال الإستنتاج غير المباشر
• الإجابة لا تكون مكتوبة بشكل صريح في النص، لكن الطالب يصل إليها من خلال الأحداث أو المعلومات المذكورة.

8. Writer's Purpose / Tone Question: سؤال هدف الكاتب أو لهجة النص
• حدد لماذا كتب الكاتب القطعة أو فقرة معينة: هل يريد أن يُشرح، يُضح، يحذر، يُنتقد، أم يُشجع؟
ثم حدد نبرة النص أو الكاتب إذا كانت تدل على أنه:
• Admiring / Appreciative: معجب / مُقدِّر (مثل الكاتب الذي يمدح دور الأطباء أو العلماء)
• Optimistic / Hopeful: متفائل / مفعم بالأمل (عند الجديد عن مستقبل التكنولوجيا أو حلول أزمة المناخ)
• Neutral & Objective Tone: النبرة المحايدة / الموضوعية
• Critical: نقدي (يُظهر عيوب فكرة أو سلوك معين مثل إهمان وسائل التواصل)
• Pessimistic: متشائم (يرى الجانب المظلم من قضية ما)
• Cynical / Sarcastic: ساخر / متكبر (يتنقد بسخرية)
• Anxious / Concerned: قلق / مهتم (يُظهر خوفاً حقيقيًا من عواقب مشكلة كالاحتباس الحراري)
• Informative: معلوماتي / يقدم معلومات
• Encouraging: مشجع
• Humorous: فكاهي / ساخر
• Warning: تحذيري

9. Sequencing Question: سؤال ترتيب الأحداث
• ركز على كلمات مثل:
• First: أولاً
• Then: ثم
• Later: لاحقاً / بعد ذلك وقت
• After that: بعد ذلك
• Finally: أخيراً
• Eventually: في النهاية / بعد مروروقت
• وحدد ترتيب الأحداث كما ورد في القطعة.

10. Suitable Title Question: سؤال العنوان المناسب
• اخترعنواناً قصيراً وواضحاً يعبر عن الفكرة العامة للقطعة كلها، وليس عن حديث أو تفصيلة واحدة فقط.

11. Paraphrase Question: سؤال إعادة الصياغة
• اخترالجملة التي تعطي المعنى نفسه باستخدام كلمات أو تركيب مختلف.

12. Do You Think / Your Point of View Question: سؤال الرأي
• حدد الأراء التي وردت في القطعة ثم قم باختيار الإجابة التي توافق وجهة نظرك التي ذكرت في السؤال.

13. Reference Question: سؤال الإشارة أو الضمير الذي يعود عليه الضمير في القطعة
• وفيه نبحث عن الاسم الذي يعود عليه الضمير... مثل:
- What does the underlined word/pronoun "........" refer to/mean?

14. Cause and Effect Question: سؤال السبب والنتيجة
• وفيه نحدد السبب والنتيجة بوضوح ... مثل:
- What caused the villagers to lose trust in the boy?

15. Lesson / Moral Question: سؤال الدرس المستفاد أو المغزى من القصة
• يُظهر هذا النوع غالباً في قطع الا fiction مثل القصص والحكايات وهو لا يسأل عن حديث محدد، بل عن الرسالة التي تتلمسها من تصرفات الشخصيات ونتائج هذه التصرفات.

تطبيق عملي على كل نقطة مهم
In many crowded cities, schools have started turning unused rooftops into small gardens. Unlike traditional gardens, which are usually planted in wide fields, rooftop gardens use containers and vertical shelves to save space.
Two years ago, a science teacher at Al-Nour Secondary School started a rooftop garden project. Students began growing lettuce, tomatoes, and mint. At first, some of them thought that working in the garden would be tiring and would take time away from their studies. However, they soon discovered that the project made science lessons more enjoyable and practical.
The students measure the amount of water each plant needs, record its growth, and use food leftovers to make compost سماد عضوي. The school also uses drip irrigation الري بالتنقيط, which sends small amounts of water directly to the roots of plants. As a result, the garden uses less water than ordinary gardens.
The vegetables produced in the garden cannot feed every student at the school, but they are used in the cafeteria once a week. Sometimes, students give herbs to families living near the school. More importantly, the project teaches students how to work as a team and how to use natural resources responsibly.
Although the project needs money and regular care, it has become popular among students and teachers. Several nearby schools have visited Al-Nour Secondary School to learn from its experience. The city education department is now considering offering grants to help other schools start similar projects.
